# Decisions

Short records. Each one says what was decided, why, and what was considered instead. Change a decision by editing its record and noting the date.

## D1. Independent rewrite derived from listmonk v7

Decided 2026-10-27.

- **Decision:** port listmonk v7.0.0-beta.1 to TypeScript and own the result. Do not track upstream.
- **Why:** the needed features (sequences, events, transactional unsubscribe) are out of scope upstream and touch the data model, sending and admin UI, so carrying them as patches would cost more than owning the code. The stack also differs: Go and Postgres upstream, Bun and SQLite here.
- **Considered:**
  - using listmonk unchanged and building sequences in each app, which repeats the same work per app;
  - a Go fork of listmonk, which keeps Postgres and a second language;
  - a hosted lifecycle tool, ruled out because the project must be self-hosted at no subscription cost.

## D2. AGPL-3.0

- **Decision:** license iglo.email under AGPL-3.0.
- **Why:** it is a derivative work of listmonk, which is AGPL-3.0, so the license is required, not chosen. Apps that talk to iglo.email over its HTTP API are separate programs and are not affected. A modified iglo.email that serves users over a network must offer its source to them; the public repository does that.

## D3. Bun, TypeScript, one executable

- **Decision:** Bun ≥ 1.4 and TypeScript, shipped as one compiled executable. Until 1.0, the executable is the only release artifact. After 1.0, an official Docker image that wraps the same executable is added (updated 2026-10-27).
- **Why:** the same stack and release model as iglo.monitor. Bun's built-in server, SQLite, password hashing and bundler remove most of the dependencies a Node port would need. The owner runs services in containers, so an image is wanted, but it only wraps the executable and does not hold up the first release.

## D4. SQLite only

- **Decision:** `bun:sqlite`, WAL mode, one database file, one writing process. Read-only helpers of the same executable may read that file ([D62](#d62-read-only-helper-processes-for-slow-reads)); they do not add a service, a writer or a release artifact.
- **Why:** the expected load (thousands to hundreds of thousands of subscribers, tens of messages per second) fits SQLite with batched writes. No database server to run or back up separately.
- **Considered:** keeping Postgres. Rejected, because it adds an external service for no needed capability.

## D5. Durable work is rows plus in-process loops

- **Decision:** every kind of background work (campaigns, outbox, sequences, webhooks) is a table with a status, a due time and a lease, processed by a loop in the same process.
- **Why:**
  - restarts lose nothing;
  - state can be queried and shown in the admin UI;
  - edits to sequences apply immediately;
  - no external services are needed.

  The scheduling logic is small. The value is in conditions and permissions, which no engine provides.

- **Considered:**
  - BullMQ or Redis, which needs an external service;
  - Effect Workflow, unstable APIs and replay-based versioning trouble with long waits;
  - DBOS, Workflow DevKit, Temporal, Restate, Inngest and Hatchet, which need Postgres or a separate server;
  - bunqueue, a young project with a single maintainer that solves only the queue part.

## D6. No Effect in v1

- **Decision:** plain TypeScript with small explicit helpers (retry with backoff, timeouts, resource cleanup).
- **Why:** the port translates Go code, which maps directly to plain async TypeScript. A second paradigm would make the port and its review slower. It can be revisited after 1.0 if the iglo.\* projects adopt it together.

## D7. Keep listmonk's HTTP API for ported endpoints, unless it is badly designed

Updated 2026-10-27.

- **Decision:** same paths, fields, envelope, pagination and auth by default. New features get new endpoints. Where listmonk's behavior follows from a poor design decision, fix it during the port instead of copying it. Every such fix is a deliberate difference listed in [API and admin UI](07-api-and-ui.md#api-compatibility-policy) with its reason and marked in the contract suite. A difference needs a concrete benefit for callers or for correctness, not a matter of taste.
- **Why:** existing SDKs, the n8n listmonk node and scripts keep working, and upstream can serve as the reference in contract tests. But iglo.email is owned, not tracked (D1), so carrying a bad contract forever costs more than one documented break now. Example: listmonk's `/api/tx` rejects any recipient who is not already a subscriber, so a one-off email needs a subscriber created first; iglo.email sends to any address.

## D8. Server-side TSX for the admin UI

- **Decision:** port v7's Go-template views to TSX rendered on the server with an in-house JSX runtime that escapes by default. Reuse v7's Alpine.js, Oat, CodeMirror, Squire and Chart.js assets.
- **Why:**
  - typed view props;
  - no client framework;
  - markup stays close to v7's, so its CSS, JS and Playwright tests carry over.
- **Considered:** a Vue 3 single-page app like iglo.monitor's. Rejected, because it means rewriting every screen instead of porting it.

## D9. Liquid for user-authored templates

- **Decision:** LiquidJS replaces Go `html/template` and Sprig.
- **Why:** its explicit template language and familiar data/filter model fit user-authored email. Application-enforced boundaries and the precise library limit semantics are required by D23; library options alone are not a complete sandbox. Reimplementing Go template semantics is not worth it, and no existing templates need compatibility.

## D10. nodemailer for SMTP

- **Decision:** use nodemailer for connection pooling, MIME building, attachments and DKIM.
- **Why:** a correct SMTP client and MIME encoder is a lot of edge cases to own for no product benefit.

## D11. Sequences are data, conditions are a JSON predicate language

- **Decision:**
  - sequence definitions and enrollments live in tables;
  - conditions are `all`/`any`/`not` over `event`, `attr`, `list` predicates;
  - raw SQL is allowed as a permission-gated escape hatch.
- **Why:** safe to evaluate, easy to show and edit in a form, and compiles to one indexed query.

## D12. Marketing delivery is at-most-once

- **Decision:** a crash may skip a marketing email (send marked `unknown`, campaign batch skipped and logged). It never sends one twice. Transactional sends retry only on errors that prove the message was not accepted.
- **Why:** a duplicate marketing email harms trust and deliverability more than a missed one, and missed ones are visible in the send log.

## D13. Outbound webhooks for subscriber state

- **Decision:** signed, retried webhooks for unsubscribes, blocklisting, deletion, bounces and failed sends.
- **Why:** apps keep consent in their own database and must learn about unsubscribes. Without this, every app polls.

## D14. Bootstrap from flags and environment, everything else in settings

- **Decision:** port, host, data directory, root URL and the first admin user come from flags and environment variables. All other configuration lives in the `settings` table. No `config.toml`.
- **Why:** listmonk already keeps most configuration in the database. One source of truth per setting.

## D15. One instance per product

- **Decision:** each product runs its own iglo.email instance and database.
- **Why:** the subscriber table, `external_id`, lists, unsubscribes and the frequency cap all assume one audience. Sharing an instance across products would mix consent and IDs. An instance is one executable and one file, so running several is cheap.
- **Considered:** multi-tenant workspaces. Rejected for v1 as a large change to every table and query.

## D16. Agents do the work, the owner reviews later

Decided 2026-10-27. Model assignments updated by the owner on 2026-10-27.

- **Decision:** the port is split into tasks ([Work plan](11-work-plan.md)) that agents do one at a time. A scheduled lead (GPT-6.1 Sol at xhigh reasoning; the chief of staff, [runbook](../chief-of-staff.md)) briefs a worker per task. All implementation, fixes and mechanical work use GPT-6.1 Terra at max reasoning. Every PR is reviewed by a two-model council (Claude Opus 5.5 at medium effort and a fresh GPT-6.1 Sol at xhigh reasoning) and merges only when both approve the same commit. Claude Opus 5.5 is used only on the council. Agents decide design questions themselves and record them here as "decided by agents, for owner review". The owner is not asked for approvals.
- **Why:** the owner sets direction and is away for long stretches. Two independent reviewers and upstream as the reference catch mistakes before merge, which matters more than speed: one task at a time, a paced schedule, and a pause on usage limits instead of retries.
- **Considered:** owner approval per PR or per wave, which stalls work while the owner is away; several workers in parallel from the start, which spends the usage limit fast and multiplies conflicts in shared contracts before they are stable.

## D17. Reject positive route ID overflow before storage

Decided by agents on 2026-10-27, for owner review.

- **Decision:** positive numeric route IDs above the signed 64-bit maximum (`9223372036854775807`) return HTTP 400 with `{"message":"invalid ID"}` before storage access. This is a deliberate API difference under D7, listed in [API and admin UI](07-api-and-ui.md#api-compatibility-policy) and marked in the focused reference and HTTP fixture cases. M0.5 carries that marker into the general contract suite.
- **Evidence:** pinned upstream `cmd/handlers.go:hasID` ignores the error from `strconv.Atoi`. The live reference request `GET /api/lists/9223372036854775808` saturates to `9223372036854775807`, reaches storage, and returns a 500 exposing a Postgres integer-range error. The iglo.email ID helper rejects the original value as invalid input.
- **Why:** callers get an accurate client-error status, no database implementation details are exposed, and an invalid ID causes no unnecessary database work.
- **Considered:** reproducing the ignored conversion error and resulting database failure. Rejected because it adds no useful contract for callers and conflicts with the SQLite signed 64-bit ID boundary.

## D18. Use Bun's normalized request path

Decided by agents on 2026-10-27, for owner review.

- **Decision:** route the normalized URL supplied by `Bun.serve`. Literal and percent-encoded dot segments are removed, and literal backslashes become slashes, before application handlers see `Request.url`; the canonical target determines the response. Route middleware, including future authentication and permissions, applies to that selected target. Other escaped parameter spellings keep the default Go/Echo behavior where the runtime exposes them. Malformed percent-escape syntax that remains visible is rejected with the upstream transport's plain-text 400 response.
- **Evidence:** exact HTTP request targets `/api/lists/%2E` and `/api/lists/%2E%2E`, sent without client URL normalization, return upstream 400 `invalid ID`. Bun presents their paths to handlers as `/api/lists/` and `/api/`, which currently return the API 404 fallback. Upstream `/api/lists\1` returns JSON 404 because it retains the literal backslash; Bun rewrites it to `/api/lists/1`. The test-only `/fixture/id\12` therefore reaches ID 12 and `/fixture\items\x` reaches item x. The focused reference and normal HTTP fixture cases mark these as D18 and use an exact-target HTTP client. M0.6 additionally records exact `GET /public/static/%2e%2e/style.css`: upstream retains the static path and returns plain-text 404, while Bun exposes `/public/style.css`, which returns the public HTML 404. Both refuse the resource; the general corpus marks the reference and actual compiled-production results as D18.
- **Why:** the required transport is `Bun.serve`, which does not expose the original pre-normalization spelling to handlers. Using its canonical URL keeps routing and middleware on one interpretation and avoids owning a second HTTP transport/parser solely for noncanonical path spellings. Ordinary URL-based clients already normalize these targets.
- **Considered:** restoring the original request target in the application router. It is unavailable at that boundary. Replacing the HTTP transport or adding a raw-socket front end would violate the chosen stack and add substantial protocol code for no supported identifier use case.

## D19. Durable database primitives and module-owned lease policy

Decided by agents on 2026-10-27, for owner review.

- **Decision:** file-backed bun:sqlite connections use WAL, foreign_keys=ON, synchronous=FULL and busy_timeout=5000 ms. Write helpers use synchronous BEGIN IMMEDIATE callbacks without automatic replay; nested or async use fails clearly. Integer handling must preserve signed 64-bit IDs.
- **Why:** a committed send cursor must survive storage recovery before an external marketing send can be treated as started. [SQLite documents](https://www.sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_synchronous) that WAL with NORMAL can lose a commit on power loss, while FULL adds a commit sync. Prefer durability and measure its cost rather than weaken the at-most-once foundation.
- **Migrations:** validate an explicit embedded numbered catalog and apply each file and its user_version change in one transaction. Before a batch with pending migrations on a database with a prior version or user schema, save one SQLite-consistent snapshot beneath the data directory's backups folder. A snapshot failure prevents migration; a file failure retains the previous successful version. No ordinary copy of a live database file. M0.4's production catalog stays empty; M1.1 supplies the first full schema, not a placeholder migration.
- **Loop boundary:** the helper owns injected-clock waiting, lifecycle and the immediate transaction around an owning store's synchronous claim callback. The owning module owns its SQL, statuses, lease eligibility, recovery and finalization; processing runs outside the transaction. Expiry alone never authorizes a marketing retry, and the helper never automatically requeues or replays work.
- **Considered:** NORMAL for faster commits, implicit nested transactions, generic retry/requeue after expiry, a central jobs table and a dummy v1 migration. Rejected because they weaken durability or blur module ownership and delivery decisions. The bounded M0.4 measurements compare equivalent native SQLite primitives with the new helpers; sustained workload validation remains M0.7.

## D20. Separate production and fixture contract coverage until auth is ported

Decided by agents on 2026-10-27, for owner review.

- **Decision:** M0.5 runs the actually ported core corpus against upstream and the compiled production executable. Its authenticated-read and unported shared-helper cases also run against an explicit test-only normal fixture, labelled separately from production coverage. M1.3 must promote the protected read to actual production auth; later domain tasks promote their helper cases.
- **Evidence:** pinned listmonk cmd/handlers.go registers /api/health in the authenticated group and /health publicly. iglo.email intentionally has no production /api/health or auth store yet. M1.3 depends on the M0.5 harness, so requiring production auth in M0.5 would create an ordering cycle or invite a placeholder endpoint.
- **Why:** build and verify the harness's authenticated transport now without weakening the production boundary or overstating parity. Fixture auth is a tiny synthetic request gate, not the later auth implementation.
- **Coverage rules:** target/profile is explicit; reports separate production execution, fixture evidence and pending production groups. Unsupported requested production cases fail clearly, never silently skip or fall back to fixture behavior. D17/D18 retain precise reference/normal expectations; this staging is not a permanent API difference.
- **Considered:** implementing a temporary public/protected production stand-in, treating an Authorization header on public /health as proof of auth, or moving the entire harness behind M1.3. Rejected because they misstate coverage or create an avoidable dependency cycle.

## D21. Support HEAD for embedded assets

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review.

- **Decision:** embedded `/admin/static/` and `/public/static/` assets support GET and HEAD. HEAD resolves the same manifest entry and returns its status, MIME type, cache policy and content length without the bytes. A missing resource is 404. OPTIONS returns 204, and OPTIONS/405 responses advertise `Allow: OPTIONS, GET, HEAD`.
- **Evidence:** the pinned live reference returns 405 for static HEAD (including missing files), 204 for OPTIONS, and 405 for POST. Those responses advertise `Allow: OPTIONS, GET`; its static file server is registered only with Echo GET. M0.6's initial port additionally omitted OPTIONS and Allow, which is a bug, not a policy difference.
- **Why:** callers can inspect resource existence and metadata without downloading CSS, JavaScript, images or fonts. The method advertisement matches what the server actually supports. Resolving the existing immutable asset manifest requires no additional handler side effects or filesystem discovery.
- **Contract:** HEAD status/type/body and OPTIONS/405 Allow differences are explicitly marked D21 in the shared reference/production/fixture corpus. Reference expectations preserve the real upstream responses. Ordinary non-asset HEAD behavior is unchanged.
- **Considered:** copying the GET-only framework registration and rejecting metadata-only probes. Rejected because it forces unnecessary resource downloads without providing a benefit.

## D22. Retain FULL durability and carry the window-boundary miss into events acceptance

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review.

- **Decision:** retain D19, WAL, FULL synchronous commits and the existing 50 messages/s plus 100 events/s targets. M0.7's report is accepted as bounded synthetic storage evidence. The occasional last event outside its exact 60-second window remains an unresolved deadline/flush-timing acceptance item for M5.1. No production throughput or delivery gate is satisfied by this spike alone.
- **Evidence:** the [committed report](../perf/M0.7-sqlite-write-load.md) records one batched repetition with 5,999/6,000 events in-window and about 4.74 ms final drain, while all campaign progress committed in-window. Both independent council reruns reproduced that finite-window effect; Opus also observed host-sensitive latency and control throughput. The report separates window completion from catch-up and does not hide the miss.
- **Follow-up:** M5.1 measures real API acceptance and commit latency separately, exact offered/completed counts, queue growth and flush deadlines over longer mixed-load runs with sending. Investigate the boundary and batching schedule before claiming strict end-to-end 100/s. M3.2, M5.1 and M6.1 retain their end-to-end checks, and future sending/scheduling/storage changes retain before/after measurement requirements.
- **Why:** the measurements support proceeding with the chosen durable storage path while leaving the observed latency limitation visible and testable at the owning production boundary. Weakening durability, relabeling drain as in-window throughput, or asserting unlimited/host-independent capacity would not be supported by this experiment.
- **Re-decided by [D86](#d86-synchronous-stays-full-everywhere-b25) (B25):** FULL stays everywhere, now with a per-writer safety analysis and a FULL/NORMAL series.

## D23. Liquid template boundaries are enforced by the application

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review.

- **Decision:** retain LiquidJS under D9, but require M1.8 to enforce a capability and resource boundary before production template use. Expose recursively copied plain data, finite scalar strings/numbers and dense arrays, with bounded input size/depth; reject executable values, accessors and class instances. Do not expose inherited array elements. Use ownPropertyOnly as an additional property-lookup restriction. Resolve only allowlisted in-memory templates through a null-prototype mapping, with no host filesystem or URL fallback. Register only reviewed, bounded-cost filters.
- **Output and work:** M1.8 must enforce finite parse/input/filter budgets and a byte-counted output emission path that aborts before accumulating output beyond its cap; a final string-size check alone is insufficient. Also account for intermediate captures, filter allocations and trusted layout composition. Choose and document concrete limits with adversarial public-boundary tests and measurements. Until actual bounds are implemented and verified, do not advertise production hard output or execution guarantees.
- **Library semantics:** parseLimit counts UTF-16 code units per parse; renderLimit is a cooperative deadline checked between rendering steps and cannot preempt synchronous filters; memoryLimit accounts selected operations and is neither a heap nor a complete output cap. All require explicit finite configuration. HTML values remain escaped unless the template explicitly uses raw; a previously rendered HTML body is inserted into the layout with `{{ content | raw }}` to avoid double escaping. URL/script contexts need their own validation.
- **Evidence:** the [M0.8 report](../perf/M0.8-liquidjs.md), independently reproduced by both reviewers, records output beyond memoryLimit, a slow filter exceeding renderLimit before rejection, inherited array iteration and inherited template-name lookup with an ordinary-object mapping. The corrected fixture denies template files/URLs/inherited names but is not a finished production template subsystem.
- **Why:** keep the chosen language while making its actual enforcement requirements testable. Correct the original broad sandbox/hard-limit claims rather than assuming an engine option provides preemption or a byte allocation cap. No new runtime service or dependency is authorized by this decision.

## D24. Disable library requeues and classify SMTP outcomes before retry

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review.

- **Decision:** retain nodemailer under D10, explicitly set pool maxRequeues to 0 for all D12-governed transports, and keep retry policy in the owning application module. No automatic marketing retry is permitted. A transactional retry requires proof that the server did not accept that message; uncertainty is unknown and does not authorize resend.
- **Outcomes:** preserve the Message-ID generated before SMTP and record recipient-level acceptance/rejection and the relevant transport stage. A partial recipient failure must not cause accepted recipients to be resent. Connection loss around DATA/final acknowledgement can be ambiguous; a 4xx/5xx label alone is not a full acceptance classification. SMTP 250 is server acceptance, not final delivery or proof of at-most-once.
- **Acceptance:** M2.1 verifies actual transport configuration, partial rejection, failure before/after DATA acceptance, TLS/auth modes, IDs and bounded shutdown. M2.2 tests proof-based transactional retry and that ambiguous/restarted/marketing work is never automatically resent, with the injected clock and crash scenarios. End-to-end M3.2/M6.1 acceptance remains required.
- **Evidence:** the [M0.8 report](../perf/M0.8-nodemailer.md) and both independent dependency inspections found default pool maxRequeues of five, disabled with zero. Successful sink/MIME/DKIM/ID tests do not exercise ambiguous provider failures. The benchmark correctly uses zero requeues and makes no production delivery claim.
- **Why:** a library default must not bypass the durable application record and at-most-once policy. D12 stays unchanged; this specifies the configuration and failure-boundary evidence required to implement it.

## D25. V1 storage identities and references

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review.

- **Identity decision:** every persistent integer identity uses INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT so automatically allocated committed IDs are not reused after deletion. This preserves the pinned upstream SERIAL/BIGSERIAL allocation invariant for users and references stored in sessions/JSON/external clients, as well as subscriber campaign high-water cursors. Application owners allocate IDs automatically and never insert old/lower identities explicitly; the SQL declaration alone cannot forbid explicit reuse.
- **Evidence:** both M1.1 round-1 reviewers reproduced a retained session identifying a different replacement user when the initial schema reused the highest deleted user ID. The new HTTP-fixture regression failed on that schema and passes after reopen with the correction; representative list/template/campaign replacements also receive higher IDs. Both fresh round-2 reviewers approved the corrected head and all acceptance passed. Production auth is still M1.3, which must test this invariant at its actual boundary.
- **Reference decision:** bounces can link to a send and have a nullable subscriber reference so a transactional recipient outside the subscriber table can still have a recorded bounce. Subscriber-linked bounces retain cascade behavior. Event retention clears nullable enrollment trigger references; subscriber deletion cascades membership/events/enrollments while retaining anonymized send/tracking history. Restricted/soft-deleted steps and other FK actions are documented in the [schema report](../perf/M1.1-schema.md) and data model.
- **Ownership:** these are schema capabilities, not implementations of auth, session revocation, consent, retention, wipes or bounce/scheduler workflows. Domain owners must preserve required data and authorization while performing their workflows; SQL cascades or nullable links alone do not finish those features. No automatic retry/recovery transition is added to a trigger.
- **Why:** stale numeric references must not acquire a new identity, and the designed external transactional sends and retention operations need representable rows. The first full migration stays unseeded; settings, auth/roles and built-ins remain their tasks. Before/after measurements are preserved in the [report and aggregates](../perf/M1.1-schema.md#council-identity-allocation-correction).

## D26. Authentication bootstrap and credential boundaries

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review.

- **Order and ownership:** implement M1.3 after M1.1/M0.5, before M1.2 settings routes, so settings never needs an unprotected or synthetic production gate. M1.3 receives an explicit injected auth policy using the existing seven-day session default; M1.2 later supplies persisted settings through the same owner boundary. Minimal role bootstrap/lookup/permission functions and read-only list-label lookup may precede M1.4/M1.5; their CRUD and full list-role/TOTP work stay with those tasks. Modules still own their SQL.
- **Bootstrap:** only when there are no users, a complete IGLO_EMAIL_ADMIN_USER / IGLO_EMAIL_ADMIN_PASSWORD pair initializes the administrator and primordial super-admin role, using Argon2id and an atomic recheck. The initial email is the reserved placeholder initial-admin@localhost.invalid and can be edited through the profile. No public first-account claim form or legacy env-as-API-token path is installed. Missing both inputs leaves public health available but administration unprovisioned and denies login; a partial pair fails setup clearly. Existing users are never overwritten or reset by bootstrap inputs. No credentials or hashes are logged.
- **Credential selection:** a nonempty Authorization header is authoritative, even when a session cookie is present. Invalid explicit credentials do not fall back to a cookie. Preserve the documented Basic and token API-user formats and credential error envelopes otherwise. The pinned upstream cookie-contains-session shim discards explicit API credentials solely for v3 browser compatibility; removing it avoids stale cookies breaking API callers or silently selecting another principal.
- **Passwords:** preserve exact password bytes through creation, login and changes; usernames retain normal trimming. The pinned create path stores a supplied password unchanged but doLogin trims it, making an otherwise accepted password with surrounding spaces impossible to use. M1.3 fixes this inconsistency and verifies the actual reference/production difference in contracts.
- **Cookie requests:** enforce session-bound CSRF protection on every state-changing cookie-authenticated request and pre-login nonce protection on password login. Use the existing nonce form field and a documented X-CSRF-Token header/readable CSRF-cookie path for API forms. Session cookies are HttpOnly, host-only, Path=/, SameSite=Lax and Secure when the configured root URL is HTTPS. Reject a supplied foreign Origin on unsafe requests; header-authenticated API clients do not need a cookie CSRF token. Rotate session identity on login, reject expired/revoked/disabled/deleted identities and never trust forwarded IP headers as a client address (D60 adds explicitly configured proxies).
- **Login limits:** default to 20 password-login POST attempts per transport peer IP per minute, with bounded instance-owned state (at most 4,096 unexpired peer entries), opportunistic expiry and conservative refusal of new entries when full. The injected clock governs expiry/window tests. Check the limit before expensive password work; valid-shaped unknown/disabled users use comparable password-verification work rather than an account-existence timing shortcut. No process-global state or real-time sleep is introduced.
- **Revocation and logout:** password changes preserve the upstream current-session exception for self-profile changes and revoke the other sessions; admin password changes revoke all target sessions. Deletion, disabling or changing authentication identity also revokes the target's sessions so re-enabling cannot resurrect an old session. A valid header-only API logout safely returns the normal success envelope without claiming the API token was revoked, rather than dereferencing a nonexistent cookie session; unauthenticated logout remains forbidden. Token revocation belongs to user deletion/disable or its future explicit lifecycle.
- **Scope and evidence:** M1.3 ports a functional password-login form with the existing TSX/public assets to exercise the actual session boundary; dashboard, password reset and full admin screens remain M4.1/M4.4, and TOTP is M1.4. TOTP-marked users must fail closed until that flow exists. Contract tests first run against the exact pinned upstream and mark these deliberate differences; additional differences need concrete evidence and lead review. No fixture-only authentication pass substitutes for D20 production promotion.

## D27. Safe user-role validation and credential type changes

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review.

- **Role validation:** creating or updating a user with a nonexistent or wrong-kind user/list role returns HTTP 400 with a safe invalid-role message before writing. No credential digest or SQL diagnostic is returned. The M1.3 reference-first corpus observed a pinned-upstream API-user creation with an invalid role returning HTTP 500 and a database error containing the generated token's SHA-256 digest. Rejecting the invalid input before storage provides a useful client error and keeps credential material out of responses. Duplicate-username handling remains its separate compatible behavior.
- **Credential types:** human password login stores only an Argon2id password hash; API credentials store only a SHA-256 token digest. Converting an API user to a human with password login enabled requires a new valid password and hashes it under the human policy. A conversion without password login clears the credential. Converting a human to an API user clears the human credential and disables password login; it does not silently create a token or add a token-rotation endpoint. Existing API-to-API updates retain their token. The supported API-user creation flow issues a fresh token once.
- **Evidence and reason:** pinned upstream permits API-to-human conversion without a password by treating the existing API digest as a human password hash, and can retain a human hash when converting to API type. The shared HTTP corpus records these source outcomes and the corrected normal outcomes without printing credentials. Requiring a credential of the right type avoids unusable or confused authentication state. D26 revokes sessions on type/credential changes, and D25 identity allocation remains unchanged.
- **Scope:** these are narrow user/auth contract differences for M1.3, not authorization for role CRUD, a new token lifecycle API or migration of real account data. Both modes mark D27 explicitly. Independent council still reviews the implementation and acceptance on the final PR head.

## D28. API profile updates and preserving a human administrator

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review.

- **API profiles:** an authenticated API user may update its own display name through PUT /api/profile without supplying a human email or password. Supplied email/password and account-control fields do not change its stored token, email, type, status, username or role. Profile updates are not a token-rotation or human-password endpoint. Keep the compatible response projection (including upstream's credential-present password_login field), without treating that projection as authority to enable password authentication.
- **Evidence and reason:** both M1.3 round-1 reviewers reproduced upstream 400 Invalid fields: email for an API name-only profile update, versus normal 200. The pinned setupUserFields sets PasswordLogin from the presence of any stored credential, and UpdateUserProfile consequently applies human-password validation and hashing to an API token holder. A display-name update should not require irrelevant human credentials or replace an API digest with a password hash. The common corpus must mark the name-only difference and verify token preservation with valid supplied password/email fields.
- **Last administrator:** preserve at least one enabled human user with the primordial Super Admin role, password login and a password. Reject conversion of that last user to API type with the existing needSuper error, just as disabling, deleting or removing its role is rejected. The pinned update-user SQL checks replacement role/status but omits replacement type, permitting this conversion and leaving no human administrator. M1.3's stronger type-aware guard is retained and explicitly marked against that source outcome. This does not forbid conversion when another enabled human Super Admin remains.
- **Acceptance:** shared reference/compiled-production cases exercise these differences through HTTP with invocation-owned synthetic accounts. A sole-admin probe runs only after the corpus has cleaned its users, verifies the owned bootstrap account is the sole enabled human Super Admin, and restores it in a finally block through the separate API administrator. Successful and rejected profile writes must omit secrets and preserve the original token in production. Existing D25-D27 boundaries remain in force.

## D29. Apply the administrator guard to the resulting account state

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review.

- **Decision:** resolve omitted/zero user_role_id and omitted/empty status to their current stored values inside the write transaction, after any password hashing, before applying the last-enabled-human-Super-Admin guard. A harmless update to that account succeeds without resending unchanged authority fields. Explicit changes that would remove the final enabled human Super Admin remain rejected under D28. Other request validation, credentials and profile restrictions are unchanged.
- **Evidence:** the M1.3 round-2 Sol reviewer reproduced HTTP 400 needSuper upstream and 200 in production for a sole administrator update supplying its existing username/name/email, type user and password_login true but omitting role/status. Pinned queries/users.sql evaluates raw role/status parameters in canEdit even though its SET expressions later preserve omitted values. Explicit zero role and empty status have the same mismatch.
- **Benefit:** callers can update ordinary user fields without redundantly resending authority fields that are not being changed. The guard protects the resulting invariant rather than rejecting a safe update because its payload is partial. This retains the existing production behavior; it does not weaken protection against disable/delete/demotion or conversion to API type.
- **Acceptance:** mark shared reference/compiled cases for omitted role/status, zero/empty defaults and each field defaulted independently, using the invocation-owned sole bootstrap administrator after other users are cleaned. Assert the exact upstream error and normal retained user identity, enabled status and Super Admin role; retain the D28 conversion rejection and final password-login check. No production SQL change is required by this decision.

## D30. Role mutations and the TOTP authentication boundary

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review. M1.4 implements this policy; it is not a claim that the routes already exist.

- **Role safety:** keep the primordial Super Admin role immutable. User-role and list-role update routes must match the stored top-level role type; a wrong-kind or child-role target returns 400 invalid ID without mutation. Role creation/update and replacement of list permissions are one short transaction, so invalid children cannot leave partial authority changes. Reject deletion of any role assigned to a user with 400 and the existing cantDeleteRole message, checking both user_role_id and list_role_id through the users owner under the same write lock. Do not depend on a foreign-key cascade to delete identities.
- **Evidence and benefit:** pinned cmd/roles.go protects only ID 1, queries/roles.sql update-role filters parent_id but not type, and internal/core/roles.go writes the parent and children separately. Both pinned schema.sql and the v1 schema use ON DELETE CASCADE on users.list_role_id, so deleting an assigned list role can remove accounts, including the last human administrator. Type checks, atomic writes and assigned-role rejection avoid unintended account loss and partial or wrong-kind permission changes. Shared reference-first cases must record the actual upstream outcomes and mark these corrections explicitly.
- **TOTP:** extend D26's authentication boundary through the second factor. A verified password for an enabled, password-login human with TOTP issues only a bounded, short-lived challenge, never a full session. Challenges are instance-owned, single use, expire after five minutes under the injected clock, and fail closed after restart. Limit them to 4,096 live entries and five failed code checks per challenge; apply the existing peer-IP attempt limit and Origin/nonce protections to second-factor POSTs. Recheck live account/credential/factor state immediately before session creation so disable, deletion, password/type/factor changes cannot revive a pending login. Enabling/disabling a factor revokes other sessions and invalidates pending challenges; retain the verified current self-service session. Setup/disable remain self-service as upstream; a path ID never grants authority over another user. Preserve D26 exact-password and CSRF behavior and keep secrets out of profile/list/error/log output. The two-factor form is functional in M1.4; M4.1 retains integrated admin screens and polish.
- **QR dependency:** authorize pinned qrcode 1.5.4 (MIT), plus its development type declarations, only for generating the existing base64 PNG provisioning response. Registry metadata was checked at https://registry.npmjs.org/qrcode/1.5.4. Bun/Web Crypto still owns random generation, HMAC and TOTP; no authentication framework or external QR service is added. QR encoding and PNG generation are format algorithms worth reusing rather than inventing. Verify Bun and the compiled executable, keep the lockfile, and preserve compatible third-party notices.
- **Acceptance:** reference-first role and factor workflows, current-role authorization on the next request for both API tokens and sessions, deterministic fake-clock challenge expiry/rate tests, concurrent revocation tests at public HTTP boundaries, actual compiled login/setup verification, browser evidence and bounded before/after storage measurements. Do not infer production coverage from test-only list provisioning. Any additional difference needs lead review and an API-policy row before merge.

## D31. Literal role-name search and safe role diagnostics

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review; M1.4 acceptance remains pending.

- **Role search:** user/list role name search uses a case-insensitive literal substring after the upstream trim/whitespace normalization. It does not promise PostgreSQL dictionary stemming or SQL wildcard expansion. Preserve upstream ordering defaults, accepted sort fields, envelopes and projections; they are not part of this exception. Keep matching deterministic, independent of the host locale. No subscriber-query/FTS or other endpoint behavior changes under this decision.
- **Evidence and benefit:** the M1.4 worker observed pinned upstream matching the role name M14 Ponies for query pony, while the candidate's literal substring search does not. Pinned queries/roles.sql combines database-configured TO_TSVECTOR/PLAINTO_TSQUERY with ILIKE; internal/core/core.go normalizes whitespace first. Role names are identifiers and labels, and literal matching gives callers predictable punctuation and language-independent selection without PostgreSQL text-search configuration or an added search dependency. Shared reference/compiled tests must capture the concrete stemming difference, case/whitespace compatibility and literal percent/underscore behavior. Record the actual reference wildcard results before claiming their correction; this is not blanket authorization for search mismatches.
- **Role diagnostics:** retain the existing error status and localized field/operation context, but omit submitted invalid permission strings and database-engine/constraint internals from role errors. Invalid permission returns the safe Invalid fields: permission (or list permission) form; do not echo the supplied value. Storage errors retain the compatible operation-level failure without SQL diagnostics. The worker observed the pinned invalid-permission response Invalid fields: permission: unknown:permission. A permission-shaped input may contain a pasted credential; echoing it and driver-generated SQL details adds no information needed to identify the bad field. This extends the safe credential-diagnostic principle of D27, without authorizing unrelated error or status changes. Record precise paired expectations for invalid user/list permissions, duplicate role names and invalid children, and ensure validation does not persist rejected data.
- **Scope:** these are narrow M1.4 API-policy exceptions. The fixer is separately authorized to add the QR package's license root to the existing scripts/assets-build.ts collector (plus narrowly required pinned license overrides in assets/licenses and HTTP asset tests), so those notices ship inside the single executable. No new release artifact, distribution path, production test control or service is added. Independent council still checks final-head behavior, evidence and all remaining differences.

## D32. Require explicit user-role permission replacement

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review, after M1.4 council round 1.

- **Decision:** POST/PUT user-role requests require a non-null permissions array. Omitted or null permissions return 400 Invalid fields: permissions before storage and leave the role unchanged. Explicit [] remains valid and explicitly creates/replaces an empty grant set. Other field validation and existing permission names are unchanged.
- **Evidence and benefit:** reviewer A reproduced a name-only PUT and an explicit null permissions PUT clearing an existing users:get grant in normal mode (200), while pinned upstream returns 500 on its non-null storage constraint and keeps the grant. The same missing/null mismatch affects POST. Silently treating an unspecified replacement as a revocation can remove users' access. Rejecting the incomplete client input avoids accidental authority changes and identifies the required field without copying upstream's database-error 500 or leaking its diagnostics. D31 does not otherwise change error statuses; D32 is this precise additional exception.
- **List children:** preserve the separate upstream contract: omitted/null permissions on an individual list entry mean an empty permission array. Replacement skips that entry, clearing its grant. Reviewer B independently reproduced upstream 200 with the prior child grant removed versus candidate 400 retaining access. Correct this compatibility bug; do not apply the top-level user-role required-array rule to list children. Explicit [] is the same clearing control. No additional deliberate difference is introduced for this case.
- **Acceptance:** paired reference/compiled cases cover omitted/null user-role POST and PUT, unchanged names/grants after rejection and explicit [] success. Shared list-child cases cover omitted/null/[] acceptance; a labelled synthetic-list HTTP fixture starts with a real grant and verifies each representation removes both its projection and effective authorization. Add the regression cases and run them against pinned upstream before fixing production. No SQL/schema change is required.

## D33. List-name search, explicit description clearing and owner boundaries

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review, before M1.5. Implementation and paired acceptance are pending.

- **Search:** apply D31's literal, case-insensitive name substring policy to list collection search and query-based deletion, with the upstream trim/whitespace normalization and deterministic casing. Percent/underscore are literal and PostgreSQL dictionary stemming is not promised. Read and delete must share the same selection predicate, including permissions and filters, so a name query cannot preview one selection and delete another. Other default/minimal/filter/pagination/sort behavior stays compatible. Confirm the concrete Ponies/pony and wildcard reference differences before implementation; no blanket permission to change other search endpoints.
- **Descriptions:** PUT /api/lists/:id with an explicit description empty string clears the description. Omitted/null description keeps the existing value as upstream does. The lead read pinned queries/lists.sql update-list: its CASE WHEN $7 != '' preserves the old text for both omission and an explicit empty string. That prevents a caller from removing a description it previously set. Distinguishing a supplied empty string gives callers a real clearing operation without changing omitted-field behavior. Preserve the other field rules, including upstream tag replacement and required names; this does not generalize D32 to list tags.
- **Counts and ownership:** M1.5's list owner owns lists SQL. Minimal subscriber-owned read functions may aggregate subscriber_lists for live counts before M1.6 supplies subscriber CRUD; do not query that table from lists. The pinned mat_list_subscriber_stats aggregates membership status, without filtering global subscriber status, and QueryLists refreshes it when caching is disabled. Use the matching live aggregate through that owner until the later maintenance/cache owner exists, with the same response projection; do not return fake zeros, introduce a maintenance loop or silently claim cached/production-subscriber coverage. Preserve the minimal endpoint's actual projection. Positive membership-count tests before M1.6 are labelled synthetic fixture evidence.
- **Reference lifecycle:** pinned update-list also updates campaign_lists.list_name. A narrow campaign-owned list-reference function may preserve that snapshot when a list is renamed in the same transaction. List deletion retains the v1 SET NULL campaign reference/name and cascades list memberships/role-child grants through the declared foreign keys; exercise those observable effects without porting campaigns or subscribers. Owners expose concrete functions, not cross-table SQL, registries or fake domain APIs.
- **Acceptance:** paired pinned-reference/compiled list contracts first, including exact search/description markers and distinct null/omitted/[] fields; current per-list and blanket authorization for read/write/delete; promotion of the existing staged list helpers to real compiled endpoints; labelled positive count/reference fixtures; restart, bulk-filter safety and sequential before/after storage measurements. No new runtime dependency, release artifact or production test route is authorized.

## D34. Native list identity width and the separate role-input limit

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review, during M1.5.

- **List identity width:** keep SQLite's positive signed 64-bit list identities. Do not cap them at PostgreSQL integer width. For an absent representable ID, GET/PUT retain the ordinary 400 List not found and DELETE retains 200 success, rather than upstream's storage-error 500. Path/query values outside signed 64-bit keep D17/existing query validation; this is not an expanded overflow allowance. Emit list IDs as exact JSON number tokens, including above JavaScript's safe integer range, and preserve their identity in path/query operations.
- **Evidence and benefit:** the worker's reference-first common corpus checks GET/PUT/single DELETE/bulk DELETE with 2147483648. Pinned PostgreSQL returns its integer-out-of-range diagnostic (22003); normal uses the existing not-found/idempotent-delete result. The v1 schema and owner bind IDs as 64-bit integers. Artificially restricting valid SQLite identities would make native storage IDs unusable, and a database error is not a useful result for an otherwise representable absent resource. A labelled fixture advances only the fresh test allocator and then uses automatic production allocation to verify 9007199254740993 is emitted/read without rounding; no production sequence control exists.
- **Separate role boundary:** the pre-existing role-child JSON parser accepts only safe JavaScript numeric integers. It rejects a per-list grant above 9007199254740991 with 400 before mutation; do not round or alias it to a different list. Blanket list scopes continue to authorize full-width list paths. This is a disclosed limitation, not full 64-bit support across every API or a reason to claim individual high-ID grants work. Record an observable rejection/no-write control and keep a follow-up for a small shared identity-input/projection change. That later change must accept lossless input through the existing numeric JSON contract and test role/user projections, rather than silently invent a string-ID API or weaken validation. No such shared change is included in M1.5.
- **Scope/acceptance:** mark the exact storage-width differences as D34 in the shared reference/compiled corpus and API policy, with ordinary int32/not-found/overflow controls retained. Keep D25 non-reusing automatic IDs. Existing before/after storage evidence remains applicable because this disposition changes documentation/coverage, not SQL or runtime behavior. Independent council evaluates both the implementation and the disclosed role boundary.

## D35. List request decoding and tag bounds

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review, after M1.5 council round 1. Implementation and paired acceptance are pending.

- **Destructive selectors:** restore pinned DELETE behavior, without a new exception. Bulk list deletion uses query-string selectors only, preserves all raw repeated tag constraints (including empty/whitespace-padded values), and does not activate query/all/tag from URL-encoded DELETE bodies. Do not reuse collection makeQuery normalization for destructive filters: upstream passes those deletion values differently. Keep first-value/enum behavior for the other selectors and validate the final current permission scope before mutation. Both reviewers reproduced unintended deletion of lists upstream preserves; discarded constraints are a blocker, not a convenience difference.
- **Go binding compatibility:** restore list JSON field aliases/case-insensitive field matching and null string-tag normalization. Use the pinned decoder's behavior for duplicate recognized fields rather than silently selecting another value. XML list create/update remains supported because the pinned Echo binder exposes it and there is no product benefit in removing a valid caller format. Parse the bounded input, map only the known model fields and keep exact string/empty/null/default semantics, rather than coercing numeric text or accepting arbitrary objects as strings.
- **XML dependency/security:** authorize fast-xml-parser exactly 5.11.2 (MIT, registry metadata checked at https://registry.npmjs.org/fast-xml-parser/5.11.2) for the list request boundary only. The worker verifies the exact dependency source/options and locked license closure, Bun/compiled behavior and embedded notices. A maintained format parser is preferable to inventing an XML tokenizer/entity implementation. Keep the existing 64 KiB body bound; reject DTD/custom-entity declarations and external entities with safe 400 before expansion or network/file access, and bound XML nesting to 64 levels. Standard XML predefined/numeric entities and ordinary text/CDATA remain supported. These explicit resource/declaration restrictions are narrow listed differences from framework binding, not authorization to remove XML or relax other input validation. No XML service, auth framework or runtime test route is added.
- **Tag boundary:** normalize tags as pinned upstream does, then reject any normalized tag longer than 100 Unicode code points with safe 400 Invalid fields: tags on both create and update, without writing. Preserve accepted 100-character strings, including multibyte characters, and all other tag replacement/containment behavior. Both reviewers reproduced upstream POST 500/22001 and PUT silent truncation to 100 versus candidate retention of 101. Consistent client validation avoids storage diagnostics and irreversible silent truncation; do not truncate, silently accept wider tags or count UTF-8 bytes as characters. Empty/null tag elements retain compatible normalization, not an error.
- **Acceptance:** common reference-first cases for every reproduced input/retention/filter result, exact D35 markers for tag statuses/storage and XML restrictions, failing-before/passing-after destructive controls, decoded XML/JSON projections through compiled real HTTP, malformed/oversized/deep/entity cases, current grant/CSRF boundaries for both formats and embedded dependency notices. Preserve D33/D34 and the complete prior corpora. Any further deviation requires concrete source/reference evidence and lead disposition.

## D36. List writes accept only writable fields

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review, after M1.5 council round 2.

- **Decision:** list create/update decoding recognizes only writable name, type, optin, status, tags and description. Response-only identifiers, UUIDs, timestamps, counts, subscription metadata and unknown fields are ignored in both JSON and XML, without validating their value shapes. A path ID still selects the update target; ignored body metadata cannot change identity, authority or stored generated fields. Keep compatible writable JSON aliases/duplicate ordering, XML field casing, tag/null handling, D33 description semantics and D35 resource/validation boundaries.
- **Evidence and benefit:** the pinned handler binds the entire read model before writing only a small subset. Reviewers reproduced discarded SubscriberCount/ID overflow, malformed CreatedAt and a nested subscriber_statuses duplicate type error causing upstream 400/no write. The candidate's partial emulation inconsistently accepted some invalid metadata, rejected valid comma-fraction timestamps and normalized invalid calendar dates. Those values are never used by list storage. Validating them serves an incidental shared Go struct rather than the caller: clients updating or cloning a returned list must otherwise strip generated metadata or obey unrelated decoder formats. An explicit writable-field boundary is simpler, makes snapshots usable as input and never turns discarded metadata into authority. Do not build another date/map/int validator for data the endpoint cannot write.
- **Precise compatibility difference:** syntactically valid POST/PUT with invalid-shaped read-only metadata may now succeed and apply its valid writable fields where pinned binding returns 400. Do not claim unchanged row/no-write in that case; verify exactly which writable fields change and that server-owned identity/timestamps/counts/subscription projections are not assigned from the input. Valid metadata accepted upstream remains accepted. Mark paired cases D36, including JSON invalid calendar/type/nested duplicates and XML range/blank/time/map shapes, for create and update.
- **Format integrity remains mandatory:** ignoring metadata never excuses malformed JSON/XML, forbidden literal XML characters, unresolved/invalid entities, DTD or resource-limit failures. Round-2 numeric-reference and literal-NUL findings must be fixed compatibly, including ordinary CDATA/comment and long leading-zero numeric-reference controls. Discarded fields still lie inside a valid bounded document.
- **Whitespace:** no new search exception. D33 requires Go TrimSpace/makeSearchString throughout list collection and deletion filters; reuse the existing exact Go whitespace set (NEL included, BOM excluded) rather than JavaScript trim, and preserve RE2 internal whitespace behavior. The BOM-padded destructive query must retain the list exactly as pinned upstream does. Reference-first retention/selection cases are required.
- **Scope:** the lead makes these small decoder/selection corrections directly, with paired reference-first regressions and full exact-head acceptance before fresh round-3 council. No new runtime dependency, schema/SQL, route, auth bypass or shared identity change is authorized. Preserve D34's disclosed role-input limit and D35 parser/notice protections. Record fresh bounded before/after controls without claiming byte-identical historical binaries when rebuild hashes differ.

## D37. Strict streaming XML list input

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review, under the runbook's three-round resolution rule.

- **Evidence:** both round-3 reviewers reproduced malformed attribute references/comment/text accepted by fast-xml-parser 5.11.2's validator; B also reproduced lossy UTF-8 replacement and parsing an unconsumed malformed suffix. Pinned Go rejects malformed consumed XML without mutation, but accepts a valid first root followed by incomplete unused data. D36 requires document integrity and does not authorize these differences. Accept all three findings and correct them, without a fourth council round or an approval claim.
- **Parser:** replace fast-xml-parser with pinned saxes 6.0.0 (ISC) and its locked xmlchars 2.2.0 (MIT) closure, removing the former dependency/notice override. The strict event parser supplies XML grammar, character/entity handling and streaming state; the application must not invent a second XML grammar with regex repairs. Registry metadata was checked at https://registry.npmjs.org/saxes/6.0.0 (gitHead 211fa0ebec9b628affc09219199639887174bfc3) and https://registry.npmjs.org/xmlchars/2.2.0. The upstream repository https://github.com/lddubeau/saxes is archived; accept this pinned bounded parser only with source inspection and the common compiled/reference regression corpus, not an implied maintenance or exhaustive conformance guarantee. This replaces D35's parser choice while preserving its API/resource policies.
- **Packaging/types:** saxes 6.0.0 omits LICENSE from the published directory. Bundle the complete license fetched from exact registry gitHead 211fa0ebec9b628affc09219199639887174bfc3 as a version-specific override, with xmlchars' published MIT notice; remove the unused old parser override. Commit a Bun patch limited to saxes.d.ts generic constraints and exact optional-property compatibility for TypeScript 6. Keep the runtime JavaScript unchanged and all project strict checks enabled; no declaration shadow or global skipLibCheck.
- **Byte/document boundary:** retain the shared 64 KiB byte-counted body reader and add a lossless byte-returning boundary; existing JSON callers keep compatible replacement decoding. Feed XML with fatal streaming UTF-8 decoding through ASCII > boundaries, stopping immediately when the first root closes, so invalid consumed bytes fail while unused suffix bytes are not decoded or parsed. No network or external entity resolver. Reject DTD before any registration/expansion (the parser does neither), reject nesting over 64, and validate XML 1.0 syntax/characters in ignored attributes/elements as well as writable fields. Reject unsupported XML versions as pinned Go does.
- **Mapping:** keep Go case-sensitive local writable names, repeated Tags, direct text/CDATA and source-order field replacement; namespace bindings do not grant authority. D36 still ignores response-only metadata, but the consumed document must be valid. Preserve ordinary/predefined/numeric/long-zero/one-pass controls and generated metadata protection.
- **Acceptance:** add shared POST/PUT/no-write malformed attribute/comment/text and invalid UTF-8 cases, compatible JSON replacement controls, valid first-root/incomplete or invalid-byte suffix cases, normal escaped/CDATA/BOM controls, exact compiled dependency notices and full local/CI acceptance. Measure the same old/new compiled operations in three serial ext4 FULL/WAL repetitions after acceptance. The lead fixes directly; no extra implementation worker, helper or review is needed. Merge only after fixes and green final-head CI, with a public lead resolution explaining the tie-breaker and all three rounds' CHANGES.

## D38. Subscriber scope, writable input and external identity

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review, before M1.6 implementation.

- **Task boundary:** M1.6 ports numeric-ID CRUD/PATCH, ID-selected delete/blocklist/membership actions and the designed external-ID GET/PUT/DELETE. GET /api/subscribers is pinned QuerySubscribers and belongs to M1.7 with search/FTS/raw-SQL/query-selected bulk/exports. Activity/bounce APIs remain later. This keeps a single worker's task bounded instead of quietly implementing an unsafe partial segment engine.
- **Sending stage:** pinned schema defaults app.send_optin_confirmation to true; InsertSubscriber/UpdateSubscriberWithLists may send after storage. The rewrite lacks SMTP/templates/public opt-in plumbing at M1.6. Until M2.5 wires it, persist real membership/preconfirmation/blocklist state without automatic or explicit opt-in delivery; leave /:id/optin unported and never claim mail was sent or queued. No fake successful sender/outbox or implicit confirmation. M1.2 later supplies domain allow/blocklist policy; M1.6 uses explicit injected empty defaults, not cross-owner settings SQL.
- **Writable boundary:** extend D36's benefit to subscriber DTOs: validate writable email/name/status/attribs/lists/preconfirm_subscriptions/external_id (and route-owned bulk fields), ignore read-only identity/UUID/timestamps and unknown metadata, never assign them. Mark reference binding errors versus accepted discarded metadata precisely. Valid JSON/XML/form writable behavior stays reference-first, including null/omission and duplicate/alias semantics. Preserve D37 strict consumed XML and existing body/resource limits; do not mistake ignoring metadata for accepting malformed documents.
- **External identity:** external_id is nullable, exact and case-sensitive, 1–2000 UTF-8 bytes when set, with no NUL or unpaired surrogate and no trimming/case folding/Unicode normalization. POST omission/null stores null; PUT/PATCH omission preserves, explicit null clears, empty/invalid types return safe 400. Duplicate assignment returns safe 409 External ID already exists, atomically retaining all fields/memberships. Subscriber projections include the field. GET/PUT/DELETE /api/subscribers/external/:external_id reuse numeric-route permissions/projection/envelopes, with a generic safe 400 when absent. No implicit upsert. Decode opaque path values once, including encoded slash, spaces and Unicode. Resolve the external target after body reads under the mutation snapshot; concurrent rekeying cannot redirect a stale request. Deleting a subscriber frees its external string without reusing numeric IDs.
- **Partial/atomic writes:** resolve omitted PATCH fields against current stored state under the write transaction after the asynchronous body read. This prevents prepopulation from overwriting concurrent unrelated changes. Recheck live identity/grants and source memberships; keep target list ownership checks and updates atomic with the subscriber write. No network calls inside transactions; actual bodies/default/null/consent semantics remain pinned except this concurrency correction.
- **Single-ID membership action:** pinned ManageSubscriberLists requires a nonempty body ids array even when the route already supplies :id. Accept that path ID without requiring redundant body IDs; bulk collection actions still require IDs. This removes an internal catch-all-struct requirement that serves no caller need. Record the reference 400/noIDs versus normal success as D38, with permission and invalid-body controls.
- **Acceptance/limits:** reference-first common corpus, normal-only new-feature corpus, real token/session and in-flight authorization tests, no-write/partial-update/uniqueness races, compiled restart and real positive list counts, all prior gates and three serial ext4 FULL/WAL controls. No schema migration/new runtime dependency, SMTP/queue, raw SQL engine, subscriber event upsert, webhook or sequence side effects are authorized. Additional concrete differences require lead disposition, not a blanket extension of D34 or this decision.

## D39. Query selector for every opaque external ID

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review, after the M1.6 reference-first draft.

- **Evidence:** the worker's raw-socket dot-path-observe.ts, independently rerun by the lead, proves Bun changes /api/subscribers/external/%2E to /api/subscribers/external/ and %2E%2E to /api/subscribers/ before routing. D18 deliberately uses that normalized URL; restoring lost raw spellings would require a different transport. D38's promise that the single path route addresses every opaque ID is therefore impossible. The lead also verified eight real loopback query roundtrips: dot, double dot, literal %2E/%2E%2E, percent, slash, backslash and spaced Unicode all survive one URLSearchParams decode unchanged.
- **Decision:** keep D38's valid external IDs, including . and .., and add GET/PUT/DELETE /api/subscribers/external?external_id=<encoded-value>. This query-selector form addresses every valid ID. Keep the existing /api/subscribers/external/:external_id convenience form for IDs whose encoded segment survives Bun normalization; exact dot segments use the query form. Do not reject valid stored dot IDs, invent double-escape conventions, decode recursively, reinterpret literal %2E as dot, or change the router/HTTP transport.
- **Selector boundary:** the query form requires exactly one nonempty external_id parameter, decoded once by URLSearchParams, validated by the same D38 external-ID rules. Missing/empty/duplicate parameters return safe 400 without writes. Body fields never supply a missing selector. The path form derives the encoded suffix from context.url.pathname and decodes it once; do not decode the router's conditionally decoded params again. Its query/body cannot override the path selector. Other endpoint selector rules do not change.
- **Behavior:** both forms share numeric-subscriber capability/list-scope checks, current identity/grants, projection, collision/rollback, omitted-field/PATCH rules and no-upsert behavior. The body external_id is an optional rekey field, distinct from the query selector. Resolve/bind the initial target identity and verify the external mapping after the asynchronous body read under the mutation snapshot; rebinding cannot retarget a pending write. No production alias/test bypass or query engine is introduced. The missing-target answer is no longer a generic 400: an absent external subscriber is 404 ([D101](#d101-external-id-404-and-test-enrollment-from-an-event)).
- **Acceptance:** normal-only GET/PUT/DELETE on both forms, dot/double-dot query lifecycle, simultaneous dot and literal-percent identities, slash/backslash/spaces/Unicode, missing/empty/duplicate selectors and no-write, scoped token/session/CSRF/Origin/live-revocation controls, rebind races, restart and no accidental writes from normalized dot-path requests. Retain the D18 raw transport observation; replace the impossible path-success assertion with canonical query success and normalization/no-write controls. This is an additive normal-only API, not claimed upstream parity.

## D40. Native subscriber and membership selector width

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review, after the M1.6 reference-first draft.

- **Evidence:** the shared reference case binds lists:[9223372036854775807] successfully but pinned queries/subscribers.sql casts list selectors to INT[] and PostgreSQL returns 500/22003. Insert/update first resolve requested lists; nonexistent lists inside range are ignored. Bulk subscription insertion is distinct and may fail a foreign-key constraint. Do not conflate those behaviors.
- **Decision:** extend D34's native storage principle to M1.6 subscriber IDs and membership list selectors. Parse valid signed-64-bit JSON integer tokens/query selectors losslessly, bind bigint to native SQLite owners and serialize identities exactly. Do not cap native identities at PostgreSQL int32 or round through a JavaScript number. Positive route IDs and overflow still follow D17. Go-invalid float/exponent integer spellings and values outside signed64 fail safely before mutation; preserve compatible null/omission/selector behavior otherwise.
- **Precise differences:** a valid native high-ID lookup uses the ordinary native lookup/missing result instead of a PostgreSQL width error. For create/update requested lists, a nonexistent 9223372036854775807 list is ignored as other missing lists are; the valid subscriber write may succeed where reference returns 500/no write. Existing managed-list replacement semantics still apply on update and must be asserted, not described as no-write. The decision does not turn missing bulk targets into successes, bypass permissions, swallow constraints or authorize partial writes.
- **Scope/limits:** subscriber/membership boundaries only; D34's role-child JSON safe-integer limitation remains disclosed and unchanged. No schema/global identity rewrite, explicit production ID seeding, new dependency or blanket response mismatch. Mark precise paired D40 cases for high missing lookups/list selection and exact no-rounding/overflow cases; label any synthetic high-existing-ID fixture truthfully. All ordinary role/auth/list corpora and stored-data invariants remain required.

## D41. Subscriber conflicts and duplicate bulk selectors

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review, after M1.6 council round 1.

- **Duplicate-email updates:** PUT/PATCH, including external PUT, return safe 409 E-mail already exists. on a uniqueness conflict, as POST already does. Keep all subscriber fields/memberships unchanged and preserve current authorization checks. Pinned upstream returns storage-error 500 with pq/constraint/SQLSTATE and submitted-email diagnostics; copying or fabricating that error serves the database implementation rather than the caller. Mark exact paired D41 status/message/no-write cases, and stop asserting invented PostgreSQL internals in normal mode. External-ID collision remains its distinct existing safe 409.
- **Bulk selector sets:** ID-selected subscriber membership actions treat repeated subscriber IDs and repeated target-list IDs as sets, applying one action per unique authorized pair. In particular, an explicit authorized add/status replacement succeeds once where PostgreSQL's duplicate ON CONFLICT tuple limitation returns 500/21000 and rolls back. This makes an action independent of harmless selector repetition. It is not automatic confirmation, re-enabling or resubscription: the explicit requested action/status is applied under the same permission and consent semantics as a nonduplicated request.
- **Safety/precision:** preserve all-or-nothing source/target authorization, exact signed64 IDs, missing-target FK failures and transaction rollback. Test duplicate source, target and both arrays with prior unconfirmed/unsubscribed state; assert the real normal write versus reference no-write and retention of unrelated memberships. Do not turn missing or unauthorized selectors into successes. Other APIs and writable-list replacement rules do not change.
- **Other round-1 findings are defects:** restore pinned email grammar/simple Unicode casing, ordered integer/null slice and attribute-map reset binding, recursive attribute Unicode replacement and finite number rejection, bounded multipart/first-value forms, XML typed scalar defaults/whitespace, Go update-name whitespace, explicit empty PATCH status, raw nested list null-tag projection, route-owned DTO validation and specific safe localized bulk errors. D38–D40 do not authorize those mismatches. No new exception is granted for discarded unrelated fields or malformed documents.
- **Work split/acceptance:** the lead fixes small store/projection/message changes directly, with reference-first paired regressions. One Terra max fixer handles the larger intertwined email/decoder/form/attribute binding work after the lead's commits, then runs full local/compiled/pinned/UI/smoke acceptance and three serial ext4 FULL/WAL before/after measurements. No new runtime dependency, schema/router redesign, helper or duplicate worker. Fresh exact-head two-Sol council follows green CI; no current approval is claimed.

## D42. Linux x86-64 only for MVP

Directed by the owner on 2026-10-28.

- **Scope:** MVP CI, reference acceptance and release artifacts target Linux x86-64 only. Move macOS and Linux arm64 builds and coverage to post-MVP. Existing platform-selection code may remain; it is not an MVP support or verification claim.
- **CI:** retain the application/compiled/UI checks and the full pinned upstream reference job on explicit Ubuntu 24.04 x64 runners. Remove the macOS preparation matrix. Keep both push and pull-request events and all existing Linux acceptance commands; this reduces each run from four jobs to two without dropping behavior checks.
- **Reason:** focus MVP work on the deployment platform and avoid macOS runner capacity and queue delays. The cancelled M1.6 macOS arm64 job never acquired a runner or executed code; its already-requested retry completed successfully before this scope change was applied. No further macOS retries or waits are needed.
- **Follow-up:** post-MVP platform work must add native CI, reference lifecycle verification and working release artifacts before claiming Linux arm64 or macOS support. Historical native preparation results remain historical evidence only. The upstream pin, host limits, independent council and exact-head Linux acceptance gates stay in force.

## D43. Reject NUL in writable subscriber names and attributes

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review, after M1.6 council round 2.

- **Evidence:** pinned PostgreSQL rejects a final writable name containing U+0000 with 500/22021 and a JSON attribute string/key containing U+0000 with 500/22P05. SQLite otherwise accepts these writes. This is separate from discarded DTO metadata and external_id's existing no-NUL rule.
- **Decision/benefit:** reject NUL in the final bound subscriber name and recursively in supplied attribute keys/string values with a safe HTTP 400 before any subscriber or membership write. Use Invalid name or Invalid attributes. as appropriate, without storage/SQL diagnostics. A predictable validation error avoids storing text that downstream native text/display/mail consumers can truncate or reject, and behaves consistently across storage backends. Do not strip NUL or partially apply a request. Other JSON scalar values and Unicode remain supported.
- **Scope:** numeric POST/PUT/PATCH and external PUT share the same validation; lists, consent, external rekeys and other fields remain unchanged on failure. Validate final operation-owned values after binding, so a discarded unknown field or an overwritten NUL value that is absent from the final bound writable data is not newly rejected. Preserve ordered attribute-map/reset semantics. Existing email/external-ID validation remains unchanged.
- **Contracts:** add precise paired cases for upstream 500/no-write versus normal safe 400/no-write, including nested arrays/keys, create retry, numeric updates, external updates and retained memberships/attributes. Do not manufacture PostgreSQL messages. The titlecase and safe bulk-delete context findings are compatibility fixes, not additional policy exceptions. No schema/runtime dependency or new endpoint.

## D44. Settings ownership, safe per-key updates and in-process activation

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review, before M1.2.

- **D14 boundary:** bootstrap host/port/data directory/initial admin/root URL remain flag/environment-owned. Keep app.root_url in the settings response as the effective bootstrap root URL; ignore that read-only field in a full settings snapshot PUT, and reject explicit per-key mutation with safe 400/no write. HTTP configuration must not override the root used by authentication, cookies or public links. No config.toml, bootstrap-secret projection or new bootstrap field.
- **Safe per-key boundary:** PUT /api/settings/:key must accept only recognized writable settings keys and apply the same typed conversion, normalization, secret preservation and invariant validation as a full candidate composed from current unmasked settings. Reject unknown/read-only keys and invalid candidates with safe 400/no write. Pinned per-key SQL otherwise accepts malformed typed values that later break GetSettings or bypass full-PUT invariants; predictable atomic client errors avoid a corrupt configuration. Mark precise paired cases; do not copy SQL diagnostics or blanket-normalize all responses.
- **Activation:** retain normal successful update envelope data:true, but update supported in-process policies without upstream's delayed SIGHUP/restart. Persist an atomic validated settings candidate and publish/read it through concrete settings-owned views, never a process-global registry or another owner's SQL. Existing modules use explicit injected policies. Apply session duration to newly created sessions without resurrecting/revoking retained identities implicitly, and apply subscriber domain allow/block rules through the subscriber owner; preserve D26 CSRF/origin/login limits, D30 TOTP bounds and current-grant checks.
