Later-owner configuration remains stored configuration, not a claim that a sender/loop/UI already exists.
- **Staged SMTP test:** M1.2 authenticates POST /api/settings/smtp/test with settings:manage and cookie mutation guards, then returns explicit HTTP 501 with a safe unavailable-until-M2.1 message. It performs no network call, sends/queues nothing and does not return fake success. M2.1 owns real Mailpit-tested behavior. Keep pinned-side tests scoped to valid local harness settings; label normal staging precisely.
- **Scope:** port settings defaults/model, masked GET, full PUT and recognized per-key PUT from exact upstream source. Seed missing defaults through the settings owner without overwriting existing rows; the migration remains unseeded. Preserve secret/UUID/normalization semantics and exact masking; no raw-secret API, eager external connection, SMTP retry engine, cron engine, OIDC/S3/postback/POP3 implementation, admin reload endpoint or dropped update-check mechanism. app.check_updates is false/no-op in native defaults and cannot be enabled; show that dropped-operation difference precisely. No new runtime dependency or schema. Further concrete differences need lead disposition while independent work continues.

- **2026-10-29 typed JSON clarification:** recognized per-key settings JSON uses the same Go invalid-UTF-8 replacement decoding as full PUT before candidate validation. Structurally valid strings containing invalid UTF-8 become well-formed replacement strings and succeed normally; pinned per-key RawMessage instead reaches PostgreSQL and returns 500/no write. Pair this exact outcome and full snapshot rollback in the reference corpus. The benefit is consistent full/key string semantics and well-formed validated storage instead of exposing database encoding failures. Invalid JSON syntax, unknown/read-only keys, bad field shapes and invalid final candidates still reject atomically under D44; no blanket error normalization or change to XML decoding.

## D45. Capture each session lifetime at creation

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review, to complete M1.2 under D44.

- **Evidence:** current user-store lookup derives expiry from created_at plus the supplied current AuthPolicy duration. A live settings getter alone would retroactively shorten/extend sessions and could revive an already expired row. The draft's blanket 501 is honest staging but is not completed M1.2 behavior.
- **Decision:** authorize the narrow src/users/store.ts, auth.ts and login.tsx seam (and owned types/tests) to persist creation-time expiry in existing session JSON. Capture one current duration when a password-only or completed-TOTP session is actually created; use that same captured lifetime for both cookie Max-Age values. Lookup/revalidation uses the saved expiry, not the current settings duration. No schema/new table, sliding refresh, reissuing old identities or raw session-data endpoint.
- **Compatibility/safety:** legacy rows without the new expiry field retain the pre-M1.2 production seven-day lifetime from their original created_at, independent of current settings. A present-but-invalid expiry is denied, never treated as legacy. Validate finite/representable expiry and creation timestamps; reject future-created/expired/malformed records safely. Keep all user/grant/credential/TOTP revocation and CSRF/origin checks, automatic identity assumptions and injected-clock boundaries. Old expired sessions stay expired after any duration increase, including across restart.
- **Configured duration validation:** native settings must express a finite, representable duration of at least one second; accept the explicit day-unit convention used by the seven-day default along with supported duration units, with precise binding/normalization tests. Invalid, nonpositive, subsecond or overflowing stored policy changes return safe 400 with no settings/policy write, rather than upstream's accepted blob followed by hidden seven-day auth fallback. Injected synthetic test policies can still use small positive lifetimes for clock-driven tests; do not impose a new arbitrary production maximum or change unrelated duration grammars such as Go mailbox scan intervals.
- **Acceptance:** exercise live full/per-key changes, old/new/expired sessions, shorter and longer policies, cookie lifetime consistency, password-only and TOTP creation, concurrent/in-flight changes, retained permission revocation, legacy fixture compatibility and restart through public HTTP/process boundaries with injected clock advancement, not sleeps. Preserve matching-secret claims truthfully until M2.1 can observe SMTP authentication. Fix the other reported binding/address/cron/false-update-check defects compatibly, without new policy exceptions or dependencies. No routine owner approval is needed; completion stays in the existing M1.2 PR and normal fixer flow without duplicate writers.

## D46. Reject NUL in final writable settings values

Decided by agents on 2026-10-28, for owner review, after the M1.2 completion fixer.

- **Evidence:** pinned PostgreSQL rejects a settings candidate containing a final writable NUL with 500/no write, while the native draft accepts and stores it. D43 is subscriber-specific and did not authorize this settings difference.
- **Decision/benefit:** after typed binding, duplicate/null handling, normalizations and secret retention, reject NUL in final writable settings string values or map keys with safe 400 Invalid data. and no write. Apply identically to full and recognized per-key candidates, atomically before any row changes. Avoid persisting text/credentials that downstream native APIs may truncate or reject and avoid reporting a database problem for invalid client input. Do not strip it silently or fabricate PostgreSQL errors.
- **Precision:** inspect the actual normalized candidate that would be persisted, not ignored raw metadata or overwritten members. D14 bootstrap-root projection remains read-only and is not newly validated as HTTP input. Values whose legitimate pinned normalization removes the NUL do not fail solely for their original spelling. Preserve D44/D45 fields/policy/secret state and all-or-nothing updates. Document exact upstream 500/no-write versus native 400/no-write cases through public GET/PUT; no new dependency, schema or broad parser exception.

## D47: Pin timezone identifiers and reference zone data

Decided by agents on 2026-10-29, for owner review, during M1.2.

- **Evidence:** native cron validation embeds the complete case-sensitive identifier set from Go 1.27.2 lib/time/zoneinfo.zip, IANA 2026c, SHA-256 b2d18a7c8fa8142097a48c99609fb3c92db5ee98bc740294e57eab8ae9f94779. It accepts US/Eastern, while the unchanged pinned reference on this host rejects that alias because its system zoneinfo lacks it. Both outcomes were reproduced through full settings PUT and masked GET, including complete rollback on rejection. Go time.LoadLocation consults ZONEINFO before system sources; the discrepancy is installed data, not case-insensitive parsing.
- **Native policy/benefit:** validate named cron locations against that pinned complete identifier set, retaining Go's empty, UTC and Local special names. Reject other names with the existing safe unknown-location error and no write. This preserves valid IANA aliases and gives the single executable portable configuration validation without Intl's broader aliases/offset syntax, host filesystem requirements or network access. Host-only additions outside the pinned set are intentionally unsupported. This does not implement a scheduler or timezone-offset engine.
- **Reference alignment:** authorize a narrow shared-reference task in the M1.2 PR: checksum-cache that exact Go archive through test/reference/prepare.ts and pass its absolute path as ZONEINFO only to the unmodified pinned listmonk process in run.ts. Add paired US/Eastern acceptance and wrong-case rejection cases. Preserve the listmonk commit/binary, API assertions, credentials, SQL, all Linux gates and resource ownership/cleanup. Never normalize away a status/body mismatch. Cached corrupt data fails closed rather than being replaced silently.
- **Limits:** Go retains its system fallback for names absent from ZONEINFO; providing the archive is not proof of identical arbitrary host extensions. The native supported set remains explicit. Document the original unaligned reproduction and new aligned results, and keep all pre-existing grammar/error and D44–D46 tests. No runtime dependency, schema, extra release artifact, external production timezone file or broad reference-harness rewrite.

## D48: Discard truncated settings array elements during ordered binding

Decided by agents on 2026-10-29, for owner review, after M1.2 council round 3.

- **Evidence:** repeated smtp JSON members can decode arrays of lengths 2, 1, then 2. Go encoding/json shortens the slice length without clearing its backing capacity, so an omitted second element can return with its old enabled flag, name, host and port. The round-3 B public reproductions are retained in the M1.2 review record. This is observable saved configuration driven by allocator storage beyond the current logical array, not an explicit value in the final element.
- **Decision/benefit:** ordered settings binding keeps only the current logical array. Shrinking discards truncated elements; a later growth binds newly exposed elements from their field zero values. Preserve ordinary repeated-field prefix merging, explicit values and existing null/empty rules. Prevent discarded SMTP or role configuration from reappearing because a decoder retains hidden capacity, and keep configuration independent of allocation history. No broad duplicate-key rejection or change to persistence/secrets/validation is authorized.
- **Contract:** record the exact difference. With the only enabled SMTP server in the discarded element, reference succeeds and retains it; native rejects the final candidate with the existing missing-default-server 400 and no write. With the first server enabled, both succeed, but native's new second element has zero values while reference exposes its retained second element. Assert masked HTTP projections and complete rollback, never private allocation state. Native does not emulate Go's backing-array retention; other ordered binding and API behavior remains pinned unless separately documented. No new runtime dependency, schema or cross-owner seam.

## D49: Activate bounded SMTP tests without global log disclosure

Decided by agents on 2026-10-29, for owner review, before M2.1.

- **Evidence:** pinned TestSMTPSettings constructs a one-connection temporary pool, sends a diagnostic message, then returns the process-wide buffered log lines. Construction/send errors expose library/provider text. Native has error-only logging and no global buffer; returning unrelated process history would disclose internals and couple the API to logging implementation.
- **HTTP contract:** replace M1.2's guarded 501 with a real SMTP test behind existing settings:manage authentication and cookie/Origin guards. Revalidate after body reading and immediately before dispatch. Preserve safe pinned semantic errors such as missing email. Use 400 Invalid data. for malformed JSON/field shapes and 400 Invalid SMTP configuration. for unusable configuration; no network on these failures. On SMTP acceptance return 200 with data:[] instead of global log lines. SMTP acceptance is not final delivery. Transport/auth/send failures return safe 500 SMTP test failed.; ambiguous outcomes return safe 500 SMTP test outcome is unknown. Do not expose credentials, keys, SMTP transcripts, raw provider/library errors or unrelated logs in responses or application logs. Pair these precise differences; do not broadly normalize other routes.
- **Bounds:** reuse the existing 64 KiB body reader and admit at most four simultaneous tests per injected messaging instance, fail-fast beyond that with 429 Too many SMTP tests in progress. and no connection. Each test forces one connection and bounded two-second pool idle/wait behavior as pinned, with finite connection/command timeouts and final cleanup. Release capacity on every completion/failure; close owned transports. Test capacities with explicit barriers, not sleeps. No process-global pool/counter, network in a write transaction or automatic retry/requeue; D12/D24 apply.
- **Scope:** the lead authorizes only the settings SMTP-handler callback and concrete main/shutdown wiring seam plus the messaging owner and tests. A request authorized when SMTP dispatch begins is an admitted operation; later revocation cannot promise to undo an already-started SMTP transaction. Held-body/capacity-denial cases must not dispatch. No sender/outbox/transactional API or background loop is introduced in M2.1.

- **2026-10-29 deadline clarification:** an inactivity timeout alone cannot bound a peer that keeps sending data. The production messaging owner imposes an absolute 60-second lifetime from dispatch, including queue time, and 10 seconds for a temporary SMTP test. Shutdown stops new admission immediately, drains for at most five seconds, then cancels outstanding owned operations and closes their sockets/transports. Start this shutdown before awaiting HTTP drainage, so a held SMTP-test response cannot prevent the deadline from starting. Use the injected clock for owner deadlines; tests advance it at protocol barriers. Deadline expiry or forced closure is ambiguous unless independent evidence already proves non-acceptance; preserve the predetermined Message-ID and available recipient/stage evidence, never requeue, and do not claim that local cancellation undoes remote acceptance. Merely racing a promise without cancelling owned work is insufficient. These finite limits bound resources and shutdown; they are not a provider delivery guarantee or a new configurable settings/API field.

## D50: Configure optional DKIM through SMTP settings

Decided by agents on 2026-10-29, for owner review, before M2.1.

- **Design/evidence:** Sending already requires configurable DKIM; M0.8 verified nodemailer's rsa-sha256/relaxed signature with a synthetic 2048-bit key. Pinned listmonk's SMTP model has no DKIM fields. An internal-only option would not make the promised production setting usable.
- **Native-only schema:** each SMTP entry may have dkim:{domain_name,key_selector,private_key} or null. Omit the object from projection when unconfigured, preserving the existing SMTP shape. A configured object requires a valid nonempty signing domain/selector and an unencrypted RSA private key of 2048–8192 bits, at most 16 KiB, supporting the verified rsa-sha256 mode. Reject invalid, incomplete or unsupported candidates atomically with safe 400 Invalid DKIM configuration.; no raw key/OpenSSL diagnostics. No DNS lookup or DNS-verification claim.
- **Secrets:** GET masks private_key, never returns PEM/key bytes. Empty private_key retains the prior key for the last matching saved SMTP UUID, consistent with the existing password rule; explicit dkim:null clears/disables the whole DKIM configuration. A new configured entry without a valid key is rejected. Preserve D44 full/per-key candidate validation, D46 final-NUL checks and D48 logical arrays. Only settings owns persistence; messaging consumes a concrete unmasked owner view. No schema/table or runtime dependency.
- **Activation/evidence:** M2.1 authorizes the narrow defaults/model/normalization/masking extension and concrete current-config wiring. New sends use current captured signing configuration; in-flight sends retain their captured snapshot without requeue. Verify actual received signatures and a body-tamper negative control through local Mailpit in Bun and compiled fixtures, including masked GET/empty-retain/null-clear/restart behavior. Mark the DKIM field and signatures as native-only against reference; do not claim upstream parity or actual DNS/provider delivery. Synthetic keys/credentials belong in regular repository Markdown. No production key material, file/URL attachment fetching, key service or extra release artifact.

## D51: Require configured SMTP authentication and expose partial acceptance

Decided by agents on 2026-10-29, for owner review, after M2.1 council round 1.

Round-2 compatibility clarification: advertised AUTH does not waive Go's PLAIN
transport guard. PLAIN requires TLS or the exact server name localhost,
127.0.0.1 or ::1; refuse other unencrypted names before sending credentials,
including the owned 127.0.0.2 regression peer. Required implicit TLS/STARTTLS
must complete before authentication. Preserve single-mailbox input per To
element and per upstream-split Cc/Bcc element; a recipient list in one element
fails locally with no SMTP effect and the safe SMTP-test 500. Go-compatible
quoted-local parsing and simple Unicode casing choose routing buckets. Saved
null header-map elements mean empty maps, matching their accepted settings
shape. These correct compatibility/security defects, not additional policy
exceptions; the additive parsed-address owner API preserves existing settings
validation behavior.

- **Authentication evidence:** both independent reviewers reproduced plain/login/cram configuration becoming unauthenticated delivery when the peer does not advertise AUTH. Nodemailer's pool skips login unless allowsAuth or forceAuth is true; pinned smtppool rejects missing AUTH before sending. Merely supplying credentials is not enforcement. Require the explicitly configured mechanism to be advertised and authentication to finish successfully before MAIL/DATA. No automatic mechanism selection or unauthenticated fallback. Use the supported forceAuth/customAuth boundary to enforce the selected mechanism without private connection inspection. Reject absent/unsupported advertised mechanisms before sending credentials; this also deliberately rejects a noncompliant peer that would accept an unadvertised mechanism, rather than disclosing a configured secret to it. Keep empty/none explicitly unauthenticated. Owner-authentication failures before MAIL are proven non-acceptance; interrupted/uncertain observations keep D24's conservative classification. Protected tests use safe 500 SMTP test failed., never raw capability/auth/provider text. Pair missing AUTH in all three modes against reference with zero message intake and retain successful and wrong-password cases.
- **Partial-recipient evidence:** pinned smtppool aborts before DATA on any rejected RCPT. Nodemailer continues with accepted recipients; the native owner exposes accepted and rejected subsets after the final successful SMTP response. An accepted To and rejected Cc produced reference500/zero intake versus native200/one intake in the protected test, without a declared difference.
- **Decision/benefit:** retain native per-recipient SMTP acceptance instead of letting one invalid address prevent delivery to other accepted recipients. The owner returns status accepted with its accepted/rejected subsets; that status means some SMTP acceptance, not acceptance of every recipient or final delivery. Never replay accepted recipients, automatically retry rejected recipients, or turn partial acceptance into proven non-acceptance. D12/D24 and later caller-owned durable policy remain unchanged. If the protected diagnostic has any rejected recipients after partial acceptance, return safe HTTP 500 with SMTP test was accepted for some recipients only. instead of 200/data:[]. This makes the diagnostic truthful without exposing addresses/provider text or implying nothing was sent. It does not undo delivery or authorize retry. Full acceptance retains 200/data:[]; all-recipient rejection and ambiguous outcomes retain D49's errors. Pair accepted To with rejected Cc and Bcc: native500/partial-message/one intake, reference500/before-DATA/zero intake, one attempt each. Existing owner subset/no-duplicate fault tests remain mandatory. No retry engine, schema, dependency or new endpoint.

## D52: Route SMTP by the actual domain of a quoted mailbox

Decided by agents on 2026-10-29, for owner review, after M2.1 council round 3.

- **Evidence:** Go mail.ParseAddress unquotes a local part, so Sender <"sender@x"@route.test> becomes sender@x@route.test. Pinned email.getPool checks that full address, then strings.Cut at the first @; with mappings x@route.test and route.test it selects the x@route.test bucket. Native takes the separator before the actual domain and selects route.test. Both independent reviewers reproduced the different received routing headers through pinned TX and native owner callers, with a valid envelope Return-Path.
- **Decision/benefit:** preserve exact full-address matching first, then route by the actual mailbox domain after the last @ in the parsed address. A quoted local part must not select a configured bucket for a different address merely because its content resembles an address suffix. This keeps provider/domain configuration meaningful and avoids sending through an unintended configured provider. Do not restore the upstream first-separator bug. Enabled/excluded/named selection, Go quoted-local parsing/simple casing and random choice within the selected bucket remain unchanged.
- **Precise difference:** From Sender <"sender@x"@route.test>, no full-address match, valid Return-Path, servers mapped to x@route.test and route.test: pinned selects the first mapping, native the second, one accepted Mailpit message each. Pair those exact outcomes in the routing corpus; ordinary/quoted-simple/Unicode full-address and domain controls still agree. No broad normalization, new endpoint, dependency or schema.
- **Other round-3 finding:** restore compatible challenge-driven LOGIN: decode each challenge, respond to Username: or Password: regardless of order, and reject unknown challenges without transmitting a response. Preserve D51 advertisement enforcement, TLS guard, safe errors, absolute deadlines and no resend. The LOGIN correction is not a new deliberate difference.

## D53. Durable outbox boundaries and native send-log contract

Decided by agents on 2026-10-29, for owner review. M2.2 implements this boundary; production marketing remains staged until M2.4.

- **Owner and staging:** messaging owns `sends`, its SQL, prepared-message persistence, recovery and the outbox loop. M2.3 owns the real tx producer, idempotency API and template rendering; sequence production stays with M5. M2.2 uses a separately compiled test-only producer to exercise the actual owner until these exist, with real production send-log routes. No production enqueue/debug route or fixture flag. Prepared payloads are versioned immutable bounded JSON with inline message bytes encoded as base64; no transport credentials, file paths or URL attachments. Payload is internal, absent from both collection and detail projections. One send has one primary recipient; any accepted Cc/Bcc subset must never be replayed.
- **Durability:** assign the send UUID and `<send-uuid@root-domain>` before enqueue commit and force that ID through messaging even if a caller or SMTP setting supplied another Message-ID. `queued -> sending`, attempt increment and lease commit before SMTP; network is outside the transaction. Persist `sent` only for actual acceptance; any partial acceptance is sent once with a safe partial diagnostic, never retried. Any uncertainty is `unknown`. A row found `sending` at process startup becomes `unknown` before claims, irrespective of lease expiry. Lease expiry alone never authorizes an automatic SMTP replay. Do not reset these rows to queued, including when a crash probably preceded SMTP. Before-claim queued work remains eligible. Shutdown closes claims first, starts the existing five-second messaging drain immediately, then records outcomes before closing SQLite. No HTTP drain postpones that deadline.
- **Retry proof:** extend the concrete M2.1 outcome with safe retry disposition, without exposing raw provider strings. Transactional auto-retry is allowed only when the owner proves no recipient was accepted and a transient failure is positively identified: actual TCP connection refusal, an explicit pre-MAIL AUTH 4xx, or complete all-recipient RCPT rejection with at least one explicit 4xx and none accepted. Permanent-only 5xx, local invalid message/configuration, missing/unsupported AUTH or unproven failure are not retry reasons. A generic code, timeout, disconnect, canceled active operation, DATA/final-ACK failure or accepted subset cannot supply the proof. Keep all other M2.1 ambiguity classifications conservative. Retry the same journal row/message ID after 1, 2, 4, then 8 seconds on the injected clock, for at most five SMTP attempts total. Marketing never automatically retries, even after proven transient non-acceptance. Library requeues remain zero.
- **Schema evolution:** keep migration 0001 intact. Authorize a small forward migration 0002 adding nullable `next_attempt_at`, nullable self-reference `retry_of` (ON DELETE SET NULL), a unique non-null retry_of index and a due-work index on sends. The only shared database change is embedding that migration in the production manifest. Keep FULL/WAL, non-reusing IDs, runner/snapshot semantics and all existing tables unchanged. Verify opening a disposable database created by the exact merged M2.1 executable preserves existing users/settings/auth and takes the expected pre-migration snapshot; the old executable correctly rejects the newer version. This avoids silently stranding existing version-1 data.
- **Native HTTP:** GET `/api/sends` and `/api/sends/:id` require the already recognized `sends:get`; POST `/api/sends/:id/resend` requires the already recognized `sends:manage`. These are explicit global send-journal grants, including external recipients; a list role or tx:send alone does not grant them. Preserve the existing primordial-role permission representation and its super-admin bypass, rather than extending the strict stored legacy permission array. Use current auth, credential precedence, Origin/CSRF and live mutation revalidation. Do not expose provider text, bodies, headers, attachments, payload or idempotency keys. Safe public metadata: id, uuid, source, kind, subscriber_id, to_email, template_id, enrollment_id, step_id, list_id, status, skip_reason, smtp_message_id, error, attempts, retry_of, next_attempt_at, created_at and sent_at. IDs retain exact signed-64-bit JSON representation; error is a bounded owner-controlled category, not an exception message.
- **Collection:** normal data envelope containing `{results,total,page,per_page}`, default page 1/per_page 20, per_page at most 100, page at most 1,000,000. Strict positive decimal parameters; fixed id-descending order. Optional exact enum status/kind/source filters and positive signed-64-bit subscriber_id/list_id filters; invalid recognized values return safe 400. No SQL query input, payload search or unrestricted ordering. Individual missing rows return 404. Keep existing route-ID/overflow/method behavior.
- **Manual resend:** accept an empty request body or JSON `{}` only, with the existing 64 KiB read bound. Return 200 with the new row's safe projection. Only `failed` and `unknown` originals can create a child; queued/sending/sent/skipped give safe 409. In one transaction, revalidate authority and eligibility, look for an existing retry_of child first, then create at most one child with fresh UUID/message ID, attempts zero and queued status. A repeated/concurrent request returns that same child's current projection, even if its status changed; a later explicit resend of a failed/unknown child can extend the chain. Keep the original unchanged and retain retry_of provenance. Do not copy unique idempotency_key or enrollment/step claim identity to a child. Missing retained payload gives safe 409. Manual resend of unknown is an intentional new send that can duplicate earlier uncertain delivery; never describe it as automatic recovery or exactly-once. No network runs in the route transaction.
- **Eligibility and marketing staging:** recheck current linked-subscriber identity/blocklist immediately before dispatch and on manual resend using subscriber-owned functions, not messaging SQL into subscriber tables. Preserve original recipient identity: deleted/replaced linked identities must not become external recipients or send to a new address owner. M2.4 owns the full marketing consent/cap/unsubscribe policy. Until it supplies that concrete policy, production marketing dispatch/resend fails closed with a safe skipped policy-unavailable result (or 409 for manual resend), never sends mail. A compiled fixture may inject an explicit marketing policy to exercise the same outbox's at-most-once/no-retry mechanics, clearly labelled as component evidence. M2.3 must keep marketing staged until M2.4; do not invent completed caps or one-click functionality. Transactional external recipients remain supported by the owner and require no synthetic subscriber creation.
- **Acceptance/benefit:** crash barriers before claim, after durable claim, during DATA, after sink acceptance without ACK, after ACK before journal commit and after sent commit; restart through real stores/routes and prove no automatic duplicate. Test durable retry deadlines, permanent and mixed/partial outcomes, concurrent resends, held-body revocation, restart and safe projections through public HTTP/SMTP boundaries. Source-derived M2.1 SMTP regressions remain required. New native endpoints are design-first, not invented upstream parity. Include serial before/after measurements on final code and preserve the existing two-job Linux-only CI.

## D54. Template ownership, safe updates and bounded Liquid rendering

Decided by agents on 2026-10-29, for owner review. Prospective M1.8 authorization; implementation and acceptance are pending.

- **Scope and bootstrap:** templates own their table, SQL, Liquid engine, compiled cache, embedded built-ins and HTTP handlers. Initialize the default campaign layout, default archive layout and sample transactional template in one application-owned transaction only when the templates table is empty, using automatic IDs; omit the deferred visual sample. Existing nonempty tables are not overwritten/reseeded. M2.3/M3/M5 own delivery callers, tracking and consent; M4 owns full admin screens. A separately compiled fixture can exercise the concrete renderer until those callers exist. No production debug/render endpoint beyond the ported previews, no Go-template converter or visual builder. campaign_visual writes return safe400; the type remains in the schema for the deferred feature.
- **HTTP and updates:** preserve the pinned eight API routes, templates:get/manage, response shapes/no_body, ordering, ordinary validation/not-found/default/deletion semantics except precisely marked differences. PUT uses the stored immutable type, rejects an explicit conflicting type and validates the resulting writable candidate. Omission retains stored values, explicit empty subject/body/body_source clears where valid, explicit null body_source clears; tx subject remains required and campaign subject stays empty. Fix upstream SQL's empty-subject-to-name assignment and raw-type validation bypass. Ignore response-only metadata, not malformed documents. Reject NUL in final writable strings with safe400/no mutation. Search uses normalized literal case-insensitive name substrings instead of PostgreSQL stemming/wildcards, as other native label owners do. Native identities retain exact signed64-bit behavior, not PostgreSQL int32 storage errors.
- **Default and references:** set-default validates an existing campaign target before atomically changing flags; invalid/missing/tx targets return safe400 without clearing the current default (upstream can clear every flag). Keep default/last-template deletion guards. Before deleting another layout, a concrete campaign-owned seam retargets primary campaign.template_id references to the current default within the short transaction. Historical/other nullable references retain D25/FK semantics; no campaign/send/sequence workflow is invented. No SQL from templates into campaign-owned tables or network in a transaction.
- **Finite input/output:** template body and body_source each at most64KiB UTF-8, subject at most2KiB, request at most512KiB. A rendering context is recursively copied plain finite data: at most256KiB encoded, depth16,10000 nodes,1024 entries per array/object and64KiB per string; reject cycles, accessors/executable/class values and nonfinite numbers without exposing host objects. Produce dense owned arrays and null-prototype records, discard inherited lookup/iteration and deny prototype magic names. These are capability/work bounds, not a claim of an absolute process heap limit.
- **Rendering budgets:** each body/layout/final HTML or plain output at most1MiB UTF-8 and subject at most2KiB. Charge a shared per-message4MiB cumulative emission/intermediate-string budget, including captures, filter results and trusted composition, before exceeding accumulation/allocation; a final size check alone is insufficient. Bound cumulative iterations/tag/filter work at10000, nesting32, and individual generated ranges/arrays at1024; preflight potentially expanding operations before allocation. Retain finite engine parse/render/memory options as defense in depth; renderLimit is cooperative, not hard preemption. Use only a documented reviewed tag/filter allowlist, including the design's default/upcase/strip/date/raw/t/track_link basics and condition/iteration/assign/capture needed by built-ins. Deny unreviewed/unknown capabilities explicitly. Bound any compiled cache (at most128 entries and4MiB retained source), invalidate on update/delete, no process-global registry or mutable render context.
- **Dependency seam:** prefer pinned LiquidJS10.30.0 public emitter/context/tag/filter APIs. If those cannot enforce the D23 boundary for nested captures/filters, authorize a minimal version-pinned patches/liquidjs@10.30.0.patch plus bun.lock/package patch registration solely to propagate one per-render budget through those paths. No alternate engine or broad library fork. Tests must prove the actual source and compiled production renderer enforces the same boundary; no private-source assertions. marked is already a listed dependency; M1.8 may add a verified, exactly pinned compatible version for Markdown, include its notices and keep all parsing/rendering local. Post-Markdown output is also bounded before excessive accumulation.
- **Capabilities and composition:** template lookup resolves only explicitly allowlisted in-memory embedded names, using own-name checks and no filesystem/URL fallback. User-authored templates cannot include arbitrary DB templates or dynamic paths; the worker documents its finite supported include/render/layout policy. HTML values escape by default, plain/subject do not; explicit raw is intentional user-authored HTML. Already rendered body enters a layout exactly once through content|raw. A campaign layout must actually contain an executable content output slot, not just that text in a comment/raw literal. Budget checks cover body, Markdown and layout composition together. date now/today uses the injected clock and UTC, not ambient wall time/timezone. Expose translation as a bounded t filter, never a callable Translation instance.
- **Later-owner data:** the documented subscriber/campaign/data/trigger/sequence/step variables are plain data, not ORM/service objects. Explicit caller-supplied URL fields are validated HTTP(S) links before built-in HTML attribute use. Until tracking owns a real link builder, track_link validates and returns its HTTP(S) input without recording/fetching, and track_view is empty. No fake working endpoints. Transactional unsubscribe_url is empty; detect a real reference at save and issue a safe attention diagnostic without logging template/data values. Pure built-ins for opt-in/status/import/reset/export are converted and renderable, but do not claim their later workflows/send effects. The existing protected SMTP test may consume the converted built-in through a narrowly injected renderer, preserving all D49-D52 auth/deadline/capacity/SMTP behavior and shared contracts.
- **Preview isolation and diagnostics:** stored and posted previews render synthetic bounded data, not private subscribers. Return HTML with CSP sandbox/default-src none (inline style and data images only), no scripts/popups/forms/base navigation, and nosniff. This intentionally tightens upstream's allow-scripts/popups preview. Preserve safe localized compile/render/field context but omit submitted source/data/URLs and raw library/storage diagnostics; bad syntax/limits400, request-too-large413, render-capacity exhaustion429. Revalidate live authority after held input/async rendering and before effects. No general HTML sanitizer or context-aware safety claim for arbitrary user-authored HTML.
- **Acceptance and reason:** source-first paired metadata/CRUD/default/search/preview/validation contracts with exact named differences, native Liquid examples rather than Go parity claims, compiled restart and isolated filesystem/network traps, inherited/accessor/capture/filter/Markdown/output exhaustion tests, independent instances, current permissions and immutable concurrent updates. Bounded rendering prevents authored templates from gaining host capabilities or unbounded expansion; safe update/default semantics preserve caller intent and a usable default. Sending/storage changes include final serial ext4 FULL/WAL/common API and synthetic-versus-production rendering measurements, with no throughput/heap/preemption/delivery guarantee or CI timing threshold.

## D55. Bound serialized template context before reflection and preserve sandbox CI

Decided by agents on 2026-10-29, for owner review. Prospective M1.8 completion authorization after the initial draft; not an acceptance claim.

- **Evidence:** draft8990057 uses Object.getOwnPropertyNames on caller-owned JavaScript objects before checking count. Large non-enumerable property sets allocate a key list before the copier can reject. Earlier for-in does not bound hidden own names or VM enumeration work. HTTP JSON callers do not need a general executable-object interface. Markdown also remains unimplemented because a final marked-output length check cannot bound expansion during parsing/rendering.
- **Decision:** make the concrete production renderer's untrusted data entry accept a primitive serialized JSON string of at most256KiB UTF-8, checked before JSON.parse/reflection. Require a top-level record, then enforce D54 depth/node/entry/string/prototype-name/nonfinite bounds and copy into dense/null-prototype owned data. Reject non-string inputs immediately without enumeration, coercion, getters, toJSON, valueOf or iterator execution. No compatibility fallback that implicitly stringifies arbitrary objects. Trusted caller composition uses explicit known fields or already bounded JSON from owning stores, not an exported arbitrary-object serializer; enforce component bounds before serialization/merge. Propagate this interface through sync/async/compiled rendering, previews, built-ins, SMTP caller and fixtures/measurements. User-facing template CRUD/preview envelopes and body semantics do not gain a JSON-string field. M2.3/M3/M5 are future internal consumers and will use this concrete boundary.
- **Why:** bounded wire data permits a finite allocation argument before reflection; rejecting executable/class/proxy/sparse/getter-bearing object inputs needs no unsafe inspection. JSON-only data still supports all documented message variables. Remaining magic-name/depth/number/UTF-8 constraints apply after bounded parsing; no process-heap or hostile in-process JavaScript guarantee is claimed. D54's general raw-object acceptance is replaced by this narrower explicit capability boundary, not described as tested and safe by enumerable-key counting.
- **Markdown seam:** retain the D54 output/intermediate/work budgets and implement local Liquid-then-marked rendering. Authorize exact marked15.0.12 after verifying its primary metadata/source/license and embedding notices (the draft worker inspected this candidate but did not install it). Prefer a reviewed bounded token/emitter/renderer path. If public hooks cannot stop allocations/expanding references before concatenation, authorize a minimal patches/marked@15.0.12.patch plus lock/package registration solely to propagate the existing message budget through those paths, analogous to the authorized Liquid patch. No broad fork, alternate Markdown implementation, post-hoc-only check or independent uncharged capture/parser budget. Test repeated references/URLs, nested token output, escaping and final layout, source and compiled.
- **CI evidence and authority:** inspected push job123281067803 of run40823480063 failed templates:compiled with bwrap setting up uid map: Permission denied, before the isolated program ran. The other reference job failed too; its log was unavailable through gh while that run remained active, so no separate cause is claimed. Keep the isolated compiled gate. Authorize narrowly scoped setup/invocation on disposable GitHub-hosted Linux runners to grant bwrap its required namespace ability (for example CI-only privileged bwrap invocation or a specific runner permission setup), with an explicit capability probe and diagnostics. Do not silently skip isolation, replace it with an unsandboxed pass or mutate this VM's sysctls/AppArmor/memory/security policy. Both existing Linux jobs remain; no platform expansion. Document runner setup separately from product guarantees.
- **Acceptance:** reject large enumerable/non-enumerable/symbol/accessor/class/proxy/cyclic caller objects without touching them; valid bounded JSON still enforces exact/one-past D54 limits and inherited/magic-name protection. Complete Markdown and admission/concurrency/authority/source/compiled matrix from the original finite brief. Correct the actual CI environment failure, rerun changed code/gates and refresh final serial measurements/hashes. Historical generic upstream settings failure remains cause-unknown unless reproduced/diagnosed; never erase it with an unconditional rerun.

## D56. Owner-directed models, review and local verification

Decided by the owner on2026-10-29, relayed by the iglo.base chief of staff; applies to iglo.email, superseding earlier role/quota/council/CI policies.

- **Models:** lead, workers, fixers and normal helpers use Claude Opus5.5 medium (`claudeAgent`, `claude-opus-5-5`, target.options effort medium). Claude Haiku5.5 medium is only for bulk mechanical work. OpenAI is only one GPT-6.1 Sol xhigh reviewer. No Terra or second Sol reviewer; the lead and that reviewer form the council. Only new dispatches change; current M1.8 stays on its branch and its legacy B review finishes without replacement.
- **Review:** lead reads and dispositions every finding, fixes small agreed issues, batches all other agreed findings into one fixer, and reasons about disagreements in the PR. Round1 is deep; later rounds review fixes and touched code only. After three rounds without agreement the lead decides and records reasons. No actual security/data failure is waived.
- **Verification:** workers/fixers/reviewer complete full local check and acceptance on this host, one role at a time and in the foreground before ending a turn. CI is workflow_dispatch only and is not a merge gate. Async agent dispatch/end-turn remains required; leaving tests running after an agent turn does not.
- **Capacity/thrift:** at most one implementation worker/fixer and one reviewer, helpers consume the implementation slot, no parallel local verification. Read only needed sections; concise status has one line per merged task and PRs retain details. Preserve existing continuous handoff, unbound45-minute recovery, locks and host limits.
- **Scheduler:** its API exposes no per-task model selection. Set the lead and project default to Opus medium and align the existing unbound prompt; explicitly report that a separately pinned schedule model cannot be set through this API. Quota monitoring can report Sol availability but no longer authorizes role substitution.
- **Reason:** owner prioritizes token thrift and useful verification over duplicate reviews, repeated CI and context overhead. The related iglo.base D25/D26 informed this local policy; no PocketBase-specific assumptions or counts are imported.

## D57. Compatible client results and safe input hardening

Decided by the owner on2026-10-29; refines the default compatibility policy in07-api-and-ui.md and worker/reviewer guidance.

- **Results:** match pinned listmonk results for requests realistic SDKs, n8n integrations, dashboard/browser forms and ordinary HTTP clients send: methods, fields/types, status/body/headers and stored data, subject to existing declared differences. Port upstream logic only where those results depend on it; use Bun/Web native APIs elsewhere when results agree.
- **Exotic input:** malformed or hand-crafted requests must fail safely with no crash, partial/corrupted data, permission/security bypass or unbounded memory. A different exact error/status for an otherwise safely rejected exotic request is hardening backlog, not a merge blocker. Keep the data format and decoder safety intact; this does not authorize accepting invalid data or broadening authority.
- **Blocking findings:** name the realistic client and request, file/line and concrete failure. Hostile-input crashes, partial writes, bypasses and unbounded memory remain actual safety defects; safe error-text mismatch alone does not. Lead explains each agreement/disagreement and backlog disposition in the PR. Existing template/send/auth safety guarantees remain and must be assessed honestly, not weakened by relabelling unsafe behavior as backlog.
- **Consequence:** existing implementations and explicit difference markers stay unless a concrete change is warranted. Future contracts separate real-client result equivalence and hostile-input safety; do not port Go internals merely to reproduce an irrelevant malformed-input diagnostic.

## D58. Stage marketing transactional sends until M2.4

Decided by agents on 2026-10-29, for owner review.

- **Decision:** M2.3 implements `POST /api/tx` fully for `kind: "transactional"` (default). `kind: "marketing"` is validated and journaled, but M2.3 records it as `skipped` with a safe reason until M2.4 provides consent, frequency-cap and unsubscribe policy. It never reaches SMTP before then. Marketing still needs a valid `list_id`.
- **Why:** consent and unsubscribe are mandatory for marketing (04-sending). Sending marketing mail without them, even briefly, is worse than a documented staged skip. The response shape and journal row are already final, so M2.4 changes only the admission result.
- **Finished (M2.4):** the staging is over. Production injects the real consent and frequency-cap policy, and marketing messages carry the unsubscribe URL and RFC 8058 headers ([Sending](04-sending.md#unsubscribe)). `policy_unavailable` remains only for a journal created without a policy.

## D59. Campaign pacing sleeps only the rest of the second

Decided by agents on 2026-10-30, for owner review.

- **Decision:** the campaign runner keeps listmonk's per-worker `app.message_rate`, but a worker waits only until one second has passed since the oldest of its last `message_rate` sends (a rolling per-worker window), instead of a full second after reaching its rate. No one-second window can exceed the rate. `app.concurrency`, the sliding window and the error threshold are unchanged.
- **Why:** M6.1 measured that the full sleep turns any time the shared thread spends elsewhere (event and outbox commits) directly into lost campaign rate. Under mixed load the 50/s target was missed at 8 × 10. The configured rate is a ceiling either way; this only stops the runner sending below it. It's a timing difference, not a result a client can see (D57).
- **Not changed:** FULL synchronous commits (D19, D22) stay. Relaxing durability is an owner decision.

## D60. Explicit trusted proxies for client addresses

Decided by agents on 2026-10-30, for owner review.

- **Decision:** add `security.trusted_proxies`, a list of IPs or CIDRs, empty by default. One function in `src/server/client-address.ts` gives the client address. When the transport peer is in the list, it walks `X-Forwarded-For` from the right past trusted hops and takes the first untrusted address. Otherwise, or with an empty list, it returns the peer and ignores the header. A hop that is not an address stops the walk at the nearest valid hop. A zero prefix (`0.0.0.0/0`, `::/0`) is refused, because it would be the blind trust D26 forbids.
- **Scope:** public rate limits, login and TOTP limits and `privacy.record_optin_ip` all use this function. It extends D26's forwarded-header rule; nothing else reads the header. `security.public_rate_limit` makes the four public budgets per-minute settings with the previous values as defaults, and both keys are native: a listmonk client's full settings update leaves them unchanged, and changes apply without a restart.
- **Why:** in the recommended reverse-proxy deployment every visitor shares the proxy's address, so one client's 30 invalid signups a minute denied all signups. Right-to-left walking is what makes the header safe: a client controls only the left of the chain, and the first entry a trusted proxy recorded is the real peer.
- **Not changed:** the 4,096-entry login table and the 10,000-entry public tables stay bounded per client. A client with many addresses can still spend many budgets; the limits bound abuse and are not a defense against distributed traffic.

## D61. Outbound webhook delivery and privacy

Decided by agents on 2026-10-30, for owner review.

- **Decision:** deliveries follow [Sending](04-sending.md#outbound-webhooks). Configuration is capped at 20 webhooks, which bounds fan-out per event, and each webhook has 4 request slots, so one failing receiver cannot starve another. Migration 10 adds `webhook_deliveries.subscriber_id` and makes the due index per webhook.
- **Privacy:** deleting or wiping a subscriber clears `email` and `external_id` from all their stored deliveries in the same transaction, and an attempt reads its bytes after any DNS step, so a claim taken before the deletion sends the cleared body. `subscriber.deleted` omits the address (`email` is `""`). On a subscriber's own wipe it omits the external ID too (`external_id` is null), and the wipe empties `list_ids` in that event and all their stored deliveries, leaving the numeric ID and UUID. On an administrative delete it keeps `external_id` until that delivery ends. A send to a non-subscriber never stores the address in a delivery, so there is nothing to find by address later.
- **Why:** an app keyed on its own ID must still learn that an administrator removed a subscriber, or its consent data goes stale, and that ID is the app's own key. A wipe is different: the person asked for their data to go, and an external ID is an arbitrary string that can hold an email address, so it cannot stay in a queued row for up to 24 hours. A list ID tied to the kept UUID reveals what the person joined, and a list can name something sensitive. The cost is that an app holding only its external ID cannot match a wipe by that key; the numeric ID and UUID are what it gets.
- **Not chosen:** keeping the address until delivery would leave a wiped address in the database for a day. Omitting `external_id` on administrative deletes would leave apps that hold no `uuid` unable to match the event.
- **Bulk budget:** a bulk action may queue at most 50,000 deliveries and is refused whole above that, before any write ([Sending](04-sending.md#outbound-webhooks), [perf](../perf/M2.6-webhooks.md)). Deliveries share the action's synchronous transaction, so the budget is what bounds its hold on the event loop. Rows are queued with one `INSERT … SELECT` per batch. **Not chosen:** splitting one action across transactions, which would give up its atomicity.
- **Imports:** a CSV import spans many transactions by design ([07](07-api-and-ui.md)), so a check before its first write describes one moment, and memberships and webhooks can change after it. Both of its checks therefore apply. The first pass refuses a file it sees over budget whole, with no write. Each batch then counts, in its own transaction and before its first write, the deliveries it will really queue against one cumulative 50,000 for the import. A batch that would cross it writes nothing and the import ends `failed` with the reason in its log. Earlier batches stay, each with all its webhooks, because their consent changes are committed and a lost consent webhook is the worse failure. So a file is not guaranteed to be refused whole, but no import queues more than the budget. **Not chosen:** blocking membership and webhook changes while an import runs, which would hold other administrators for minutes; a budget that stops short of the limit, which only narrows the race.
- **Maintenance deletes:** `DELETE /api/maintenance/subscribers/:type` also spans many transactions ([07](07-api-and-ui.md)) and follows the import rule. The whole selection is refused with no write if it is over budget at the start. Each batch then counts, in its own transaction and before its first write, the deliveries it will really queue against one cumulative 50,000 for the request, so a change of webhooks during the run cannot discount what is already queued. A batch that would cross it writes nothing and the request ends 400. Earlier batches stay, each with all its webhooks, and a repeat is a new action over what is left, with its own budget. **Not chosen:** recomputing the budget from subscribers removed so far times the present fan-out, which lets a webhook disabled mid-run hide deliveries already queued.
- **At-least-once, not at-most-once:** D12 covers marketing email, where a duplicate harms a person. A duplicate webhook is deduped by `id`, and a lost one leaves an app's consent wrong.

## D62. Read-only helper processes for slow reads

Decided by agents on 2026-10-30, for owner review.

- **Decision:** analytics reads run in short-lived child processes, as subscriber query reads do since M1.7, not on the server's thread. The compiled executable re-runs itself with an internal argument, so the single release artifact stays (D3). This refines "one process" in D4 and the [process model](02-architecture.md#process-model): one process writes and owns everything; a helper opens the file read-only with `query_only`, does one read in one snapshot and exits. D15 is about one audience per instance and is unaffected.
- **Why:** `bun:sqlite` is synchronous and a running SQLite step cannot be stopped, while pixels, redirects and sends share the server's thread. At a million rows an in-process read stalled a pixel-sized write for 2.7 s, and even the six-row dashboard card held the thread for about 55 ms ([measurements](../perf/M3.3-analytics.md)). In a child the worst gap was about 10 ms, and a read can be killed.
- **Budgets:** two children at once, 32 queued reads, 30 s to wait for a slot, 30 s for a child to run, 256 MiB resident per child, 32 MiB per response. Queued reads are refused rather than held, and a child over a limit is killed; the client gets a whole error ([07](07-api-and-ui.md)) and never partial JSON. The parent holds at most two responses of 32 MiB, and briefly a second copy of each as it joins them. After that a response belongs to the HTTP server until it is written or the connection times out. B7 counts those bytes: the reader keeps a process-wide egress budget of 64 MiB for finished responses larger than 64 KiB that are not yet fully written, and a read that meets a spent budget is refused with 503 before its child starts, or when it reaches the front of the queue. A finished response is never cut short, so the worst case is the budget plus one maximum response per running child (64 MiB + 2 × 32 MiB). Responses of 64 KiB or less are sent whole and not counted; the server's connection limits bound those. Cached dashboard summaries are not reader responses and are unchanged.
- **Streaming:** count rows are the only result that grows with the range, so the child streams them from an ordered query, holding one period at a time. The server cannot stream onward, because a child that fails midway must become an error.
- **Cancellation and shutdown:** the server owns every child. Closing the reader stops admission, rejects queued reads, kills running children and waits for them to exit. A client disconnect removes a queued read or kills a running one. A cached dashboard read that starts a refresh does not cancel it on disconnect, because the refresh is shared and fills the rows for everyone.
- **WAL:** a helper holds a read snapshot, so a checkpoint cannot pass it. The 30 s limit bounds one snapshot, not the WAL: readers that overlap continuously keep it from resetting. B7 forces the gap: once reads have overlapped for 15 s without the reader going idle, new reads wait until the running ones finish, and the server then runs `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(PASSIVE)` before the next read starts, so the next write restarts the log. WAL growth under no-gap polling is bounded by about 15 s plus one read of writes, not by the polling ([measurements](../perf/B7-load-memory.md)); the cost is that reads wait up to one read's duration (at most the 30 s limit) once per 15 s of continuous polling. Subscriber query helpers (M1.7) have no gap of their own; they are rare admin operations. `app.cache_slow_queries` still removes dashboard polling from the helpers altogether.
- **Staged selections (B7):** subscriber query helpers write the IDs they select to a file in `stage/` beside the database (one signed 64-bit integer each) and report progress on stdout. The parent opens the file, unlinks it at once and reads 500- or 200-ID batches by position, so a 1,000,000-ID selection costs the parent about a batch, not about 40 MiB of boxed integers. The unlinked file disappears when its descriptor closes, including when the server is killed; the runner removes files left between creation and unlink when it starts. Bulk actions over a query selection read their candidates from the file inside the same write transaction as before, and `per_page=all` pages over 1,000 rows (with or without a raw query) are staged the same way. The pool of two large selections stays, now bounding disk and children rather than memory.
- **Event latency (B7):** a shared write-batching point across loops was tried and rejected; see [the measurements](../perf/B7-load-memory.md). D22 is unchanged.
- **Not chosen:** Bun Workers, because a running SQLite step cannot be stopped (M1.7). A long-lived helper pool, because a process per read is what gives kill, memory return and a fresh snapshot for about 150 ms of start-up. Unbounded `.all()` with a larger memory limit, because the budget would then depend on the data.

## D63. Bounce receipts and retained provider text

Decided by agents on 2026-10-30, for owner review.

> [!NOTE]
> The Mailgun parts of this decision are superseded by [D89](#d89-narrow-complete-product-scope-owner-2026-11-07) and task B35: Amazon SES is the only sending and bounce provider. `bounce_receipts` is retained and now serves Amazon SNS / SES only (`sns:<MessageId>`).

- **Decision:** migration 12 adds `bounce_receipts` (`key`, `expires_at`), committed in the same transaction as the bounce it admits. A key is `token:<token>`, held until the token's signature window ends, or `event:<id>`, held for 24 hours from receipt. Mailgun's retries span hours, and a day covers them.
- **Why durable:** a replay must be refused after a restart, and a failed request must leave no receipt so that its retry can succeed. An in-memory set does the first badly and a table outside the transaction does the second badly.
- **Pruning:** an accepted request removes at most 100 expired receipts and adds at most two, so a backlog shrinks while traffic lasts and stops growing when it does not. Deleting them all in one request held the single writer for 1.9 s over 980,000 rows, and a batch for about 1.3 ms ([measurements](../perf/M3.4-bounces.md)). An expired key still waiting for its turn counts as absent, so the backlog cannot change an answer. A receipt's validity window is bounded. Cleanup runs only on provider traffic, so expired rows stay through an idle period and until later requests drain them, and the row count has no hard limit, since there is no global admission cap beyond the public rate limit.
- **Retained text:** Mailgun's `meta` keeps event ID, event, severity, reason, SMTP code, and the delivery message and description, cut to 1,000 characters with addresses replaced. Everything else in the payload is dropped. The message and description explain a bounce to an operator, but can hold other personal text a pattern cannot find.
- **Wipe and export:** rows with a subscriber go with that subscriber and appear in their export, under the `campaign_views` switch. Rows of unlinked sends are found by address and go on that address's wipe or deletion. A late bounce for an anonymized send is not stored. `bounce.recorded` never carries `meta`.
- **Unreachable rows:** a row for a recipient that disagrees with the delivery has no subscriber or send, so no wipe or export can find it. It keeps only codes and a fixed `source`, which is what makes it safe to keep, and `send_uuid` when a send was identified. A bounce of an address the send's subscriber has since left keeps the send link but no subscriber, so it follows the same rule and counts toward no threshold. The cost is that an operator cannot see why a copy bounced.
- **Not chosen:** storing the copy's address or a hash of it to reach those rows, because that keeps more about someone who is not a subscriber in order to delete it later. Keeping the send link on those rows, because it reads as the send's own delivery failing in the list, the webhook and an app keyed on the send. Dropping `message` and `description` everywhere, because operators then cannot tell a full mailbox from a policy block.

## D64. The upload directory is a fixed boundary inside the data directory

Decided by agents on 2026-10-30, for owner review.

- **Decision:** the real path of `upload.filesystem.upload_path` must be a directory strictly inside the real data directory. This is checked with every symlink resolved, on save (400) and on each file operation. A refused directory found at startup, or later, refuses media and nothing else: the reason is logged at startup and shown in the API error, nothing is served, uploaded, swept or attached, and campaigns with media pause. Only files with a `media` row of the exact stored name are served. The path cannot change while any media row exists. The startup sweep removes only regular files, named as uploads name them, directly in `.iglo-email-uploading` in that directory.
- **Why:** with a lexical check, a symlink to the data directory let an anonymous client download the SQLite database, and any absolute directory became public. Changing the path rebound every row by name, so media URLs returned and `DELETE` removed another directory's files. Each row keeps meaning one location without a new column.
- **Operators:** other storage is mounted at the upload directory, which is a real path inside the data directory. A symlink out of it is refused. Moving media means deleting it first; there is no migration tool. A database that already names a refused directory starts with media refused until the mount is corrected. With no media rows the setting can also be changed through the API; with rows it cannot, so the operator mounts the storage or deletes the media first.
- **Limits:** a check and the file operation after it are separate calls, so someone who can write to the data directory could swap a directory between them. That person can already read the database. Crash-orphaned final files stay on disk unswept and are not served.
- **Not chosen:** exiting at startup on a refused directory, which this decision first said. It holds the same boundary as refusing media does, because the check runs on each file operation, so it adds no protection, and it takes down unsubscribes, transactional mail, webhooks and the settings API over one feature. An allowlist of roots in the bootstrap configuration, because a mount gives the same result with no new setting. Storing a directory on each row, because it adds a schema change to support files scattered across directories. A migration at save time, because copying files inside a settings request is the wrong size of work.

## D65. Adapted browser specs run on Playwright as a development dependency

Decided by agents on 2026-10-30, for owner review.

- **Decision:** `@playwright/test` 1.64.1 is a development dependency, pinned to the release that drives the Chromium revision already in `~/.cache/ms-playwright`. `bun run e2e` runs the adapted upstream specs against the compiled executable, spread over `E2E_WORKERS` independent stacks (default 2), each with its own generated data directory; the specs of one file run serially on one stack. It is local and not a CI gate; no release artifact contains it.
- **Why:** M4 tasks adapt upstream's Playwright specs, and keeping the same runner and locators keeps an adapted spec close to its upstream file. `agent-browser` is kept for the one-off checks each task reports.
- **Details:** spec files end in `.e2e.js` because `bun test` claims `.spec.js`. Mail goes to the loopback SMTP sink of `test/tx/sink.ts` instead of Mailpit, so a run needs no Docker or reference preparation; specs that need Mailpit keep using `bun run reference:run`. Each test is its own client through `security.trusted_proxies`, so a run stays inside the real login limit instead of a test-only bypass.
- **Not chosen:** a hand-written runner over `agent-browser`, which would mean rewriting every upstream spec. Running through `reference:run`, which starts upstream and Postgres that these specs do not use.

## D66. Authored HTML in the admin page goes through an allow-list

Decided by agents on 2026-10-31, for owner review.

- **Decision:** the rich-text editor parses authored HTML with `DOMParser`, in a document with no browsing context, and copies across only listed elements and attributes (`assets/admin/js/richtext/sanitize.js`). No new dependency. The content converters read HTML through an element of such a document, never `innerHTML` on an element of the page, which runs image handlers even detached.
- **Why:** a campaign body is written by whoever holds an API credential and read by an administrator on the admin origin. listmonk mounts it as it is, so `<img onerror>` ran with that administrator's session. Alpine also initialises any element added inside its component, so an `x-init` in a body runs too. A block-list would have to know every such name; the sandboxed preview does not protect an editor.
- **Details:** every entry goes through one function: Squire's `setHTML` and `insertHTML` cover load, value changes, paste, drop and the source and insert dialogs. The tree is copied, not edited, because a `<form>` can shadow its own properties with named controls. Kept: text and the structure, table, text and inline elements of a newsletter, with layout and presentation attributes, `style`, `class`, `id`, `aria-*`, `data-embed`, and `name` on `a` only. URLs keep no scheme (relative, or Liquid such as `{{ unsubscribe_url }}`), `http`, `https`, `mailto`, `tel`, `sms`, `ftp` and `cid`, plus `data:image` as PNG, GIF, JPEG or WebP on `img src`. Removed with contents: scripts, styles, frames, objects, form controls, media, SVG, MathML and anything else not in the HTML namespace. Any other unlisted element goes and leaves its contents; comments go without a mention, as Squire drops them anyway.
- **What a save changes:** nothing until someone edits in the rich-text editor. The editor mounts the sanitized copy and the form keeps the stored body, so a save without an edit, a preview or a switch to another format uses the original. An edit there replaces the body with the editor's copy, and a warning above the editor names what was left out and says to change the format to raw HTML first to keep it. That warning also lists what a paste lost. Raw HTML, Markdown and plain text are unchanged: their editors show text, and mail sending never uses this code.
- **Accepted limits:** `style` is kept as written, because a newsletter needs it, so a body can restyle or cover part of the page it is mounted in, without running anything. Remote images wait for the operator to ask ([D71](#d71-admin-pages-have-a-content-security-policy-and-the-editor-stays-in-the-page)). `id` is kept for anchors and can define a `window` name, which no admin code reads. An orphan `<td>` or `<tr>` loses its tags when parsed as a document. Squire keeps normalising what it mounts, as `strong` to `b`; that is the editor's behaviour, not this list's. Squire's `root.innerHTML` for undo only ever sees this output, which holds no raw-text or foreign elements to reparse differently, and its paste fallback needs a browser without `clipboardData.items`.
- **Not chosen:** DOMPurify, which is a sound option but a runtime dependency for a list this short, and whose defaults keep SVG, which nothing here needs. Isolating the editor in a sandboxed frame, which Squire and the page's toolbars, dialogs and media picker do not support without a rewrite. Rewriting the stored body on load, which would change what is sent without the author doing anything.

## D67. A restored database holds all sending until an operator releases it

Decided by agents on 2026-10-31, for owner review.

- **Decision:** `backup` and the migration runner write a hold into their snapshot itself, after `VACUUM INTO`. A database with a hold sends nothing, by any path, until `iglo.email backup --release-hold --consent-reconciled` ends it on a stopped server. `/health`, reading the admin and API, and every write that records a person's wish keep working. `/health` answers the same bytes, since a balancer's body match would otherwise drop a held instance and the unsubscribes behind it; the hold is an `X-Iglo-Email-Hold` header there and the reason is in the admin shell.
- **Why:** a restore loses the deliveries and opt-outs after the snapshot, so admission checks have nothing to refuse with. A review reproduced a scheduled campaign reaching someone who had unsubscribed, twice, through the documented procedure. At-most-once holds only within one database history, and pausing running campaigns by hand missed scheduled ones, queued mail and sequences. Safety that needs an operator step fails when the step is missed, so the data carries it: a restored database starts held however it was copied.
- **Storage:** a `restore.hold` row in `settings`, which is in every schema version a backup can have, so no migration is needed and none can clear it. `defaults` does not name it, so `GET` and `PUT /api/settings` neither show nor write it. The row stays after a release, with `released_at`, as the record of it. A row that cannot be read counts as a hold. `backup` of a held install keeps the row, so the hold keeps the date its gap began.
- **Sending paths.** One owner, `src/settings/hold.ts`. These are all the ways a message reaches the emailer or a webhook reaches the network, and each has a case in `test/backup/hold.test.ts`:

| Path                                                        | While held                                                           |
| ----------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Campaign runner, running and scheduled                      | Claims nothing; the status stays                                     |
| Outbox loop, marketing and transactional, and its retries   | Claims nothing; rows stay `queued`                                   |
| Outbox dispatch of `POST /api/tx`                           | Refused with 503 before a row exists. `async` messages queue instead |
| `POST /api/sends/:id/resend`                                | 503                                                                  |
| `POST /api/campaigns/:id/test`                              | 503                                                                  |
| `PUT /api/campaigns/:id/status` to `running` or `scheduled` | 503; pausing and cancelling work                                     |
| `POST /api/settings/smtp/test`                              | 503                                                                  |
| Sequence scheduler                                          | Claims nothing; enrollments wait                                     |
| `POST /api/enrollments` with `test`                         | 503                                                                  |
| `POST /api/subscribers/:id/optin`                           | 503                                                                  |
| Opt-in mail from an admin write or a public signup          | The write lands; the mail is a row that stays `queued`               |
| `POST /subscription/export/:subUUID`                        | 503 page                                                             |
| `POST /admin/forgot`                                        | 503 page, for every address alike                                    |
| Webhook delivery                                            | Claims nothing; deliveries stay pending                              |

- **Last gate:** `emailer.push` and `emailer.test` make the same check and throw, so a path added later and missing from this list fails closed. It stands behind the loops rather than replacing them, since a refused push would mark rows failed.
- **Release:** refused without the acknowledgement flag, and refused while anything has the database open, which an EXCLUSIVE lock attempt detects. The server reads the row and never writes it. A hold stops duplicates and sends on stale consent. It does not recover a lost opt-out: the operator does that during the hold, as [the guide](../operations.md#restore) sets out.
- **Account recovery:** a restored database has old passwords and `/admin/forgot` is refused, so `iglo.email reset-password --data-dir <path> --user <name>` sets a password locally. It reads the password from stdin, on a stopped server detected as a release is, and uses the users owner's rule, hash and `setPassword`, which ends that user's sessions. It leaves the hold in place and the second factor required (D30), so getting back in never needs a release. A challenge still follows for a TOTP user. Control of the machine proves the same as the mailbox did, and no more.
- **Pre-migration snapshots:** the application creates them for going back, so they are held like a backup: an avoidable gap otherwise. The hold goes in the snapshot and never in the live database. The runner has no restoring rollback: a failed migration rolls back its own transaction and startup stops, so the live database stays at its version, unheld, and the snapshot stays in `backups/`. An unversioned database is not this application's schema, whatever its tables are called, and gets no hold.
- **Accepted limits:** the hold is in what `backup` and the migration runner write. A disk image or a hand copy does not add one, though a copy of a held database keeps its row, and a pre-migration snapshot from before this rule has none. A running install that takes a backup is not held itself. Rows queued during a hold go out at release, with their consent checked then.
- **Not chosen:** a `--restored` start flag or a procedure of SQL statements, which fail open when forgotten. A new table, which older snapshots lack and which would need `backup` to create it. A hold in the settings API, where one saved form could clear it.

## D68. Anthropic models only, Opus reviewer

Decided by the owner on 2026-11-01; amends D56 and the reviewer it named.

- **Models:** every role uses an Anthropic model. Lead, workers and fixers stay on Opus 5.5 medium, and Haiku 5.5 medium stays limited to bulk mechanical work. Fable is not used. OpenAI, Codex, Sol, Terra and other providers are not used for any role, and nothing falls back to them.
- **Reviewer:** one Claude Opus 5.5 reviewer (`claudeAgent`, `claude-opus-5-5`, effort `xhigh`) replaces Sol. The owner named the model; the lead kept the previous reviewer's `xhigh` depth. This replaces the earlier rule against Claude reviewing Claude work.
- **Independence:** the same model family wrote the code, so independence now comes from process. The reviewer is always a fresh delegate that did not write the code, works from the PR, the brief and its own local checks, and in round 1 receives no lead conclusions.
- **Unchanged:** council size, rounds, finding rules, local verification and everything else in D56 and D57.

## D69. A file lock enforces one process per data directory

Decided by agents on 2026-11-01, for owner review.

- **Decision:** the server holds an exclusive lock on `iglo-email.lock` in the data directory from before it opens the database until after it closes it. A second server exits nonzero with one line naming the directory, and nothing reaches the database. `reset-password` and `backup --release-hold` take the same lock and refuse with the same error. `backup`, `backup --verify`, `--help`, `--version` and the helpers of D62 never touch it.
- **Why:** the leases and at-most-once rules of the loops assume one process, and D4 says so, but nothing enforced it. An accidental second copy could send a message twice.
- **Mechanism:** a separate SQLite file, held by a connection with `locking_mode = EXCLUSIVE` after one small write, with its journal in memory and no sync. That is an fcntl lock on the file, which needs no new dependency. The kernel frees it when the process dies, also on `kill -9`, so there is nothing to clean up. Children do not inherit it, and symlinks or relative paths to one directory meet the same lock. Proved through compiled processes in `test/bootstrap/instance-lock.test.ts`.
- **The write is required:** SQLite opens a file it cannot write read-only, and there `BEGIN EXCLUSIVE` succeeds and holds nothing. So a file owned by another user stops the server with an error instead of turning the lock off, which a lock without a write did when tested.
- **A leftover file:** a kill cannot leave anything to recover, since the only write is one page and no journal reaches the disk. A file that is not a database is emptied in place and locked. It is not deleted: two processes that each delete and recreate it could hold locks on two different files.
- **Starting together:** two processes can each make the other's attempt fail while neither holds the lock, so a busy attempt retries four times within about 50 ms before refusing. One serves; a refusal beside a live server takes those milliseconds longer. Two holders were never seen with or without the retry.
- **Limit:** a POSIX lock needs a local filesystem, which [the guide](../operations.md#one-process-per-data-directory) says, and WAL needs one host anyway. Deleting the file under a running server lets a later process start.
- **Stopped-database check stays:** `withStoppedDatabase` keeps its EXCLUSIVE attempt on the database. The lock answers "is a server running", and that attempt also answers "is anything reading", such as a running backup, as D67 requires.
