- **Why:** the owner wants agents to make any edit or change a setting. The API document already describes every route, so a generated tool cannot drift from the API, and each exclusion is a named decision that a test enforces.
- **How to apply:** a new documented endpoint becomes a tool with no code change. If it must not be an agent action, add it to `EXCLUDED_OPERATIONS` with a reason, or the coverage test fails. Replacing a generated tool with a hand-written one means adding a `HANDWRITTEN_COVERAGE` entry.

## D99. Events grant consent on single opt-in lists (lead, 2026-11-16)

Brief B84, from the first production adopter's integration report: an app grants marketing permission on each purchase, and a read-then-add in the client raced with unsubscribes and had to know about them.

- **Decision:** `POST /api/subscriber-events` and each item of `/batch` accept `subscriber.grant_lists`, 1 to 20 distinct positive IDs of single opt-in lists. Inside the event's transaction, after the subscriber upsert and before any exit or trigger, each list gets a confirmed membership if the subscriber has none; an existing membership is never changed and a blocklisted subscriber gets none. The response's `grants` reports each list as `added`, `member`, `unsubscribed` or `blocklisted`. The table is in [05-sequences.md](05-sequences.md#events-api).
  - Refused with 400 `Invalid fields: subscriber.grant_lists` and nothing written: a list that does not exist, a double opt-in list (a grant cannot replace the opt-in mail), or a malformed field.
  - Needs `subscribers:manage` and manage scope on every listed list, revalidated in the transaction; for an existing subscriber it also needs manage scope on at least one list the subscriber already has a membership on, as PUT's scope check does, so a limited token cannot take over a subscriber outside its lists (an existing subscriber with no membership at all can then be granted only by a token with manage-all); otherwise 403 with nothing written. In a batch, each refusal fails only its item.
  - A repeated `idempotency_key` replays the first request's `grants` and changes nothing.
  - `PUT /api/subscribers/lists` is unchanged and remains the listmonk-compatible admin override that can set any status.
- **Why:** a grant must happen before the event creates its enrollments, because a `PT0S` marketing first step is checked at once; granting afterwards ends the enrollment `unsubscribed`. Doing it inside the event's own transaction makes the consent and the trigger one fact, and it cannot race an unsubscribe, which is a write on the same row.
- **Considered:** the client reads the membership first and skips subscribers that have one (races, and every client must know it); making `PUT /api/subscribers/lists` refuse to reverse an unsubscribe (breaks the listmonk-compatible override that admins use); a separate consent endpoint (a second way to do the same job, D89); granting a double opt-in list (would assert consent the app cannot prove).
- **Storage:** no migration. The replay record in `events.enrollments` is `{started, ended, grants}`; rows written before this decision have no `grants` and replay as none ([03-data-model.md](03-data-model.md#events)).
- **Cost accepted:** the event transaction writes one membership per new grant, and the events token needs `subscribers:manage` as well as manage scope for grants. Measured in [B84-event-grant](../perf/B84-event-grant.md).
- **Revisit:** if an app needs to grant a double opt-in list, or to grant on a list it can only read, that is a new decision, not a flag on this one.

## D100. Sequence steps read event aggregates (lead, 2026-11-16)

Brief B85, from the first production adopter's integration report: a "your month" email sent 30 days after a subscription starts needs a sum, a distinct count and an event count over the app's own events, and a line shown only when a subscriber ever had an event took two steps with opposite conditions and two near-identical templates. The sequence engine had said it does not aggregate, so the app ran a scheduler of its own.

- **Decision:** a sequence step accepts `aggregates`, 1 to 10 named aggregates. Each is an event predicate of the condition language (`event`, `where`, `since`) with `fn`: `count` (the default), `sum` or `distinct` of a `data.*` `field`. The scheduler computes them when the step sends, after the condition and the frequency cap admit it and before rendering, and the template reads them as `aggregates`. The rules and the table are in [05-sequences.md](05-sequences.md#aggregates).
- **Why:** one step then states the totals an app would otherwise compute, and a conditional line becomes one template with `{% if aggregates.x > 0 %}`. The validator and the `since` bounds are the condition language's own, so an aggregate matches exactly the events a condition with the same predicate would count. One dialect, not two (D89).
- **Considered:**
  - `sql` in an aggregate: it would run an arbitrary statement on the scheduler's path; refused.
  - Aggregates on campaigns or outside sequences: no decision reads them.
  - Truncating a large window at a count limit: a partial total presented as complete. Refused; a bound would end the decision instead.
  - Aggregates over another subscriber's events: a different decision, since it crosses the subscriber boundary.
- **Decided details:**
  - `sum` is exact within ±(2^53 − 1). A sum beyond that range, or one that overflows SQLite's 64-bit integers, is not returned rounded: the step is skipped with `aggregate_limit` like an aggregate over the limit, so a total that cannot be exact is never retried into a different answer.
  - `distinct` compares values as `eq` does (`5` and `5.0` one value, `"5"` text, `true` not `1`) and does not count missing values, JSON `null`, objects or arrays, since no `eq` predicate matches the last two. With no value it is `0`.
  - The reads use the read path of the scheduler's other event lookups, one query per aggregate on the subscriber's events, bound to the subscriber, outside any transaction.
  - `aggregates` is one of the fields whose change discards a waiting decision.
- **Bound (lead decision, 2026-11-16; amended in review):** each aggregate reads at most 10,000 events of its `event` name in its window (`AGGREGATE_LIMIT`): the newest 10,001 such events are read (`LIMIT 10001` on the `(subscriber_id, name, occurred_at)` index, bound to the subscriber, with `since` as the lower bound), and `where` is applied to those rows only. So one read costs at most that many rows, whatever `where` keeps, and the reads run on the scheduler's thread. An aggregate whose window holds more than 10,000 events is not truncated and returns no total, whatever `where` matches. The step is not sent: it records a `skipped` send with `skip_reason = 'aggregate_limit'`, logs one attention message without personal data, and advances, whatever `on_false` says. There is no retry loop, and the enrollment is not ended. The first version counted only matching rows, which let ten `where` misses over 100,000 events cost 595 ms per decision on the measuring host (the reviewer measured 729 ms), above the brief's 250 ms; the window bound fixes that case at about 7 ms. Shortening event retention for the events aggregates read was the other option; it is not taken, since retention still bounds what exists and the limit bounds each decision. Measurements: [B85-aggregates](../perf/B85-aggregates.md).
- **Storage:** migration 20 adds `sequence_steps.aggregates`, a nullable JSON object, with `ALTER TABLE`. Steps without aggregates store `NULL`.
- **Cost accepted:** a decision on a step with aggregates reads each aggregate's window, up to 10,001 events of its name. A step without them pays one null check and one more template field. Throughput without aggregates shows no regression ([B85-aggregates](../perf/B85-aggregates.md)).
- **Revisit:** if aggregates must read beyond one subscriber, or if real use shows 10,000 is too low; a new limit needs its own measurements.

## D101. External-ID 404 and test enrollment from an event (lead, 2026-11-16)

Brief B86, from the first production adopter's integration report: a client that syncs by external ID could not tell "no such subscriber" from "malformed selector", since both answered `400`, and the message text had to be matched.

- **Absent external subscriber is 404.** `GET`, `PUT` and `DELETE` on `/api/subscribers/external/:external_id` and `/api/subscribers/external?external_id=…` answer a well-formed selector that matches no subscriber with `404 {"message":"Subscriber not found."}`. A malformed, missing or duplicate selector stays `400`, and a subscriber outside the caller's list scope keeps its `403`. Both refusals write nothing. The numeric route keeps listmonk's `400 Subscriber (id: ) not found`, since its IDs are listmonk's ([D57](#d57-compatible-client-results-and-safe-input-hardening)).
- **Why native:** these routes have no listmonk counterpart ([D38](#d38-subscriber-scope-writable-input-and-external-identity), [D39](#d39-query-selector-for-every-opaque-external-id)), so the generic missing-target 400 serves no compatible client. A stale selector is absent too: if its subscriber loses that external ID, or the ID is rebound to another subscriber, while the request is in flight, the answer is 404 and nothing is written.
- **The stray word.** The old absent-path message came from the `globals.messages.notFound` template with the `{globals.terms.subscriber}` term substituted into it. On current main the absent path answered `Subscriber not found`, not the report's "Subsubscriber", so the stray word was not reproduced; the placeholder is gone from the external path now. The text is a literal, `Subscriber not found.`, because the template has no full stop and the brief fixes the exact body clients match.
- **Considered:** keeping the 400 and documenting its text: clients would keep matching a message. A new route family: unnecessary, since the status is the contract.
- **Revisit:** if a template-driven message for native errors is wanted, a decision for all native 404s, not for this route.

- **Test enrollment from an existing event.** `POST /api/enrollments` accepts an optional `trigger_event_id` for manual and test enrollments. The event must belong to the subscriber and be named as the sequence's `trigger.event`. Otherwise the request is refused with `400 Invalid fields: trigger_event_id` and nothing is written; an unknown ID gets the same refusal. The enrollment records it as its trigger, so `since: "trigger"` conditions and aggregates ([D100](#d100-sequence-steps-read-event-aggregates)) measure from its `occurred_at`, and the template's `trigger` data is that event.
- **Timing unchanged.** A manual or test enrollment's first wait still starts from its creation (now), and a test keeps its one-minute cap. That holds on [reschedule](05-sequences.md#reschedule) too: the wait counts from creation even when `trigger_event_id` names an event 10 days old, so a reschedule does not make the step due at once. An event-started enrollment's wait still counts from the event, as before. The origin is stored in `enrollments.wait_from_event` (migration 21, default 1 for every existing row; 0 for an API-started enrollment). `reentry`, `force` and the test rules are unchanged.
- **Considered:**
  - A "run this step now" endpoint: rejected, since one way to move a job forward is enough. Rescheduling and the test cap cover the need.
  - Counting waits from the event's time: rejected, because a wait counted from a past event would be due at once, which is not what a test of a delay needs.
  - A 404 for a wrong event name: rejected, since the event exists and is simply not this trigger; `400` matches the other invalid-field refusals of this body.
- **Revisit:** if a test must replay a step's wait from the event time, a new decision with its own timing.

## D102. The `number` Liquid filter (lead, 2026-11-16)

Brief B88, from the first production adopter's integration report: a summary email built from `aggregates` (D100) reads "you pulled 1234567 items", and the allowed filters could not format a number. Apps used to pre-format numbers into event data; they cannot for numbers iglo.email computes.

- **Decision:** one filter, `number`, beside the other reviewed filters in `src/templates/renderer.ts`. `{{ 1234567 | number }}` renders `1,234,567`; `{{ 1234.5678 | number }}` renders `1,234.568`; `-1234` renders `-1,234`; `0` renders `0`. It is the `Intl.NumberFormat("en-US")` default (at most three fraction digits, rounded half away from zero), with negative zero and values that round to zero shown as `0`.
- **Input:** a finite JavaScript number is formatted. Every other value is returned unchanged, so it renders as it would without the filter: a string (a numeric string too) keeps its text, a boolean renders `true` or `false`, nil renders empty, and an object, an array or a non-finite number is refused by the output step exactly as it is without the filter.
- **No arguments.** Any argument is a render error like the other filters' bad arguments. There is no locale argument: the app ships English only (D89 defers translations). When translations exist, the filter may follow the app language; that is a later decision, not this one.
- **Output bound:** the output is charged to the message budget like the other filters. The bound is the number's own digit count: a typical total (up to 15 digits) is under 25 characters, and the largest finite number, 309 integer digits, renders at most 412 characters (digits, 102 separators, a sign).
- **Applies everywhere the shared renderer runs:** campaign, transactional, sequence step, opt-in and other templates.
- **Order with `default`.** `number` passes nil through, so the fallback goes after it: `{{ n | number | default: 0 }}` renders `0` for a missing `n` and `1,234,567` for `1234567`. `default` is unchanged and returns text, so `{{ n | default: 0 | number }}` leaves a present number ungrouped (`1234567`); write `number` first.
- **Considered:**
  - A locale argument now: no caller needs one, and it would be a second way to format a number in a product that ships English only (D89).
  - Formatting numeric strings: a string is data the app chose to send as text; formatting it would silently change what it meant.
  - Refusing non-numbers with an error: a template whose value is sometimes text would then fail on the text. Pass-through renders it as the template did before the filter was added.
  - Capping the output at the aggregate range: the bound would refuse a finite number the app supplied; the size bound is stated instead.
  - Making `default` keep a number so that `default: 0 | number` formats: `assign`ed defaults would then compare and print differently (`== "0"`, `contains`, `.size`), which saved v1.1.0 templates rely on. A minor release does not change that; the order above works without it.
- **Revisit:** if a locale or currency is needed, a new decision with its own argument and tests.

## D103. Manual enrollment needs a readable subscriber; `fn: null` is refused (lead, 2026-11-17)

Brief B91, from the B84–B86 review: `POST /api/enrollments` checked that the subscriber existed and nothing else about the caller's view of it, so a role with `sequences:manage` and list access to list A alone could enroll a subscriber who was only on list B. With `test: true` or a `trigger_event_id` the enrollment then sent that subscriber mail, or rendered their event data into a test step.

- **Decision:** `POST /api/enrollments` answers `404 Subscriber not found` for a subscriber the caller cannot read under the rule of `GET /api/subscribers/:id`: `subscribers:get` or `subscribers:get_all`, and a list grant (read or manage) on one of the subscriber's lists, or `lists:get_all`. The check runs inside the write transaction, after the caller's permission is revalidated and before any other rule (blocklist, trigger event, reentry, test). The refusal is the answer for an absent subscriber, with the same body, and writes nothing, so it does not reveal that the subscriber exists.
- **Unchanged:** `DELETE /api/enrollments/:id` and `POST /api/sequences/:id/steps/:step/reschedule` are sequence operations on enrollments that already exist, gated by `sequences:manage`; they name no subscriber. Their responses keep `subscriber_id`, as before.
- **`fn: null` is refused.** An aggregate's `fn` defaults to `count` only when the key is absent (05-sequences.md#aggregates). `"fn": null`, and any value that is not `count`, `sum` or `distinct`, is `400 Invalid fields: steps.<i>.aggregates.<name>.fn` with nothing saved. The other step fields keep their defaults on `null`, as before.
- **Why:** an enrollment is a write that starts mail and reads event data about the subscriber it names, so the subscriber must be one the caller could already open. A 403 would have told a limited caller that the subscriber exists; a 404 with the absent body does not, and matches how GET hides such subscribers. `fn: null` silently saved as `count`, so the saved definition was not the one the author sent.
- **Considered:**
  - A 403 for an unreadable subscriber: reveals existence, as above. Refused.
  - Checking the list scope on the subscriber's memberships only, without `subscribers:get`: GET does not do that either; a caller without a read permission gets nothing from GET, so it gets nothing from enrollment.
  - Applying the same rule to `DELETE` and reschedule: they act on a sequence's enrollment, which the caller can already see through the sequence, and changing them is a separate decision.
  - Treating `fn: null` as `count` with a warning: a silent rewrite of what the author sent.
- **Behavior change:** a `sequences:manage` caller without `subscribers:get` or `subscribers:get_all` can no longer enroll any subscriber by hand (404). A `subscribers:get_all` caller without list read scope is refused a list-B subscriber the same way, as GET refuses it with 403.
- **Revisit:** if the list enumeration on `GET /api/enrollments` or the `subscriber_id` on DELETE responses must also follow the subscriber rule, a separate decision: the list shows enrollments of any subscriber to `sequences:get`.

## D104. A queued send that shutdown cancels stays queued (lead, 2026-11-17)

Brief B92. `emailer.close()` cancels the operations still waiting for a pool connection. Before this decision, a cancelled operation that had not started reported `stage: "local"`, so the outbox journaled its row `failed` (`sends:production`, the remainder behind a held send with one connection). It now reports `stage: "not-started"`, and the outbox returns the row to `queued` without counting an attempt, as it already does for a restore hold. A send that had started still settles `unknown`. Nothing is re-sent: a not-started send made no SMTP attempt, so it goes out once after the next start.

- **Unchanged:** the 60 s queue expiry (`local`, journaled `failed`), except when it fires during the 5 s shutdown drain, where the row returns to `queued` like any other not-started send (safe: never attempted), and every other not-started path.
- **Evidence:** [B92 note](../perf/B92-ci-steps.md); the remainder was `failed` before and `queued` after, and `sends:production` passes.
- **Considered:** failing every cancelled queued send at shutdown, which is the old behaviour; it would mark an unattempted marketing send as failed, and a send the process never tried must stay queued.

## D105. A sequence step previews and test-sends with sample data (lead, 2026-11-17)

Brief B97, from the first production adopter's second report. A step's template reads `trigger.data.*`, `aggregates.*` and `subscriber.attribs.*`, and the only ways to see a step in an inbox were a test enrollment, which needs an existing subscriber and a stored event, or a throwaway transactional template with pre-rendered HTML. The template preview rendered with a dummy context, so numbers were blank and `trigger.data` empty.

- **Decision:** `POST /api/sequences/{id}/steps/{step}/preview` (`sequences:get`) renders one step's subject and body with the context a real send would use, and returns it with that context. `POST /api/sequences/{id}/steps/{step}/test` (`sequences:manage` and `tx:send`) sends the same render to one to five addresses, labelled `[Test] `, through the emailer's test path. Neither writes an enrollment, a send row, a statistic or a tracked link. `POST /api/templates/preview` takes an optional `sample` (JSON, the same shape), and `preview_template` takes it over MCP. The step's render is built by one builder (`src/sequences/step-data.ts`) used by the scheduler and both routes, so a preview renders what a send renders.
- **Context:** a `subscriber_id` names a readable subscriber (D103: otherwise `404`, as for an absent one), else the dummy subscriber. A `trigger_event_id` must be that subscriber's event under the sequence's trigger name (`400 Invalid fields: trigger_event_id`, as D101). Otherwise the subscriber's latest event under that name is the trigger, and with no event `trigger` is null, as for a manual enrollment. Aggregates are read for a real subscriber exactly as the scheduler reads them, measured from the trigger's `occurred_at`, or from now without one. A `sample` replaces the named fields of the chosen subscriber, trigger and aggregates, and only those. A sample aggregate the step does not define is refused (`400 Invalid fields: sample.aggregates.<name>`), and so is a non-number. A sample is bounded as template data is (256 KiB, `parseTemplateData`).
- **Test sends** render once per address. The address is the context's `subscriber.email` unless the sample names one. A blocklisted subscriber's address is skipped, and all skipped is `400`. Addresses are validated as subscriber emails are, one to five and distinct. A restore hold refuses them (as campaign tests). They are queued to the emailer with `copies: false` and not awaited, with at most four in flight (`429` past that), and are never retried.
- **Template preview:** without `sample` the response is byte for byte what it was; the dummy context gains `aggregates: {}`, so `{{ aggregates.x | number | default: 0 }}` previews as `0`.
- **Unchanged:** the scheduler's data, its rendering and its sends (the extraction is behaviour-preserving, and its tests pass unchanged); marketing sends stay at-most-once; no step is journaled or counted by a preview or test; the send log is unchanged.
- **Considered:** previewing a step through `POST /api/templates/preview` alone (a template does not know its step's aggregates, subject or trigger); journaling test sends (they would count in statistics and could be confused with real sends); test sends only to existing subscribers, as campaign tests are (the adopter needs an owner's inbox, and `tx:send` already allows any address through `POST /api/tx`); defaulting to a random subscriber's latest event (it would leak another subscriber's data into a preview). A preview's `since: "enrollment"` and `"last_step"` bounds measure from now and from the trigger, because there is no enrollment to measure from.
- **Evidence:** [B97 measurement](../perf/B97-step-preview.md).

## D106. Bootstrap API user from the environment (lead, 2026-11-17)

Brief B98, from the first production adopter's second report. A fresh install had no API credential, so a provisioning script first logged in through the form: it fetched the login page, scraped a nonce, read a CSRF cookie and sent it on every call, only to create an API user and its role.

- **Decision:** `IGLO_EMAIL_API_USER` and `IGLO_EMAIL_API_TOKEN` create an API user with the built-in Super Admin role. They are read from the environment only (a token in argv shows in process lists), and only on a start where no users exist, the condition of the first administrator. Both or neither, and the two pairs are independent. The admin and API rows are written in one transaction. Nothing is printed. The token must have the format of a generated token, 48 letters and digits; the user name follows the admin username rule and must differ from the admin's. Errors name the variable and never the value. The token is stored as its SHA-256 digest, as a generated token is.
- **Unchanged:** API authentication (`Authorization: token <user>:<token>`, compared by digest); generated tokens and `POST /api/users`; the first administrator's rules and messages. After the first start the variables are not read, so a malformed value there is not reported. To change the token, create another API user and delete this one.
- **Considered:** a CLI subcommand that prints a generated token (it needs the server stopped and a shell, which the image lacks); a token derived from the admin password (it couples two secrets); letting Basic auth with the admin password call the API (it changes listmonk's auth contract).

## D107. Deploy diagnostics: root URL, untrusted forwarding and a deploy checklist (lead, 2026-11-17)

Brief B98, from the same report. A bare host in `IGLO_EMAIL_ROOT_URL` crash-looped the container with a message that did not say what to change. Behind a PaaS router every client looked like the router until `security.trusted_proxies` was set, and nothing said so. A scheduled backup has to run the executable without a shell, and the docs did not say how.

- **Decision:**
  1. A root URL error names the value, quoted, and the fix. The suggestion `such as https://<value>` is an allow-list, not a deny-list: it is printed only when the whole value is a DNS host name with at least one dot (labels of letters, digits and hyphens, 1 to 63 characters, no label starting or ending with a hyphen, no empty label, no trailing dot), optionally followed by `:<port>` (1 to 65535, no leading zero) and by a path that starts with `/` and holds no whitespace, backslash, `?`, `#` or `@`. The candidate is then parsed and kept only if its hostname is the value's host in lower case, its port is the given port (empty when none was given; 443 reads back as empty, because https drops its default port), and the validator accepts it. Every other value gets no suggestion and names its wrong part: a missing colon after the scheme (`https//host`, `https/host`, `HTTP//host`), a bare scheme word or a single label (`https`, `localhost`), a port outside 1 to 65535 (`host:0`, `https://host:0`), whitespace or a backslash, a scheme not followed by `//`, a wrong scheme, credentials, a query or fragment, or a missing host. A value that has a scheme never gets a suggestion, because `https://` in front of it gives `https://https://host`, which passes as host `https`. A value with `@`, `?` or `#` is not echoed, because it may hold a password or a token. A bare host stays refused: guessing `http` or `https` would silently produce wrong links and cookie flags.
  2. A request with `X-Forwarded-For` from a peer outside `security.trusted_proxies` writes one attention line to the error logger: the peer address, the setting and the operations section. It writes once per process, and again after the list changes. The header is still ignored, so address resolution is unchanged (D60), and the state is one flag. The `GET /api/logs` line is the fixed text "X-Forwarded-For from an untrusted peer ignored: see security.trusted_proxies", without the address: that route needs only `settings:get`, which is not an administrator-only permission, and the log buffer takes no text from an error. The address is on stderr.
  3. `docs/operations.md` gains a generic checklist, "Deploying behind a reverse proxy or PaaS", and "API user for an app integration", which gives the permission set of each job from the operation table. The environment and Docker sections list the API variables.
- **Unchanged:** D60's address policy; the root URL rules (an absolute http or https URL without credentials, query or fragment) for every value that was accepted before, except port 0 (below); startup and exit behaviour.
- **Tightened (deliberate):** port 0 is refused, in a full URL (`https://host:0`) and in a bare value (`host:0`), with "the port must be from 1 to 65535". Port 0 is not a port a client can connect to, and the stored root URL would carry it into every link. No URL with a usable port is affected.
- **Tightened (deliberate):** `https:/host` and `http:host` were accepted before, because the URL parser rewrote them to `https://host` and `http://host`. They are now refused with "the scheme must be followed by //". The reason is that the validator and the stored value disagree. The server stores `IGLO_EMAIL_ROOT_URL` as given (`app.root_url`), and links and cookie settings use that string, not the parsed URL. So an accepted `https:/host` put a broken root into every email link while the start looked fine. Refusing it at start is the safe behaviour, and no URL that was written correctly is affected.
- **Considered:** guessing the scheme from the host (wrong links and cookies); accepting a scheme-less root URL as an alias (a second way to do one thing, D89); logging every untrusted request (one line per request on a busy proxy); suggesting `https://` for a value that already has a scheme (it gives `https://https://host`, which is accepted); a deny-list of scheme-like prefixes to skip (the first version let `https//host` through as host `https`, so the rule is an allow-list of host names that the suggestion is parsed to confirm); the address in the log buffer's line (the buffer's list is closed and `settings:get` is not admin-only, so the line is one fixed text and stderr keeps the address).

## D108. MCP is the agent path for content; the server says so and the README links to it (lead, 2026-11-17)

Brief B99, from the same report. An integrating agent worked only through REST and believed the MCP endpoint had about ten tools. The server exposed every operation, but nothing it sent said so: the first sentence of the MCP section described the D91 scope, the README said only "an MCP endpoint at `/api/mcp`", and no text told an agent which surface to use for which job.

- **Decision:** the surfaces split by who the caller is. REST is for the application: code that runs in the app or its deploy (events, transactional sends, subscriber sync, a provisioning script in the app's repository) uses the HTTP API with an API user, versioned and documented at `/api/openapi.json`. The MCP endpoint is for an agent working on an instance for a person: authoring and testing content (templates, sequence steps, campaigns), uploading media, inspecting subscribers, events, sends and bounces, and changing settings. Both apply the same permissions (D98). The MCP endpoint leaves out the operations D98 excludes, and caps uploads and answers at 1 MiB (REST media takes 25 MiB); it has no capability REST lacks. An agent uses the MCP endpoint for those jobs when it is connected and falls back to REST otherwise.
- **Server instructions.** The `initialize` result carries `instructions` (a field of the MCP spec, present in every protocol version the server negotiates, `src/mcp/routes.ts`), about 1,500 characters: what the server is and for whom, read `template_reference` before writing Liquid, the authoring loop (`get_sequence`, then `update_sequence_step` or `update_template`, `preview_sequence_step` with a real `subscriber_id` or a `sample`, `test_sequence_step`), that a test send's `true` means it was queued and the person checks their inbox, that a test writes no send row, so `get_sends` and `get_bounces` are for real sends and a send is never repeated to check it, real or test, that a test which fails to deliver appears in `get_logs` (B101), that a failed call returns the API's own message as a tool error, that reading users and roles is a tool (`get_users`, `get_user_roles`) while changes to them, two-factor sign-in, bulk deletes, deletes addressed by a query, query-selected bulk changes and whole-settings replacement are not tools, and that uploads and answers are capped at 1 MiB. A test checks that every tool the text names exists in `tools/list` for an admin credential.
- **Tool descriptions.** Every tool that takes Liquid says in one sentence to read `template_reference` before writing it. The test finds those tools from their input schemas (a `subject`, `altbody` or `from_email` field, or a string `body`, at any depth), so a new Liquid input without the pointer fails. The hand-written ones are changed in `src/mcp/tools.ts`; the generated ones (`create_sequence`, `update_sequence`, `transact_with_subscriber`, `convert_campaign_content`) take the sentence from their operation's description in `src/openapi/operations.ts`, so it also appears in the document.
- **README and operations.** The README gets an "If you are an AI agent" section near the top with the split in four lines, a `.mcp.json` for Claude Code's HTTP server shape (`type`, `url`, `headers`), the `claude mcp add` line, and links to the operations guide for the role to create and to `/api/openapi.json`. It does not repeat the bootstrap variables or the permission sets; those stay in `docs/operations.md`, which the MCP section now opens with the split and the authoring loop.
- **Unchanged:** the tool set and names (D98), permissions, annotations, the 1 MiB answer cap, and the REST API. No file is added to the repository for a client; the client configuration is text in the README, so no token can be committed.
- **Considered:**
  - A separate CLI or SDK for agents: a second way to do the same job (D89).
  - A Claude Code skill or plugin bundled in the repository: client-specific, and the server instructions reach every MCP client.
  - Dropping REST guidance for agents: applications must not depend on an agent's MCP session, so the split keeps REST for them.
  - Writing the Liquid reference into each tool description: the reference is one tool, and a pointer keeps the descriptions short.

## D109. Test sends run on their own SMTP pool, and an undelivered test is logged (lead, 2026-11-17)

Brief B101, from the B97 backlog and a B99 fixer finding. A campaign test and a sequence-step test went to the same keyed pool as real outbox, campaign and sequence sends. A test to an address whose receiving path stalls (a greylisting tarpit, a slow relay) reached the 60 s cut-off on a started operation; the cut calls `cancelPool`, which destroys every socket of the pool and settles every operation on it. Real sends that were mid-transaction on that server settled `unknown` (counted, never retried), so a test could lose real mail. Before this decision the defect was reproduced (`test/messaging/test-pool.test.ts`): a stalled test and a real send in DATA on one server with `max_conns` 2, on the injected clock; the real send settled `unknown` at the test's cut, and settles `accepted` now.

- **Decision:** `emailer.push` takes `testPool: true` (campaign and step test sends pass it). Such a send uses its own pool per SMTP server, keyed like a real pool but kept in a separate table, so no socket is shared between a test and a real send. Its cut-off, its connection limit and its shutdown behaviour are the real pool's: the normal 60 s cut-off, the server's `max_conns`, and the 5 s drain then cancellation at shutdown (D104). Configuration changes retire test pools as they retire real ones.
- **Unchanged:** the send-rate limiter accounting for test sends, the restore-hold refusal (D67), the 429 in-flight caps, copies off for tests, the SMTP settings test's temporary pool, marketing at-most-once (no send is retried), and every real-send path.
- **Logging:** a test send whose outcome is not `accepted` (refused, rejected, timed out, `unknown`, or handed back at shutdown) writes one attention line. Its stderr text names that it is a test send, the campaign or sequence and step, the recipient and the stage and status, and no message body. The `GET /api/logs` line is the fixed text "Test send not delivered: the server error output names the recipient and stage", so the buffer holds no address (as D107). A delivered test writes nothing.
- **Considered:** a single pool with a per-operation timeout that cancels only its own operation (the library and the socket model do not allow cancelling one socket from the pool's queue without the other operations' state); a test-only limit of one connection (it would serialise test sends for no gain, since each test already has an in-flight cap); logging a test's failure as a `GET /api/logs` line with the address (the buffer is read by `settings:get`, which is not admin-only).
- **Evidence:** [B101 note](../perf/B101-test-pool.md).

## D110. Liquid rendering is bounded by the deterministic budgets, not the wall clock (lead, 2026-11-19)

Brief B103, section 3. The release run's campaign finished one push short: one subscriber's message took more than one second of real time to render under CPU contention, and the renderer's liquidjs `renderLimit: 1000` (a deadline on `performance.now()`) threw `template render limit exceeded`. That subscriber was recorded `failed` and never retried. The deadline applied to every path the shared renderer serves: campaign, transactional, sequence step, opt-in and preview.

- **Decision:** the renderer sets no liquidjs `renderLimit` (the library default is unbounded) and makes no wall-clock check. Liquid render time is bounded only by the deterministic D54 budgets: work 10,000 steps, 4 MiB cumulative emission per message, 1 MiB per output, nesting 32, ranges 1,024 iterations, parse 64 KiB. This amends D54 and the `renderLimit` line of [06-templates](06-templates.md): D54's limits and errors are unchanged, and for Liquid they are now the only time bound.
- **Scope:** this decision covers Liquid only. The Markdown stage (`src/templates/markdown.ts`, marked's lexer) runs after the Liquid render and was not bounded by these budgets; D111 (B104) bounds only its inline link-title scan (B105/D112 track the rest). marked's link scanning is super-linear: a 4 KiB Markdown body takes seconds, and 8 KiB about 40 s, on the server thread (measured on origin/main during the B103 review; the removed deadline never covered Markdown either, so this is not a regression). It is a separate defect, recorded as D111 in [B104](../briefs/B104.md). D111 bounds only marked's inline link-title scan; the other Markdown scans and the D54 reservation are bounded by [D112](#d112-markdown-cost-is-charged-in-characters-marked-scans-the-output-reserve-is-linear-and-campaign-bodies-may-be-256-kib-lead-2026-11-19) (B105), which is required before the v1.3.0 tag.
- **Unchanged:** every D54 budget and its error, the memory bound (`memoryLimit`), the parse limit, the renderer's public API, and every send path.
- **Considered:** a higher deadline (host load would still turn into lost mail, later); a deadline on the injected clock (the render is synchronous and the clock does not advance during it, so it could preempt nothing); a retry of a render that exceeded the deadline (a retry adds nothing the budgets do not already bound, and a marketing render must not be repeated).
- **Evidence:** the B103 run 1 log (`template render limit exceeded` for one subscriber, the campaign at 259 of 300 sent); [perf note](../perf/B103-render-deadline.md) (median of 20, before and after, for the cases that reach each budget: the largest is 4.02 ms; the largest Liquid render measured, 94 ms for `upcase` of 512 KiB of `ß` applied twice, is still far below one second); `test/templates/render-deadline.test.ts`, which fails with the deadline and passes without it.

## D111. Link titles must follow whitespace, so the inline link-title scan is linear per link attempt (lead, 2026-11-19)

Brief [B104](../briefs/B104.md). A user with only `campaigns:get` could send 8 KiB of `[](` to a Markdown preview and hold the server for about 38 s (origin/main: 37.5 s in the HTTP test), and a GET sent during that render waited the same time. The D54 work budget did not bound the scan, because it charges per token, not per character scanned inside a token. Performance is in the [perf note](../perf/B104-markdown.md).

- **Scope:** this decision bounds marked's inline link-title scan only. It does not change the block-level scan, the reflink scan or the D54 reservation, which still refuses some ordinary bodies of about 4 KiB. Those costs predate this decision and are tracked in B105/D112. Markdown is not bounded overall by this decision.
- **Cause:** marked 15.0.12's inline link rule accepted a title with no whitespace before it (`[ \t]*`). The destination is greedy, so an attempt backtracked over each position and, at every `"`, `'` or `(`, ran a title scan to the next closing quote or parenthesis: O(n²) per attempt, with one attempt per `[` token.
- **Decision:** a title must follow whitespace: `(?:[ \t]*\n[ \t]*|[ \t]+)`. Only the empty separator is removed, so a title on the line after the destination still applies. A title can then start only at the end of the destination, so an attempt is linear, and the existing per-token work charge bounds the scan. The patch is `patches/marked@15.0.12.patch`, regex only; no marked version change and no new dependency.
- **What changed** (checked on origin/main and on this branch with the same inputs):
  - A glued title that contains whitespace is text now. `[x](u"a b")` and `[x](u(a b))` were links with a title (`href="u"`, `title="a b"`) on origin/main; they render as text here, as goldmark v1.8.6 does (after a destination that stops at the space, goldmark requires a title to start immediately, and `b` does not).
  - An angle-bracket destination glued to its title is text now. `[x](<u>"t")` was a link with title `t` on origin/main and in goldmark; it is text here. This is the one new difference from listmonk. It is listed in [07-api-and-ui.md](07-api-and-ui.md#api-compatibility-policy) as `Markdown link titles (D111, B104)` and in `src/openapi/differences.ts`, for the preview (GET and POST), content and test operations.
- **What did not change:** a glued title without whitespace in a plain destination (`[x](u"t")`) is part of the URL, `href="u%22t%22"`, on origin/main and here. A title after a space or a newline still applies. Ordinary Markdown output is pinned by `test/templates/markdown-compat.test.ts` (33 documents). Every D54 budget and error, the renderer's public API, the work counter and its charges, the reservation, and every send path are unchanged.
- **Considered:** (a) upgrading marked to 17.0.6 or 18.2.1, which fix the link case but ship a minified bundle, so the budget patch (about 245 lines) would need rewriting and other output could change. (b) A wall-clock deadline, refused by D110. (c) Charging scanned characters inside the regex: a regex call runs to completion and cannot be interrupted. (d) An input size cap: 4 KiB of `[](` already takes 5 s. (e) Glued titles with a bounded length: each backtrack position still scans to the cap, and longer titles would change.
- **Evidence:** the [perf note](../perf/B104-markdown.md) (families before and after; enumeration of repeating units; ordinary render medians, unchanged). `test/campaigns/markdown-time.test.ts`: the concurrent GET, sent over a socket while the preview renders in a child process, answers in 40.7 s on origin/main (assertion `< 1000 ms` fails) and within the bound here; the changed-title test fails on origin/main and passes here; the glued-without-whitespace and title-after-space tests pass on both.

## D112. Markdown cost is charged in characters marked scans, and the output reserve is a linear upper bound (lead, 2026-11-19)

> Superseded in part by D113 (B106): the per-regex work charges and the token-based reserve are replaced by a dry-run bound through Bun's Markdown API; the 128 KiB source cap, the 1 MiB output limit and the no-deadline rule stand.

Brief [B105](../briefs/B105.md). Two defects on v1.2.1 and origin/main. The D54 reservation refused ordinary Markdown campaigns from about 2 KiB (an 8-story newsletter saved, then its preview answered 400 and a send failed for every subscriber), because it summed prefixes of every array and charged each text twice. And marked's block-level and reference scans were uncharged or charged per KiB, so a 16 KiB block shape held the server for 4 s and five concurrent previews for 20 s. Performance is in the [perf note](../perf/B105-markdown-cost.md).

- **Decision:**
  1. **Reserve.** `reserve()` charges one upper bound of the output per message, computed once per token with no prefix sums: escaped text and titles count each special character at its entity length, `href` counts its `encodeURI` length, raw HTML counts once, and each tag has a per-type allowance (a list item, a table cell, a paragraph and so on). It is an upper bound on every output the renderer writes, including a block text token in a loose list item (written as a paragraph), a task item's checkbox (written after the reserve, so reserved per task item) and image alternative text (bounded by six times the raw bytes of its children, since the text renderer can print escapes twice). `markdown-reserve.test.ts` checks actual ≤ reserved over the reviewer's shapes, the constructs above and a seeded random corpus. A bound above 1 MiB is refused before parsing, and the post-render 1 MiB check is unchanged.
  2. **Work.** Markdown has its own work counter, separate from Liquid's 10,000 steps, shared by preview, test send, send and content conversion, with a limit of 36,000,000 units (`WORK_LIMIT` in [markdown.ts](../../src/templates/markdown.ts)). A unit is about 5 ns. The limit is reached by large shapes, not by ordinary mail: the story-shaped newsletter (headings, an image, links, a list and a table, per 1.2 KiB) counts 12.3 M scan and step units at 64 KiB and 24.5 M at 128 KiB, so by linear extrapolation it reaches the limit near 190 KiB (the reviewer estimated 140 to 160 KiB); a dense table with an emphasis run in each cell counts 23.8 M at 64 KiB and 35.3 M at 96 KiB, and head refuses it from 104 KiB. Origin refuses that table from 64 KiB on the 1 MiB output limit, so it accepted no such input. The container refusal times are in the perf note. marked charges the characters each scan consumes after it runs, each token (800 for a block token or an inline token that starts with a special character, 200 for plain text) and each loop exec (600 for an emphasis closer, 200 for a punctuation or skip mask, and a sixteenth of the masked source for each mask copy). Two scans that can be quadratic are checked before they run and charged nothing: the emphasis closer search (30 per character of the remaining paragraph) and a blockquote attempt at a line that starts with `>` (16 per character of its window, up to the next blank line, since marked's quote pattern can fail after scanning the whole window); a failed check refuses the input. Each other scan that can run past its token is charged for what it examines: the lazy heading scan to its stop line (64 per character), the paragraph window and its stop line (20 per character, plus 400 per line), the examined part of a closer search (30 per character, the rest of the paragraph after its last exec), the opener run of `*` or `_` (8 per character), the e-mail lookahead run (3 per character), the reference search (8 per `[` times the paragraph length, before the search, when a definition exists), a failed link or code span attempt (8 per character of the rest of the input), a failed strikethrough attempt (40 per character), the URL back-pedal (4 per character) and the last token's consumption. The overshoot is one regex exec. The full table pattern (it runs only when its delimiter passes) is charged 40 units per character it matches, not 1: that regex costs about 200 ns per character on the misaligned `|\n--\n` shape, which at 1 unit per character ran 1.8 s over HTTP at 128 KiB (perf note, round 2). Paragraph text keeps its charge of 40 units per character (20 for the block consumption and 20 for the window before the stop line): its regexes measure about 70 ns per character matched, so the stop-line search is covered by that window and is not weighted separately. Weighting is per shape, not a rescale: ordinary tables and newsletters price at about 1 ns per unit.
  3. **Patch.** `patches/marked@15.0.12.patch` is the only dependency change: no version change, no new package, no lockfile change. Its regex changes keep every match: the table pattern runs its body group only when the delimiter passes, a paragraph's stop line is the same pattern without the body, and the heading precheck runs the heading pattern only when an underline precedes the first stop line. Copies of strings are charged as the text they add, not the whole accumulated string (those were quadratic). The fence-like alternative `~{3,}[^\n]*\n`, which marked uses to test a paragraph's stop line and its table and paragraph lookaheads, is `~{3}[^\n]*\n`: it matches the same lines and cannot backtrack through a long tilde run (a run of 64 KiB ending a paragraph took 1.5 s; it now takes milliseconds).
  4. **Source cap.** Campaign bodies keep D54's 64 KiB source limit. The Markdown source after Liquid expansion is capped at 128 KiB (`MARKDOWN_SOURCE_LIMIT` in [markdown.ts](../../src/templates/markdown.ts), twice the body limit), and the cap is checked before marked sees the source, on preview, test send and send. A larger source is refused with a 400, "Markdown is larger than 128 KiB after template expansion", without parsing. The cap is a size bound, not a time limit, so D110 holds. A Liquid loop can expand a 64 KiB body to the 1 MiB output limit; every expansion within the cap is required to render or be refused within the target (`markdown-liquid.test.ts`). Campaign previews, `text`, test sends and MCP show the message, and a campaign send logs it per subscriber and sends nothing to them. Sequence and transactional templates never render Markdown, so the cap does not apply to them (the error branch at `src/templates/routes.ts:177` is unreachable; removed in B106). Known limits after the B105 round-3 review (lead decision on PR #157): marked's hard-break whitespace lookahead is super-linear on a paragraph followed by whitespace-only lines (0.65 s for a literal 64 KiB body, 2.44 s at the 128 KiB cap via Liquid), and three failed-attempt charges refuse some 7–20 KiB line lists (`!`, `[`, `~`, lone backtick) that main also refused. Both are pre-existing classes in marked's regex lexer and are replaced structurally by [D113](#d113-markdown-uses-a-linear-time-commonmark-parser-lead-2026-11-19) (B106). `test/templates/render.test.ts`'s exact UTF-8 output bound now runs at the cap boundary (a source of exactly 128 KiB after expansion renders, one byte more is refused with 400), because a 1 MiB Markdown source cannot pass the cap. This follows from the cap and is accepted (lead, 2026-11-19).
  5. No wall-clock deadline (D110). No retry. Marketing sends stay at-most-once, and no network call runs in a transaction (unchanged).
- **Unchanged:** every D54 budget and error; the 4 MiB emission budget and the 1 MiB output limit; Liquid's 10,000 steps; D111's link-title rule; the renderer's public API; every send path.
- **Output identity.** Wherever origin/main accepts a document, head returns the same bytes: the 33 pinned B104 documents; 4,500 seeded random documents built from 60 Markdown fragments, each accepted by origin's production path and identical on head; and 111 cases compared with origin's production path, reserve included (67 accepted by both, identical; none accepted by origin is refused now). Documents origin refuses by the reservation can now render (for example a 12,000-cell table). The reserve bound holds on 10,000 seeded random documents (actual ≤ reserved in every case, the largest ratio 1.0).
- **Considered:**
  - (a) Upgrading marked to 17.0.6 or 18.2.1. B104 measured 31–35 s on `--\n` at 64 KiB for both, and both ship a minified bundle, so the budget patch would be rewritten and other output could change. Not taken; a dependency change would need output parity on every pinned document and the lead's acceptance first, and the patch meets the target.
  - (b) A wall-clock deadline: refused by D110.
  - (c) A cap on the body alone: a 64 KiB body can expand to 1 MiB through Liquid, so it would not bound the output. Decision 4 caps the expanded source at 128 KiB instead, and the work limit and the reserve bound what that source can cost.
  - (d) One unit per character: charging the remaining input for each block token refused a 256 KiB newsletter in a measurement (about 128 million units), so the charge follows the scan instead.  - (e) An exact render for the reserve: it costs the render it is meant to bound.
- **Evidence:** the [perf note](../perf/B105-markdown-cost.md) (before and after, the sweeps, the fuzz, the newsletter sizes, goldmark differences, the Liquid expansions). Tests: `test/templates/markdown-budget.test.ts` (newsletters at 4, 16 and 64 KiB, the output bound through a Liquid loop, a work refusal, the 128 KiB source cap refused at once and a source of exactly 128 KiB accepted), `test/templates/markdown-compat.test.ts` (pins for 4, 16 and 64 KiB), `test/templates/markdown-runs.test.ts` (every marker run, at the end of a paragraph, list item, blockquote and table row, with and without a newline, within a second), `test/templates/markdown-reserve.test.ts` (actual ≤ reserved over the reviewer's shapes, the constructs the first reserve missed and a seeded random corpus), `test/campaigns/markdown-newsletter.test.ts` (preview, test send and campaign send at each size), `test/campaigns/markdown-liquid.test.ts` (1000 KiB Liquid expansions of each marker and shape through preview, the same shapes at 128 KiB of expanded source, and five concurrent 128 KiB previews measured from before a GET is sent), `test/campaigns/markdown-ordinary.test.ts` (the reviewer's five ordinary documents, rebuilt from the r2 review at their stated line counts, 16.7 to 20.1 KiB, and a 60 KiB document of 200 tables of 5 rows and 10 cells, each through preview and a campaign send to two subscribers; the tables must render as 200 `<table>` elements), `test/campaigns/markdown-time.test.ts` (reviewer shapes and families at 64 KiB, the reference shape with a definition, five concurrent previews measured from before they are sent).
- **Known limit (B105 round 2):** a 64 KiB document of unpadded one-character tables (`|h|h|…|`, about 440 tables) is refused by the work limit at 36.0 M units. Origin refuses that document, and the 5×10 test shape and the realistic table documents, by its own output limit. Head renders the 5×10 test shape at 21.6 M units and a realistic table document with prices, links and emphasis at 14.6 M. The unpadded shape is valid Markdown but is not what a campaign author writes; it is recorded here rather than priced lower.
- **Not fixed (pre-existing, unchanged by this decision):** `[x](()" ")` is a link to `(` with text `" "` on origin and head; goldmark renders it as text. A link-destination rule, not a cost; backlog. marked also writes a raw `&` in `href` where goldmark writes `&amp;`, and goldmark adds heading IDs, XHTML output and typography; these differences predate B105 and are not changed.

## D113. Markdown uses a linear-time CommonMark parser (lead, 2026-11-19)

Brief B106, after the B105 round-3 review (PR #157). Three review rounds of per-regex charging in the pinned marked patch each found new super-linear shapes in marked's regex lexer (hard-break lookahead, failed-attempt rescans); the approach does not converge.

- **Decision:** replace marked with a CommonMark parser whose running time is linear in its input by design, chosen and evidenced in B106 (candidates: micromark/mdast-free HTML path, or another maintained parser with a documented linear-time guarantee and a pathological-input test suite). listmonk renders Markdown with goldmark (CommonMark), so output moves toward listmonk's; every output difference from today's is listed in 07-api-and-ui.md as a deliberate difference or a parity fix.
- **Kept:** the 64 KiB body limit, the 128 KiB post-Liquid cap (D112), the 1 MiB output limit, a true `reserve()` bound, no wall-clock deadline (D110).
- **Evidence (B106 phase 1, 2026-11-20):** [perf note](../perf/B106-markdown-parser.md). Proposed: `Bun.markdown`, native to the Bun 1.4.5 runtime, so no dependency and no build step. It passes 652 of 652 CommonMark 0.31.2 examples (marked 599 with GFM off), runs at most 11.7 ms on every 128 KiB family and scales linearly. micromark 4.0.3 is quadratic (17 of 64 families time out at 128 KiB, with or without GFM) and is rejected. markdown-it 15.0.3 is the fallback: 153 ms at most, no guarantee, one MIT dependency. Its output differs from today's on about 1,700 of 2,118 corpus documents, most of them random mixes, and from goldmark on 583 of them. Constructs alone, `Bun.markdown` differs from goldmark only on D111's glued title and on a table with mismatched header cells, which GFM rejects.
- **Lead decision (2026-11-20): `Bun.markdown`, no new dependency.** `marked` and `patches/marked@15.0.12.patch` are removed. micromark is rejected (quadratic on families that time out at 128 KiB); markdown-it is the fallback, not used.
- **Bun's Markdown API is unstable, so the output is tied to the pinned Bun.** Checked at tag `bun-v1.4.5`: `docs/runtime/markdown.mdx` documents `Bun.markdown.html`, `render` and `react`, with an "**Unstable API** — This API is under active development and may change in future versions of Bun" callout, and describes the parser as written in Rust. The 1.4.5 type declarations (`bun.d.ts`, `namespace markdown`) match. Markdown output depends on the pinned Bun (`packageManager`, Docker image `oven/bun:1.4.5`). Any Bun upgrade must pass the Markdown pin suite: the B104 and B105 pins in `test/templates/markdown-compat.test.ts`, the newsletter pins, and the family and bound tests in `test/templates/markdown-families.test.ts`.
- **Options:** listmonk's GFM set (tables, strikethrough, task lists on); bare-URL autolinks off, matching goldmark's no-linkify (a parity change, D57, listed in [07](07-api-and-ui.md#api-compatibility-policy)); heading IDs off, as today; raw HTML passes through, as listmonk's `WithUnsafe`.
- **Output bound.** Bun's `render` callbacks cannot reproduce `html()` exactly: there is no callback for a hard break (`<br />`), so output built from callbacks would lose them. The bound is therefore a dry run through `Bun.markdown.render` whose callbacks return nothing and charge an upper bound of the bytes `html()` writes for each element (escaped text, tags and attributes, a task item's checkbox, an encoded href, raw block HTML). A hard break is bounded at 7 bytes, and raw inline HTML at the source's length, since no callback sees either. The bound is refused above 1 MiB before `html()` runs; the real output is checked once more after it. The bound is an upper bound by test, not by proof: `test/templates/markdown-families.test.ts` checks it on every family at 128 KiB, and `test/templates/markdown-reserve.test.ts` on a seeded random corpus. Example: `hr-run` at 128 KiB has a bound of 1,703,952 bytes against an `html()` output of 1,048,587 bytes, and is refused before the output is built.
- **Work and time.** The per-regex work charges are removed: the parser is linear by measurement (the 128 KiB families take at most tens of milliseconds, and scaling is linear on the four families measured at 16–128 KiB), not by a documented guarantee.
- **Parity changes and differences** are listed in [07](07-api-and-ui.md#api-compatibility-policy): bare-URL autolinks off, CommonMark corrections, XHTML markup, the task-list markup (a deliberate difference), the GFM table header rule, and tables wider than 128 columns (Bun's parser limit, a deliberate difference, measured: 128 is a table, 129 is not). The 128-column limit is the one difference that comes from Bun rather than from a choice; it is listed rather than worked around, since working around it needs a second parser.
- **Status:** accepted (lead, 2026-11-20). Implemented in B106 phase 2. Output evidence: [perf note](../perf/B106-markdown-parser.md); every changed pin equals goldmark's canonical output. D112's per-regex charges and token-based reserve are superseded by this decision; its size caps stand.

## D114. Test sends leave a trace (lead, 2026-11-19)

Brief B108, from the first production adopter's v1.3.0 verification on its dev instance (SES in us-east-1, bounces over SNS). Three gaps. A step test whose only address is blocklisted answered `400 Invalid fields: emails`, which says nothing of why. A test to a bouncing address left no trace: SES accepts every recipient at SMTP time, so the test returned `true`, `GET /api/logs` showed nothing (B101 covers SMTP-time failures only), and the SNS bounce that followed matched no send, since test sends are not journaled (D105). A real send's bounce is recorded within seconds; a test's was dropped without a word.

- **Decision:**
  1. **All-skipped step test.** When every address is blocklisted, a step test answers `400 No recipients: blocklisted: <the addresses the caller gave>`. Mixed lists still send to the rest and answer `{"data": true}`. A campaign test keeps listmonk's own answer (`campaigns.noKnownSubsToTest`), as listmonk's route, so only the step route changes.
  2. **Test-send records.** Each step and campaign test chooses its Message-ID before the send (`newMessageId`, or a Message-ID the campaign's own headers name), and every one is recorded in `test_sends` (migration 22), owned by `src/messaging/test-sends.ts`, in one write transaction before any message is handed to the emailer. A failed write answers `500` and sends nothing. A row holds the Message-ID (lowercase, without brackets), the kind (`campaign` or `sequence_step`), the campaign, sequence and step IDs and `created_at`; it holds no address. Rows last seven days: the retention loop (`createPurge`, on the injected clock) removes them, and a lookup ignores a row past the window. The rows per request are bounded by the test-send in-flight caps. A row is never a send: no `sends` row, statistic, webhook or analytics.
  3. **Bounce matching.** An SES notice (signature verified, topic allowed, receipt not yet taken) whose `mail.headers` Message-ID is a test's record, and for which no send resolves, is recorded nowhere and acts on nobody. After the commit it writes one attention line: `A test send bounced` or `A test send got a complaint` in the log buffer, so `GET /api/logs` shows that fixed text. Its stderr line names the kind, the campaign or sequence and step, the recipient (the address the notice itself names, `bouncedRecipients` or `complainedRecipients`, not a stored one), and the bounce type and sub-type or the feedback type. SNS delivers at least once, and the receipt makes a repeated notice one line. A notice with no matching Message-ID, or past the window, is handled as before.
  4. **Agent guidance.** The `get_logs` operation says it lists recent attention and error lines by their fixed text, including a test send that was not delivered and one that bounced or drew a complaint later. The MCP server instructions and the authoring loop say the same, and add that silence in `get_logs` may be receiver delay or filtering. The instructions stay within about 1,500 characters.
  5. **Operations.** The proxy checklist recommends the proxy's network as a CIDR, not its single address, and says why: Docker gives a recreated container a new address on the network. It shows how to find the subnet and names Coolify's default network. The backup section schedules `backup --verify` as its own task after the backup, with the exact command.
- **Behaviour change (deliberate):** before this decision, a notice of a test to a subscriber's address was recorded as a real bounce of that subscriber, because the match fell back to the address or to the `X-Listmonk-Subscriber` header that a campaign test carries. A hard bounce then blocklisted a subscriber because of a test. A test's notice is now never recorded, so no subscriber is blocklisted, unsubscribed or deleted by it. No other bounce changes.
- **Unchanged:** the SNS signature, topic, age and receipt rules; the bounce actions and counts for every real send and for every notice with no test record; the send journal, statistics and webhooks; test sends' SMTP pool (D109), caps and copies policy; the log buffer's closed list of fixed texts (two entries are added); marketing at-most-once.
- **No personal data:** `test_sends` holds no address. A row is a random Message-ID, the kind and numeric IDs, and `created_at`, so it is not a record about a person, and a subscriber's deletion or wipe has nothing in it to remove. A notice's address comes from the provider's own notice and is written to stderr only, never stored by B108. Rows are removed after seven days by the retention loop. The test that deletes a subscriber after a test send checks that no address reaches the API, the log buffer or the table's values.
- **Matching uses the header, not SES's ID.** `mail.messageId` is SES's own identifier, not the one iglo.email set. The header is the same one campaign attribution reads, so it needs SES's "Include original email headers", as operations.md already requires for attribution. A notice without original headers matches no test.
- **Considered:**
  - Journaling test sends as sends: they would count in statistics and webhooks, which D105 refused.
  - Matching by recipient and time: it is wrong for real mail to the same address.
  - Recording a test's bounce as a real bounce, with the action suppressed: it still writes a row that the subscriber's history and exports show, and a later change to the action would act on it.
  - Memory only: lost on restart, and bounces arrive after restarts.
  - A separate attention table: the log buffer already carries fixed lines to `GET /api/logs`, and the table would add a second store for the same thing (D89).
- **Evidence:** [B108 measurements](../perf/B108-test-send-records.md).

## D115. Bun 1.4.5, and JSON arrays from SQLite are converted explicitly (lead, 2026-11-20)

Brief B107, after the revert of the unreviewed Bun 1.4.5 bump in B107a (#158).

- **Decision:** the runtime is Bun 1.4.5 (`packageManager`, `@types/bun`, the release pin in `scripts/release.ts`, the container image in the brief recipes). Every SQL statement that puts a JSON array built by a subquery into a JSON object wraps that subquery in `json(...)`. Webhook payloads carry `list_ids` as a JSON array on every Bun version.
- **Why:** on `oven/bun:1.4.5` (SQLite 3.54.1) a scalar subquery's `json_group_array` lost its JSON subtype, so `json_object` stored the array as the string `"[1,2]"`. `oven/bun:1.4.4` (SQLite 3.53.4) keeps the array. A runtime's SQLite is not a contract, so the array's type must come from the SQL, not from the driver. The four webhook and bounce tests that failed on 1.4.5 are unchanged, and a test pins the array in every delivered body that carries `list_ids`.
- **Audit:** `webhook-blocklisted` and `webhook-deleted` (`src/subscribers/store.sql`) were the only splices from a scalar subquery. `webhook-unsubscribed` (a derived table), the delivery-clear and delivery-end updates and `delivery-insert-many` already used `json(...)`. Campaign list and media columns are read as text and parsed in JavaScript, so no version can change their type.
- **Unchanged:** the payload shape, the event set, the delivery window, at-most-once marketing sends, and the WebKit revision (Bun 1.4.5 pins the same `d6375c72…` as 1.4.4, so `notices()` changes only its Bun version).
- **Considered:**
  - Pinning Bun 1.4.4 and waiting for a fix: the runtime would keep an SQLite behaviour the next version may change, and the release gate would still rest on an unstated driver property.
  - Converting in JavaScript (`JSON.parse` of each list): it moves each row through the runtime and makes the payload SQL a mix of two encoders, so the cost is not justified.
  - Relying on Bun's `json()` subtype across subqueries in general: that is the behaviour that changed, so it is not a basis.
- **Evidence:** the root-cause table on each runtime, and the before and after runs of `test/webhooks/webhooks.test.ts` and `test/bounces/bounces.test.ts` on `oven/bun:1.4.5` ([B107 measurements](../perf/B107-bun-1.4.5.md)): the four named tests and the new `list_ids` array test fail before the fix, and all pass after it, while on `oven/bun:1.4.4` they pass both before and after.

## D116. Test send unsubscribe links open an explanatory page (lead, 2026-11-20)

Brief B112, from the first adopter's full Gmail pass of ten sequence step tests on v1.4.0. Every test renders correctly, but a person who clicks Unsubscribe in a test mail to see the flow reached a 404: a step test's `unsubscribe_url` and `manage_url` name the placeholder send UUID of D105 (`00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000`), which no send has.

- **Decision:** `GET /m/00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000/unsubscribe/:subUUID` (any `:subUUID`, with or without `?manage=true`) answers `200` with the public page layout and one message: "This link is from a test send. Unsubscribing from a test does nothing; in a real message this link opens the unsubscribe page." The title is "Test send". `POST` to the same path answers the same page, for the page form and for a one-click `List-Unsubscribe-Post` alike. Both are `noindex`, and the strings are English in `i18n/en.json`, which other languages fall back to.
- **Writes and reads nothing:** the placeholder path looks up no send and no subscriber, so the answer does not reveal whether `:subUUID` exists, and no unsubscribe, blocklist, webhook, event or tracking row is written.
- **Unchanged:** a real send UUID is never the placeholder (sends use random v4 UUIDs), so every real `/m/:sendUUID/…` path behaves as before, including the `px.png` and `link` routes for the placeholder. Campaign test sends (`/subscription/<campaign UUID>/<subscriber UUID>`, listmonk's path) carry the campaign's own UUID, not the placeholder, so they are left as they are; the campaign preview's placeholder link is listmonk's `/subscription/` path and is also left as it is.
- **Considered:**
  - Documenting the placeholder only: a dead link in a mail is the confusing part, and the page costs one route.
  - Making a test unsubscribe real: a test must not change a subscriber (D105), and a mail client's one-click POST on a test mail would otherwise unsubscribe the subscriber the test was rendered for.
  - Answering `404` with a note: a 404 reads as a broken link, which is the reported problem.
- **Evidence:** the tests in `test/unsubscribe/test-send-page.test.ts` and the step test-send case in `test/sequences/step-preview.test.ts`; no sending, scheduling or storage path changes, so there is no measurement.

## D117. Campaign test sends are inert: their links name a test placeholder and their message is untracked (lead, 2026-11-20)

Brief B115, from the owner's directive (no known gap open before go-live) and the B112 review. A campaign test to a subscriber on the campaign's list rendered the real campaign UUID and the real subscriber UUID into the unsubscribe, manage and `List-Unsubscribe` links. A click on Unsubscribe in that mail, or a mail client's one-click POST, unsubscribed the subscriber from the campaign's lists and queued a webhook. Its tracked links and view pixel recorded clicks and views against the real campaign and subscriber.

- **Upstream (listmonk v7.0.0-beta.1 at 6fcd95f, read from GitHub):** `TestCampaign` (`cmd/campaigns.go`) calls `sendTestMessage`, which compiles the campaign and pushes `NewCampaignMessage` through `PushCampaignMessage`, the path of a real send. No test branch exists. `TrackLink` registers each URL (`CreateLink`) and returns the link URL with the real campaign UUID and the subscriber UUID, the dummy UUID when individual tracking is off. `TrackView` emits the pixel with the same UUIDs. `UnsubscribeURL` and `ManageURL` are `<root>/subscription/<campaign UUID>/<subscriber UUID>` (`?manage=true`), `List-Unsubscribe` carries the unsubscribe URL, `OptinURL` is `<root>/subscription/optin/<subscriber UUID>`, and `MessageURL` is `<root>/campaign/<campaign UUID>/<subscriber UUID>`. So an upstream test mail unsubscribes its recipient, and its clicks and views count as a real send's do.
- **Decision:** a campaign test message is untracked, and its per-subscriber links name the test placeholder `testSendUUID` = `00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001` where a real send names its campaign: `unsubscribe_url`, `manage_url` and `List-Unsubscribe` (`/subscription/<test>/<subscriber>`), `message_url` (`/campaign/<test>/<subscriber>`) and `optin_url` (`/subscription/optin/<test>`). `track_link` returns the URL unchanged and `track_view` renders nothing, as a step test's do (D105). Every one of those routes answers the D116 test page for any subscriber UUID: `200`, `noindex`, the same body for `GET` and `POST` (the page form and one-click alike), no lookup, no write, no webhook. Real sends and real links are unchanged.
- **Why not the nil UUID that the brief names:** `/subscription/00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000/<subscriber>` is already a live path. The double opt-in mail (`src/public/mail.ts`) and transactional preference links (`src/tx/routes.ts`) name it, and its POST ends a subscriber's unconfirmed subscriptions (listmonk's opt-in behaviour, `src/campaigns/public.tsx`). Answering the test page on that path would stop opt-in mail from unsubscribing. So the test uses its own reserved UUID, and `dummyUUID` keeps its meaning.
- **Why the opt-in and message links too:** the opt-in POST confirms a real subscriber's double opt-in lists, and the message route renders a real subscriber's page, so a test could change state through either. Both answer the test page: a branch in `src/public/routes.tsx` (opt-in) and in `src/public/archive.tsx` (message), beside the campaign route in `src/campaigns/public.tsx`.
- **Unchanged:** who receives a test (known, non-blocklisted subscribers in the caller's scope, D109 pool), the `[Test]`-less subject, `X-Listmonk-Campaign` and `X-Listmonk-Subscriber`, the Message-ID record (D114), marketing at-most-once, and every real send path. The `campaign.uuid` value in Liquid data stays the real campaign's, since it is data and changes no state.
- **Considered:**
  - An unknown subscriber UUID in the links: the test page would answer `404`, the confusion D116 removed.
  - Dropping the links from test mail: a mail's unsubscribe link has to stay a link.
  - Tracking a test's links in their own table: a test must not create link rows (D105).
- **Deliberate difference from upstream:** upstream's test sends are tracked and unsubscribe the recipient. This is recorded in `07-api-and-ui.md` and `src/openapi/differences.ts`, and the contract suite marks it: `test/contract/campaigns.ts` checks the upstream behaviour under `reference` and the D117 behaviour in production. In production, `test/contract/analytics.ts` and `test/contract/subscriber-activity.ts` take their tracked links from a real send, since a test send no longer yields one.
- **Evidence:** `test/unsubscribe/campaign-test-send.test.ts` (test links, following them, and a real send as a control), `test/public/test-send-links.test.ts` (message and opt-in links, with a real opt-in link as the control), and the updated `test/campaigns/campaigns.test.ts`. The change adds no work to a send and removes link registration from test sends, so it has no measurement; the lead may ask for one.

## D119. Public pages share one branded shell, and an empty public form is a 200 (lead, 2026-11-20)

Brief B121, from the first adopter's v1.4.1 verification. The `/m/` unsubscribe pages, the test-send page, their error and result pages, and the public 404 rendered the iglo.email shell with `siteName` only: the default logo and favicon, and no `/public/custom.css`. The subscription routes were branded, so one site looked two ways. Separately, `GET /subscription/form` with no public list answered `500`.

- **Decision (branding):** every subscriber-facing HTML page takes one site getter, `PageSite` (`src/public/layout.tsx`: the site name and the shell settings), and renders through `PublicShell` with it. The `/m/` routes, the campaign subscription routes, the archive's message and hidden pages, the rate-limit page, the public 404 (registered for unmatched paths by `registerPublicRoutes`) and the bounce route's disabled 404 all take it. The unsubscribe and campaign routes take it as a required dependency, so the fallback that dropped the site is gone. No such page renders the unbranded shell while settings exist.
- **Decision (empty form):** `GET /subscription/form` with no public list answers `200` with the branded message, "No lists available to subscribe.", under the form's own title, "Subscribe" (a 200 page is not an error, so it does not say "Error"). Nothing else changes: the form still needs a list to show, and `POST /subscription/form` answers as before.
- **Deliberate difference from upstream:** upstream answers the empty form with `500` (`cmd/public.go`, `SubscriptionFormPage`, at 6fcd95f). An empty list set is a configuration state, not a fault, and a `5xx` triggers the operator's monitoring. Recorded in `07-api-and-ui.md` and checked by `form-empty-d119` in `test/contract/public.ts` (`500` under `reference`, `200` in production).
- **Test pages:** the opt-in and view-in-browser routes of a test send answer their own wording (`public.testSendOptinInfo`, `public.testSendMessageInfo`), since the unsubscribe wording (D116) does not fit them. Both keep the D117 behaviour.
- **Unchanged:** every status code except the empty form, the default output apart from the shell's links (a `/m/` page without custom settings now carries the `/public/custom.css` link, as the subscription pages always did), and every real send path.
- **Operator pages too (B121 review, lead decision, listmonk parity):** the sign-in, two-factor, forgot-password, reset-password and archive-preview pages take the same site getter, so `PublicShell` and `publicMessage` require it and no page can render the unbranded shell. The admin's own console pages keep the admin shell.
- **Unmatched paths (B121 review):** the router answers an unmatched path with the registered not-found whose prefix is longest, whatever the order of registration. So the public catch-all (`/`) never takes `/admin/` (307 to sign-in when signed out, the admin error page when signed in) or `/api/` (JSON 404). A bare router with no public pages mounted answers the public 404's status and type with no body.
- **Considered:**
  - Passing the branding into each route separately: that is the bug the brief reports; one getter stops a route from forgetting it.
  - A 404 for the empty form: every visitor reaches it, and a 404 reads as a broken link; a 200 with the message does not.
- **Evidence:** `test/ui/public.test.ts` through the compiled server (admin and API paths keep their own answers, the public 404 stays for other paths, and the sign-in, forgot-password and reset pages show the site's logo), `test/public/branding.test.ts` (the `/m/` pages, the test-send page, the POST result page, the 404 and its HEAD, the hidden archive, the 429 page and the empty form under the Subscribe title), `test/public/test-send-links.test.ts` (the two new test-page wordings) and `form-empty-d119` in `test/contract/public.ts`. The change adds no work to a send, a schedule or storage, so it has no measurement.

## D120. A campaign without recipients is finished by a scan, not by its start (lead, 2026-11-20)
