A running campaign whose audience is empty is not finished by the wake that started it. The runner leaves it running for one scan interval (5 s), and the first scan after that finishes it with `sent` 0 and the finished notification. Until now the wake finished it at once, so a pause or cancel sent right after the start was refused as "Only active campaigns can be paused.". That broke the shared `campaigns-status-pause` check in `contract:campaigns`, which had never run in CI (B122).

- **Why this is the fix:** [D97](09-decisions.md#d97-the-campaign-runner-wakes-on-start-lead-2026-11-13) says the wake only starts sending sooner, and that the result is listmonk's. listmonk creates a campaign's pipe on its scan, so an empty campaign finishes on a scan after its start. The wake now keeps that result.
- **Unchanged:** every HTTP response, every status transition, the finished state and notification, what is sent, and a campaign with recipients, which still starts at the wake. Pause and cancel still take effect at the next batch.
- **Timing:** a campaign with no recipients finishes 5 s after its start here (measured median 5.01 s on the compiled executable, and exactly 5.0 s on the injected clock), and 0 to 5 s after its start in listmonk. A client that waits for `finished` sees the same final state.
- **Bounded state:** the deferral keeps one `retryAt` entry per deferred campaign until it finishes or starts. A campaign paused or cancelled inside its 5 s window keeps its entry until restart, which is one map entry per such campaign and acceptable for the reasons in [docs/perf/B122-runner-empty-campaign.md](../perf/B122-runner-empty-campaign.md).
- **Considered:**
  - Keep the immediate finish and rewrite `contract:campaigns` to expect `finished`: the contract would then never see a running campaign in production, so its pause, cancel and running-stats checks would lose their upstream comparison.
  - Drop the wake on start: that reverses D97, which keeps it.
- **Evidence:** `a campaign without recipients is finished by a scan, not by the wake` in `test/campaigns/runner/runner.test.ts` (injected clock: still running after the wake, finished by the scan), and `contract:campaigns` in the container, where production now passes `status-pause`, the running-stats checks and the D117 test-send checks that follow them. The reference result is unchanged. The change does not touch the send path. Its before/after measurements (empty-campaign timing and the campaign throughput of the existing harness) are in [docs/perf/B122-runner-empty-campaign.md](../perf/B122-runner-empty-campaign.md).

## D121. Four API answers follow the document: MCP probes and refusals, and step IDs are checked before the sequence (lead, 2026-11-20)

`contract:openapi` in CI ran for the first time with B122 and found four places where the served answer and the OpenAPI document disagreed or the order of checks was wrong. The fixes are to the server, except the MCP document entries, which already described the behaviour the tests check.

- **`GET /api/mcp`** answers `405` with the API's JSON error envelope, `{"message":"Method Not Allowed"}`, and `Allow: POST`, where it answered `text/plain`. The document declares every `405` as that envelope. The operation is anonymous: a method refusal reads no credential, so it needs none, and the document says so.
- **`POST /api/mcp`** without a credential answers `401`, as before, and the document now declares it (`No valid credential.`). It is MCP's authorization convention, not the API's `403`, and `test/mcp` checks it.
- **Step routes** (`/api/sequences/{id}/steps/{step}/preview` and `/test`) check the step ID's form before the sequence is looked up, as the reschedule route does. A malformed step is `400` whether or not the sequence exists; a well-formed step of a missing sequence stays `404`.
- **Unchanged:** every other status code, every success body and every write.
- **Considered:** documenting the `text/plain` `405` instead. The API's error envelope is what every other refusal uses, so a client reads one shape.
- **Evidence:** `contract:openapi` (the document's answers and refusals are exercised, the step preview and test take a sample, and every schema is seen with a real value), `test/mcp`, and `test/sequences` (the step-preview and definitions suites).

## D126. Privacy and blocklist need a paired send or campaign (lead, 2026-11-20)

Brief B123, from the first adopter's security audit of main 08ac50f, reproduced black-box against the compiled binary. Three findings. `GET /api/subscribers/:id/subscriber-events` had no permission check, so `subscriber_events:get` (declared in the roles and in [07](07-api-and-ui.md)) was not enforced, and an API user without it read events with their data, over REST and MCP. A subscriber UUID alone was enough for the sub-only actions: `POST /subscription/:camp/:sub` blocklisted with any v4 campaign UUID, because existence was never checked, `POST /subscription/export/:sub` mailed the data and `POST /subscription/wipe/:sub` deleted the subscriber. A step test with `subscriber_id` put the real subscriber's UUID into `unsubscribe_url` and `List-Unsubscribe`, and a test goes to addresses the operator chose (the scenario audit's HIGH, [D105](#d105-a-sequence-step-previews-and-test-sends-with-sample-data-lead-2026-11-17)).

- **Decision:**
  1. `subscriber-events` needs `subscriber_events:get` as well as the subscriber read scope, on REST and MCP, as enrollments and sends need their own permission. The OpenAPI operation declares it (`alsoPermissions`). The other declared permissions of `src/subscribers/routes.ts`, the query routes and `src/events/*` are enforced; no other declaration was unenforced.
  2. A campaign path pairs a subscriber only when the runner delivered that campaign to them: a `campaign_deliveries` row, which the runner writes before it pushes the message. A send path pairs as before: a marketing send with a list, and that subscriber.
  3. A path that pairs nobody writes nothing and answers the path's existing unpaired page (404), the same whether or not the subscriber exists, so no UUID is confirmed by it.
  4. The sub-only `POST /subscription/export/:sub` and `POST /subscription/wipe/:sub` are retired (404).
  5. New paired routes: `POST /m/:send/export/:sub` and `…/wipe/:sub`, and `POST /subscription/:camp/export/:sub` and `…/wipe/:sub`, under the privacy limit. The manage page's privacy and blocklist forms post to the page's own paired base: `/m/<send>` for a send's page, `/subscription/<campaign>` for a campaign's. Campaign messages write no `sends` row, so a campaign's page cannot post to `/m/`.
  6. The placeholder campaign (`dummyUUID`, D117's opt-in and transactional mail) keeps its two flows: its POST ends unconfirmed subscriptions, and its manage save ends the pending lists it does not keep (r1, below). It cannot blocklist, change a name or touch a confirmed list, and its manage page offers no name, blocklist or privacy form. Its links pair any subscriber, so they answer as before for a real subscriber, and the answer to a mismatched or absent subscriber is the same.
  7. A step's preview and test name the placeholder subscriber UUID in the data (`subscriber.uuid`), in `unsubscribe_url`, `manage_url` and the `List-Unsubscribe` header. Its name, email and attributes stay.
- **Unchanged:** the marketing send's pairing for blocklist and privacy; the `/m/` link route; the D116 and D117 test pages; `/subscription/optin/:sub` (confirming a pending opt-in is the subscriber's own consent); webhooks, which queue only from the unsubscribe effects of real changes; the privacy effects (export mail, wipe and its webhook); marketing at-most-once; every real send path; and the scheduler's data, which keeps the real UUID.
- **Residual (r1):** the placeholder's preference save no longer renames or unsubscribes a confirmed list (revision below). The one-click and the pending-opt-in confirm still take only the subscriber UUID, as opt-in mail always has; they end or confirm only pending subscriptions.
- **View pixel (r2, lead):** `GET /campaign/:camp/:sub/px.png` records a view only for a delivered campaign. With individual tracking, the pixel's subscriber must be one the runner delivered the campaign to, and that view names them. Without it, every pixel of mail the server sent names the dummy subscriber, and counts one anonymous view when the campaign has any delivery; a pixel naming a subscriber the campaign was delivered to (mail sent while tracking was on, now off) also counts one anonymous view. The rule is recorded as a deliberate difference in `07-api-and-ui.md` and `src/openapi/differences.ts`. Every other request, an unsent campaign included, gets the same 1×1 image and records nothing. Pixels of delivered mail keep counting under the tracking mode they were sent with, and mail sent with tracking on still counts (anonymously) after tracking is turned off. Mail sent with tracking off and read after it is turned on records no view (its pixel names only the dummy subscriber, which then has no anonymous rule); upstream counts it anonymously. Accepted: it affects only the switch window and keeps the rule "a counted view names a delivery". Tested through HTTP with the runner's real pixels, and through `views` on the campaign statistics API (`test/campaigns/pixel-pairing.test.ts`, `test/campaigns/campaigns.test.ts`).
- **Residual of anonymous tracking (r2):** with individual tracking off, the pixel names no subscriber, so a request for the dummy subscriber of a delivered campaign still counts one view. The count is the setting's own trade-off: it counts mail the server sent, and it names no one. Blocking it would also block the real pixels of anonymous tracking, which must keep counting. It is the one request class that still records a view without a pairing, and it inflates a count rather than exposing data. The owner decides whether anonymous tracking stays.
- **Considered:**
  - Refusing the placeholder's export and wipe, which is what item 5 does, while leaving the tx preference page with no privacy form: a subscriber whose only mail is transactional exports and wipes through the paired pages of their marketing or campaign mail, or through an operator. Chosen.
  - A per-link signed token: it would stop forwarded mail on every path, but it changes every link listmonk users hold. Not chosen.
  - `403` for an unpaired path: it tells a caller the path exists. `404` with the unpaired page is the answer for an unknown subscriber too.
- **Evidence:** `test/public/paired-actions.test.ts` (attacks write nothing, campaign pairing, manage page privacy, the placeholder's flows, marketing sends' privacy), `test/sequences/step-preview.test.ts` (no real subscriber UUID in a step test's message), `test/subscribers/subscriber-events-permission.test.ts` and `test/mcp/subscriber-events-permission.test.ts` (REST and MCP), and the updated `contract:public` (`privacyPath`, `export-bad-uuid`) and `contract:webhooks` (the wipe is paired). No send path changes, so there is no measurement.
- **Revision r1 (council review of PR #171, lead, 2026-11-20):** three blockers and a docs fix. Written test first; see the evidence below.
  - **(c) The placeholder writes pending opt-ins only.** Its manage page lists pending lists and has no name field. Its save ends the pending lists it does not keep, and touches no confirmed list and no name. The one-click and the confirm on `/subscription/optin/:sub` are unchanged. Opt-in mail sent before this change keeps working.
  - **(a) A transactional send pairs through its journal row.** Its `manage_url` names the send's own UUID: `/m/:sendUUID/unsubscribe/:subUUID?manage=true`. A transactional mail carries no `unsubscribe_url`: that key rendered empty for transactional templates, so it is offered for marketing mail only (r2 correction). The route pairs a transactional send by its journal row (kind transactional, the subscriber as recorded). Its page manages confirmed lists and the name. Its POST without `manage` ends pending opt-ins, as (c) does. Blocklist and privacy are refused: they need a marketing send. The privacy part is superseded by [D123](#d123-public-pages-and-subscriber-mail-say-what-happened-and-a-wipe-asks-first-lead-2026-11-21) item 8: export is offered on a transactional link, wipe is not. The RFC 8058 headers stay marketing-only. Old transactional links keep the placeholder path and degrade to (c).
  - **View in browser pairs the way the campaign does.** `GET /campaign/:camp/:sub` answers only a subscriber the campaign was delivered to. Any other path, including an absent subscriber, answers the invalid-link 404 with the same body. The D117 test placeholder keeps its test page.
  - **Docs.** `DELETE /api/campaigns` lists `campaigns:manage` alongside `campaigns:manage_all`, as its handler accepts both.
  - **Deliberate difference from upstream:** upstream's placeholder manage page renames the subscriber and unsubscribes confirmed lists, and upstream's view in browser shows an undelivered draft to any subscriber UUID. Both act on the UUID alone. Production answers (c) and the paired 404. Marked in `test/contract/public.ts` (`prefs-placeholder-save`, `prefs-blocklist-placeholder`, `view-draft`), and recorded in `07-api-and-ui.md`.
  - **Evidence:** `test/public/paired-actions.test.ts` (the reviewer's rename repro writes nothing; a pending opt-in confirm works; a transactional preference link manages confirmed lists through `/m/:send`; an old transactional link ends only pending opt-ins; view in browser pairs and answers unpaired with the same page); `test/e2e/specs/forms.e2e.js` now sends the campaign and follows its delivered mail's link.

## D125. A self-wiped person is not silently re-opted into marketing (lead, 2026-11-20)

Brief B124, from the first adopter's security audit of main 08ac50f, reproduced black-box against the compiled binary. MEDIUM, consent. A self-wipe deleted the subscriber, and its `subscriber.deleted` carried `external_id: null` (D61), so the app could not tell who wiped. On the app's next event the subscriber was created again, `grant_lists` answered `added`, and a marketing sequence started, even one with `reentry: "never"`.

- **Decision:**
  1. **Suppression record.** A self-wipe writes a suppression in the subscribers module: keyed hashes (HMAC-SHA-256) of the normalised address and, when set, of the external ID, the list IDs the person had, and the wipe time. The key is one random 32-byte secret per database, made by migration 23 and read only by the subscribers module. The tables are in [03](03-data-model.md#suppressions-suppression_keys-b124-d125).
     - A create (`POST /api/subscribers`) or an import row that matches takes no list. A public signup that matches joins only double opt-in lists, unconfirmed, with its opt-in mail; the person's own confirmation through that mail clears the suppression.
     - An event's `grant_lists` answers `suppressed` for every list that is not blocklisted, and grants nothing ([05](05-sequences.md#events-api)). The match is made after the upsert, on the address and ID the event leaves.
     - An event does not start a sequence with a marketing step for a matching subscriber. A sequence of transactional steps still starts.
     - `DELETE /api/suppressions` with `{"email"}` needs `subscribers:manage`, matches the address only, and answers `{"removed": n}`. Nothing lists or reveals a suppression.
  2. **Webhook.** A self-wipe's `subscriber.deleted` carries `external_id` and the `list_ids` the person had, captured before the row is deleted; `email` stays `""`. An administrative delete's payload is unchanged. The external ID and list IDs of a `subscriber.deleted` delivery leave storage when that delivery ends, delivered or failed.
- **Unchanged:** D61 otherwise. A wipe still clears the address and external ID from every earlier delivery of the person, and clears their list IDs from those deliveries. Transactional mail, marketing at-most-once, existing memberships and every consent change. The operator's `PUT /api/subscribers/lists` and an admin `PUT /api/subscribers/{id}` are not gated: they are the listmonk-compatible override D99 keeps. A manual enrollment is not refused either; the scheduler's consent check still ends a marketing step for a subscriber with no membership, as it does for any subscriber without one.
- **Why:** the app needs the external ID and the lists to honour the erasure in its own records, and the suppression stops the next event from re-opting the person. Hashes keep the rows free of plain data: the table holds neither the address nor the ID, and the API never returns them. The key sits in the same database, so the hashes protect the rows, not a copy of the file. That is the trade the brief accepted.
- **Considered:**
  - *Suppression alone:* each integrating app would have to implement suppression itself, and any app that did not would re-opt people, which is the bug.
  - *Webhook alone:* the app could mirror the erasure but not stop the re-opt.
  - *Keeping the plain address in the suppression:* D61 refused it.
  - *Refusing a manual enrollment into a marketing sequence:* a second refusal path with its own error for one case the scheduler's consent check already ends.
- **Cost:** one read of the secret and one indexed lookup per created or imported row that asks for a list, per event that asks for a grant, and per event that would start a marketing sequence. An import's budget check (D61) counts a suppressed row's list changes as if they would happen, so it can refuse a batch a little early; it never queues more than the budget. Measured in [B124-suppression](../perf/B124-suppression.md).
- **Upstream:** `cmd/public.go` (`WipeSubscriberData`, the subscription form's insert) at 6fcd95f deletes the subscriber and inserts with the requested lists, so the differences are the suppression alone. The difference is in [07](07-api-and-ui.md) (the deliberate-differences table) and `src/openapi/differences.ts`. The new route is in its `New endpoints` table.
- **Review r1 (adopter review, lead approved, 2026-11-21):**
  - `DELETE /api/suppressions` needs `suppressions:manage`, a permission of its own, not `subscribers:manage`. It is an iglo-only permission, listed with the others that upstream does not have (`validPermissions`, not the stored Super Admin list), so the built-in Super Admin holds it through `roles.can` as it holds the other iglo-only permissions, and role 1 keeps upstream's 28 stored permissions. No default role has it; an administrator grants it to a role like any iglo-only permission, and the role editor's defaults leave it off. A caller without it gets 403 and nothing is removed, over REST and MCP. The first review round had put it in the stored list and added migration 24; the reviewer's blocker (upstream's Super Admin has 28) removed both.
  - An import that unsubscribes applies to a suppressed address. A suppression withholds consent, and an unsubscribe needs none. An import that grants consent still takes no list for a suppressed address.
  - The deliberate differences are marked in the contract suite: the create after a wipe in `test/contract/public.ts` (`wipe-suppresses-create`), the grant result and the wipe payload in `test/contract/webhooks.ts` (`wiped-grant-suppressed`, `wiped-external-id`). Each is labelled with D125 and its reason.
- **Revisit:** if an operator needs to list suppressed people, or an app needs the suppression to survive a re-created subscriber under another address, that is a new decision.

## D122. Personal pages send hardening headers, export mail is throttled per subscriber, and private webhook targets are refused by default (lead, 2026-11-20)

Brief B125, from the first adopter's security audit of main 08ac50f, every finding reproduced against the compiled binary. MEDIUM, three findings. A paired export sent a data mail on every request: twelve POSTs gave ten mails to the victim, and each new client address got ten more a minute, at the operator's SES reputation. The manage page of a personal link (it shows the name, and its forms carry UUIDs) sent no `Cache-Control`, no `frame-ancestors` and no `Referrer-Policy`. And `webhooks.block_private_addresses` defaulted to off, so a webhook to `127.0.0.1:9000`, to `169.254.169.254` or to a Docker service name was accepted, and a signed POST reached an internal container.

- **Decision:**
  1. **Export mail per subscriber.** The data mail has the opt-in mail's window and allowance: one mail per subscriber per ten minutes (`createThrottle`, the same constants as opt-in mail), in addition to the per-client `privacy` limit. A request inside the window is answered exactly as one that mails: the same page, no mail. The throttle is kept in memory, bounded by the opt-in cap, and reads the injected clock.
  2. **Hardening headers on every personal page.** Every HTML answer under `/m/`, `/subscription/` and `/campaign/` carries `Cache-Control: no-store`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY` and `Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'none'`. The policy joins one the page already sets (the campaign view keeps its sandbox), and a page that already names `frame-ancestors` keeps its own. One router middleware (`src/public/headers.ts`) sets them, so a route added under these paths cannot miss them; it also covers the 404, 429 and error answers. JSON, images, the pixel and the archive are unchanged.
  3. **Private targets refused by default.** `webhooks.block_private_addresses` is true by default. Refusal happens at delivery, as before, after DNS, for every address a name resolves to, and the request connects to the address that was checked with the configured `Host` and TLS name, so a second lookup cannot rebind it. Redirects are not followed.
- **Upgrade (superseded by r1 below):** the settings store seeds every default with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, and the earlier release seeded `false` into every install. An upgrade keeps the stored value, as it keeps every stored setting. A stored `false` cannot be told apart from an explicit choice of `false`: the admin form saves every key, and the data has no per-key record of who set what. So a new install gets true, and an install that upgrades keeps its stored `false`, including one that never set the key. Such an operator turns the block on under Settings → Security. The release notes say so, and [operations](../operations.md#upgrades) carries the step.
- **Unchanged:** the webhook delivery itself (signing, at-least-once, the lease, the four slots per receiver, the redirect refusal); marketing at-most-once; the export's sections and its wipe; the paired-path rules of D126; the rate limits; and the archive's and the campaign's own policies.
- **Considered:**
  - *Flip every stored `false` to true on upgrade.* Every install that deliberately delivers to its own network would stop delivering after the upgrade, with no error the operator would see at once. Chosen in r1, with the notice below, which makes that break visible at the first start.
  - *A per-key provenance marker.* It would need a record of who first set each key; the existing rows have none, and a new one would not reach them. Not chosen.
  - *Headers in each route family's own helper.* Three families would each need the call, and a fourth route would miss it. One middleware on the router is the seam (the admin pages' policy works the same way, D71).
  - *Refusing the export at the paired path with a 429.* It tells a caller the subscriber exists and is being exported. The same answer as a success is the no-oracle rule.
- **Cost:** the export throttle is one map of at most 5,000 subscribers, and a full map refuses new keys, so a flood of different subscribers can hold back data mail for ten minutes, as a full opt-in map does. Each personal-page answer sets four headers. Neither is measured: both are constant work on a request that already renders a page.
- **Consequences for embedding:** superseded by r1: the signup form stays frameable.
- **Deliberate differences from upstream:** the export throttle and the headers are not in upstream, and are marked in `test/contract/public.ts` (`export-throttled-same-answer`, `personal-page-headers`), labelled with D122. The default is a native setting, so the contract run for webhooks is production-only, as before (`setting-default`, `default-refused`, `restart-keeps-stored-false`). They are in [07](07-api-and-ui.md) and `src/openapi/differences.ts`.
- **Evidence:** `test/public/public.test.ts` (throttle: twelve requests from three peers send one mail, the same answer each time, and a mail after the window), `test/public/headers.test.ts` (every personal family, its 404 and 429, and the campaign page's CSP merge), `test/webhooks/webhooks.test.ts` (each private, loopback, link-local and unique-local family refused before any request, a public name connecting to its checked address), `test/settings/webhook-default.test.ts` (a new install refuses; a stored false is kept when the store opens again), and `test/contract/webhooks.ts` (the default refuses `localhost`, the loopback receiver and `169.254.169.254` with no request sent; a restart keeps a stored false).
- **Revisit:** none open. The upgrade rule is r1.
- **Revision r1 (lead decisions on B125, 2026-11-21):**
  1. **Upgrade.** Migration 24 (`migrations/0024_private_targets_default.sql`) sets a stored `false` for `webhooks.block_private_addresses` to `true`, once. It runs only at schema 23 and changes only a row that still holds `false`, so an operator's later `false` survives a restart. (A pre-migration read, `migrationTurnsOn` in `src/webhooks/upgrade.ts`, was made in r1 and removed in r2 with the one-time notice.) At every start while the block is on, one attention line per enabled webhook whose target the block refuses (r2: the notice is a check at every start, not a one-time upgrade notice; the reason is in the r2 item below). The line names the webhook ID and the host only, never the path, the query or the secret. The log buffer gets the fixed text "A webhook target is refused by the private-address block".
  2. **Signup form frameable.** `GET /subscription/form`, and its POST answers including the limited and error answers, keep `Cache-Control: no-store` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, and carry no `X-Frame-Options` and no `frame-ancestors`. Every other personal page keeps all four. The form's answers show no subscriber data: the POST result is one of two fixed texts, and its errors are fixed messages.
  - **Revision r2 (lead review of PR 173: the notice runs at every start).** The notice is a check of every enabled webhook's target at each start while the block is on, not a one-time upgrade notice, so a refused target is named again at each restart until it is fixed or the block is off. The check is the delivery's own (`refusesTarget`, DNS included): it lives in the webhooks module, in the delivery loop's start, which reads the store's enabled targets and the block through the loop's own options, and runs in the background after the bind. Each lookup gets the delivery's 10 s deadline on the injected clock; past it the check gives no answer and no line. The lookups run together, so one hanging name delays nothing else. `src/webhooks/upgrade.ts` is removed, and `main.ts` reads nothing before the migrations. The log line is unchanged: webhook ID and host only, with the fixed buffer text.
  - **Revision r2, e2e.** The bounces spec stores `webhooks.block_private_addresses = false` for its loopback receiver and restores it, as the webhooks spec does. The webhooks spec already stored `false` in its `beforeEach`. The import spec's webhooks target a TEST-NET address, which the block refuses anyway, and its budget refusal sends nothing. The campaign editor's loopback receivers are plain HTTP receivers, not webhooks. The measure scripts and the worked example store `false` before they create a loopback webhook.
  - **Considered:** a notice without the flip, leaving never-set installs with the block off. Rejected: it keeps most existing installs open, which is what the audit found.
  - **Evidence:** `test/settings/private-target-upgrade.test.ts` (the migration: a false flips once, a later false survives a restart, a stored true is untouched, a fresh install stores true), `test/bootstrap/private-target-upgrade.test.ts` (the compiled executable: the upgrade turns the value on, and each restart names webhook 1 while it is refused and never the public webhook 2; fixing the target, or setting `false`, stops the line), `test/webhooks/webhooks.test.ts` (every start names each refused enabled target once; a hanging lookup gets the 10 s deadline on the injected clock and delays no other target; fixing the target or setting `false` stops it), `test/public/headers.test.ts` (the form's answers keep two headers, the other personal pages keep four), `test/contract/public.ts` (`signup-form-frameable`), and `test/schema` (schema 24).

## D118. Mail without an alt body gets a generated text part (lead, 2026-11-20)

- **Decision:** every outgoing HTML message without an explicit alt body is sent as `multipart/alternative` with a `text/plain` part generated by one converter, `plainText` (`src/messaging/plain.ts`), built on Bun's native `HTMLRewriter` (no dependency). It covers sequence steps (real and test), transactional mail, campaign mail and test sends, and system mail (opt-in, data export, admin notifications, password reset, the SMTP test). An explicit alt body (a transactional `altbody`, a campaign's stored `altbody`) is used unchanged. The converter drops `head`, `style`, `script`, `title`, comments and elements hidden by an inline style (`display:none`, `visibility:hidden` or `collapse`, `mso-hide:all`, `max-height:0` with `overflow:hidden`). It removes the invisible characters U+034F, U+200B to U+200D, U+00AD and U+FEFF wherever they are, and turns a no-break space (U+00A0, U+2007) into a space, alone or in a run, so spacing never glues words. A link reads `text <URL>`, and `<URL>` alone when the text is the URL or empty (see the revision below); the href is used as rendered, tracked or not. Block elements break lines, list items get `- `, character references decode once (the HTML 4 Latin-1 names and common punctuation; other names are left as written), whitespace collapses, nothing is hard-wrapped, and the result is trimmed. The output is never longer than the input in bytes, so it is linear in the input, and it is charged to the message's budget (`MessageBudget.charge`), which every part of a message shares. It is stored in the outbox payload as `altBody`, so the 1 MiB text bound and the 8 MiB payload bound apply to it. `plainText` is async because `HTMLRewriter` streams; each caller awaits it before the send is queued, so no network call and no wait happens inside a database transaction. Public mail's enqueue becomes a promise that logs its own failure, as a failed mail hook is logged everywhere else in that module.
- **Unchanged:** the HTML part, headers, tracking, Message-ID, marketing at-most-once, the message size limits (the text part counts toward them) and every explicit alt body.
- **Deliberate difference from upstream:** upstream sends the HTML part alone for campaign and transactional mail without `altbody`. Recorded in [07](07-api-and-ui.md) and `src/openapi/differences.ts`, and marked by `plain-alternative-d118` in `test/contract/tx.ts`, which runs on both targets: upstream's expectation is the HTML part alone. The reason is deliverability and text-only readers: a spam filter scores HTML-only mail (`MIME_HTML_ONLY`), and a client that derives text from HTML shows hidden preheader text as the first line (the adopter's 1.4.0 matrix).
- **Considered:** no text part (the status quo; rejected for the reasons above). A third-party HTML-to-text library (a new dependency, and HTMLRewriter covers the rules). Hard wrapping at 78 columns (format=flowed complications, and clients wrap). A full HTML5 entity table (its size buys little; unknown names stay as written and are not guessed).
- **Known edges:** hiding by a class from a `<style>` block is not evaluated, only inline styles. A transactional message whose HTML, generated text and attachments together exceed the 8 MiB payload bound (about 1 MiB, 1 MiB and 4 MiB) is refused at enqueue with 400 `invalid message`, where the HTML alone was sent before. That band needs near-maximum parts in every field, so it is not a practical case, and the bound itself is unchanged.
- **Evidence:** `test/messaging/plain-alternative.test.ts` (the adopter's preheader shape in a sequence step; transactional and campaign sends without an alt body; an explicit altbody kept exactly; links; a hidden span; references, invisible characters, list and cell shape, skipped and hidden content; a near-cap newsletter), `test/contract/tx.ts` (`plain-alternative-d118`), and [the perf note](../perf/B120-plain-alternative.md) (cost per message before and after).
- **Revision (lead, 2026-11-21), before review:**
  - **Link format.** A link reads `text <URL>`, and `<URL>` alone when the text is the URL or empty. RFC 3986 Appendix C delimits a URL in plain text with angle brackets, and link parsers, like mail clients' linkifiers, absorb a closing `)` into the URL, which broke the e2e opt-in link (fixed in this record rather than in the spec). The converter, its tests, `04-sending.md` and the [07](07-api-and-ui.md) row follow it. The property test checks length only, so it is unchanged.
  - **E2E helper.** The consent spec's link reader is unchanged from `origin/main`: with angle brackets, its `[^\s"'<>]` class already stops at `>`. A `)` fix was tried and reverted; the reader stays strict.
  - **Send-rate test 7b.** The rolling-second check at the sink is asserted for the receipts before the restart. After the restart the limiter starts a fresh schedule and receipts carry connection latency, so a receipt pair one interval apart can fall in one second. That boundary is queued in B118, not fixed here.
  - **Send rate in the operations guide.** The cap is enforced when a send is granted and handed to SMTP; receipts include connection latency, so the cap is set slightly below SES's maximum sending rate (`docs/operations.md`, step 6). The restart boundary is not changed here (B118).
  - **Review r1 (council, 2026-11-21), fixed in this record.** An href and a style are decoded once, since the rewriter returns attribute values as written: `&amp;` reads as `&` in the text part's URL, and `display&#58;none` hides. Text outside any element is kept (document-level text). Hidden and skipped elements end where the HTML rules end them, including an implied end (a `td`, `li` or `p` closed by its sibling or by the block that follows); a self-closing SVG or MathML element has no end tag to wait for. A link is one entry on the element stack, so nested links are linear: 70k nested links finish within a fixed bound, and the review's 840 KB template no longer delays `/health`. A conversion fault sends the message without a text part, as before D118, and logs nothing per message. Every path charges its message's own budget (`renderSync` and `renderStep` take one; no fresh budgets). Consecutive links with one href write the URL once, and a symbol-only link writes nothing. A URL's `<`, `>`, whitespace and control characters are percent-encoded. An output longer than its input is sent without a text part: a backstop, since a long escaped URL can outgrow the markup around it. **413 band:** the tx 16 MiB request total counts the generated text, so about 94 recipients of 120 KiB HTML are now refused with 413 where about 136 were accepted (the text is about 45% of the HTML). This stays: the request total bounds memory, and the text is held with the HTML. A caller that needs more recipients per request sends in more requests. The `hidden` attribute is not in D118's list and is not treated as hidden.
  - **Review r2 (council, 2026-11-21), fixed in this record.** **The tracking cap (superseded by r4):** the r2 record tracked at most 256 open elements. Bun's rewriter charges every end tag for the end-tag handlers still pending, so a stack of N nested elements cost about N squared (150k nested `<b>` took 5.7 s in the review). r4 replaces the 256 cap with the held count below. **A new link** ends the open link's output while the rewriter keeps the outer element nested; the outer element stays on the stack until its end tag, which keeps the stack in step with the rewriter. **Symbol-only link text is empty text**, so its URL is written unless a neighbour with the same href already wrote it; this supersedes the r1 rule that a symbol link writes nothing. **Attributes:** a legacy name without `;` followed by `=`, a letter or a digit stays literal, as a browser leaves it, and text decodes the longest legacy prefix. C1 numeric references map to Windows-1252. **Heads:** a head with no end tag ends at `<body>` or at the first element that is not head content, and text directly inside it ends it. **`center`** is a block. **Schemes:** `javascript:`, `vbscript:` and `data:` links keep their text and write no URL, read after a browser's stripping of controls, spaces, tab and newline.
  - **Review r3 (council, 2026-11-21), fixed in this record. The held count.** Bun's rewriter keeps an end-tag handler for each element until its end tag fires. An implied end (a `p`, `li`, `td`, `tr`, `dd` or `option` closed by its sibling or by the element that follows) pops the converter's own entry, but the handler stays held. The converter's stack stays shallow while the held count grows, and each later end tag pays for every held handler, so the conversion was quadratic. The count is now registered minus fired, which is what Bun holds. Past **1024** held handlers the converter throws, which rejects the rewriter's output, and the message goes without a text part, as a conversion fault does. Nothing is logged per message. Two leftovers: `&colon;`, `&Tab;` and `&NewLine;` are named references, so `javascript&colon;` is dropped like `javascript:`; and `select` and `option` are blocks, so `<select><option>a<option>b</select>after` reads as three separate lines, `a`, `b` and `after`, not `a b after`. The cost: a valid message with more than about 1024 omitted end tags now sends without a text part. The times measured for this bullet are superseded by the r4 bullet below.
  - **Review r4 (focused verification, 2026-11-21), fixed in this record. One cap.** Every open element holds an end-tag handler, so the held count (registered minus fired) measures nesting as well as omitted end tags. The 256 open-element cap is removed. In r3 an untracked mode past 256 open elements skipped the count, so the 1024 cap never tripped for those elements; the 256 cap adds nothing once every element counts. A message nested past 1024 open elements, or with more than 1024 held handlers, sends without a text part; that is the cost of the bound, and it is the same cost as for omitted end tags. The worst shape the cap allows (1023 open elements, then stray end tags to 1 MiB) takes 370 ms against 131 ms for flat markup of the same size, and keeps its text part. Measured in the container (`bun test/messaging/measure-plain-nesting.ts`, median of 5 runs per size; the [perf note](../perf/B120-plain-alternative.md) has the tables): nested shapes at 1 MiB take under 1 ms and send no text part; held and stray shapes take 1.4 to 3.6 ms at either size, with ratios of 0.29 to 0.71 for 4x the input. Tests: each nested kind at 1 MiB is under 1 s with no text part past the cap; each held and stray shape at 4x the input costs at most 5x the time; the worst linear case is under 1 s; 4000 unclosed `<p>` give no text part; and a 12 KB unclosed-`<p>` template and an 11 KB template of unclosed spans with stray end tags each leave `/health` answering within 200 ms.

## D131. A refused sequence start is named with its reason, and campaign skips keep one (lead, 2026-11-21)

Brief B116, from the first adopter's scenario and visual audit of main 08ac50f and B103's fault injection. Two gaps. A campaign delivery skipped at its push (the subscriber unsubscribed or was blocklisted between the claim and the push) was journaled `skipped` in `campaign_deliveries` with no reason, and nothing read that table. A blocklisted subscriber's event started no sequence and recorded nothing, for marketing and transactional sequences alike, so an operator could not see why mail did not go.

- **Decision:**
  1. **Send reasons.** Every `skipped` send row names a reason. Every send path already wrote one; the list in [03](03-data-model.md#sends) now gives the codes the code writes. The brief's names map onto existing ones: `blocklisted` is `subscriber_blocklisted`, `capped` is `frequency_cap`, and `late` and `render_failed` already exist. `suppressed` is not a send reason: [D125](#d125-a-self-wiped-person-is-not-silently-re-opted-into-marketing-lead-2026-11-20) withholds sequence starts, not sends.
  2. **Campaign deliveries keep the reason.** Migration 25 adds `campaign_deliveries.reason`. The push-time check names why a subscriber no longer qualifies: `unsubscribed`, `subscriber_blocklisted`, or `subscriber_unavailable` (its UUID or address changed after the claim). A subscriber deleted after the claim takes its delivery row with it (cascade), so it is not sent and not listed (lead correction 2026-11-21 after the adopter check; no tombstone, since a deleted subscriber leaves nothing to report on). `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/skipped` (`campaigns:get`, paged) lists them with the subscriber ID, reason and time, and no address. A campaign delivery is not a send, so it is not journaled in `sends` and not in `GET /api/sends`. Deliveries skipped before migration 25 keep no reason, since none was stored.
  3. **Refused sequence starts.** An event that would start a sequence, or replace its running enrollment, but cannot names the refusal in the response's `enrollments.skipped` as `{sequence_id, reason}`: `subscriber_blocklisted` for every sequence, or `suppressed` for a marketing sequence (D125). No enrollment is made for it. The outcome goes in the event's `enrollments` column, which already held the first request's outcome for idempotent replay, and is written whenever a refusal is named. `GET /api/subscribers/{id}/subscriber-events` returns each event's `skipped`, and the admin's events tab shows it as "Sequences not started". An event older than 7 days names nothing: that is the trigger window, not the subscriber.
  4. **Events page query.** The page sorts its IDs alone and reads the rows by ID. The outcome is a wide column, and carrying it through the sort of all of a subscriber's events cost about 2.4 ms per 50-event page.
- **Unchanged:** every send path, its skip conditions and statuses; marketing at-most-once and the frequency cap; the enrollment table, `reentry`, the active-enrollment rule and the scheduler; the consent checks; the blocklist exit of an enrollment (`exit_reason` `blocklisted`); the send log, statistics and webhooks; D125's suppression; the campaign claim, cursor and cap; event recording, and an idempotent replay, which returns the first request's outcome, now with `skipped`.
- **Naming:** a condition has one reason name everywhere. A blocklisted subscriber is `subscriber_blocklisted` in the send journal, in campaigns and in sequence starts. `suppressed` is new and is used only for sequence starts.
- **Native, not a difference:** upstream has neither the events API nor campaign delivery reasons. The new response field and endpoint are documented in [05](05-sequences.md#events-api), [04](04-sending.md) and [07](07-api-and-ui.md). The `skipped` field is added to existing responses, which clients that ignore unknown fields keep reading.
- **Considered:**
  - *An enrollment in a terminal `skipped` state:* the status CHECK needs a table rebuild, and `any-enrollment` counts every enrollment row, so a refused start would make `reentry: never` refuse for good once the subscriber is unblocked. A new table would be a second store to keep in step with events and their retention. The event's own outcome column already exists and is replayed.
  - *Journaling campaign skips in `sends`:* `source` allows only `tx` and `sequence`, and a campaign row would count in statistics, the frequency cap and webhooks. The delivery row is the campaign's record.
  - *A log line per skip:* a refused send is a normal outcome, not a fault (AGENTS logging rule), and a log line is not visible through the API.
- **Cost:** [B116 perf note](../perf/B116-skip-reasons.md). A refused event writes its outcome, +0.011 ms (about 7%) for a blocked event; plain and started events are unchanged within noise; the events page is faster (1.34 ms to 0.90 ms for 50 events); the push-time check is 4.43 µs to 4.64 µs per recipient. Migration 25 adds one nullable column and a partial index, with no backfill.
- **Numbering:** [B129 to B132](../status.md) hold D127 to D130 in the status file, so this record takes D131.
- **Evidence:** `test/scheduler/skip-reasons.test.ts` (blocklisted marketing and transactional starts, a suppressed marketing start while its transactional sequence still starts, `late`, `frequency_cap`, and every skipped send row naming its reason), `test/campaigns/runner/runner.test.ts` (unsubscribe and blocklist at push, a changed identity), the events and sequence tests, now expecting `skipped`, and `test/schema` at version 25.

## D123. Public pages and subscriber mail say what happened, and a wipe asks first (lead, 2026-11-21)

Brief B126, from the first adopter's scenario and visual audit of main 08ac50f (53 real sends, 8 step tests, 219 screenshots at 1280 and 390 px). Six findings: the data mail looked like listmonk's default, with a bare subject, HTML only and the overstated "all data recorded on you"; the wipe copy read like cancelling a paid subscription and confirmed with a browser `confirm()`; a revisited unsubscribe link showed the form again; the header logo linked to an empty `href` when there was no subscription page; the error pages said "Invalid UUID(s)", "E-mail message not found", "404 - Not found" and "Too many requests"; and the manage page's name field had no label.

- **Decision:**
  1. **Data mail and opt-in mail.** The data mail's subject is "Your data from <site name>", its body is the site's logo or name, the wording is "the data this mailing service holds about you", and its footer names the site, linked to `app.site_url` when set and plain text otherwise. It keeps the JSON attachment and gets the B120 text part (D118), which prints the link as `Site name <URL>`. The opt-in mail is branded the same way. The admin's system mails (password reset, SMTP test, campaign and import notices) show the site name as the logo's alt text and in the footer.
  2. **No product credit.** The public shell's footer and its `poweredBy` prop are gone, and the system mails carry no "Powered by" line. The `public.poweredBy` key stays for the campaign and sample templates, which are B129's.
  3. **Wipe.** A wipe POST without `confirm=true` answers a page, "Delete your email data?", that deletes nothing. The page's button posts `confirm=true` to the same path, and only that POST deletes. Its "Keep my data" link returns to the page the send or campaign shows, the manage view only when preferences are on. The wipe copy names the address, name and list memberships, and says the account with the site is not closed. No `confirm()`.
  4. **Already unsubscribed.** A marketing link (a send's or a campaign's) revisited after every membership it covers is unsubscribed answers "You are already unsubscribed from these emails: <public list names>." with no form, or "You are already unsubscribed from these emails." when none of those lists is public. The memberships decide whatever the list's type, so a private list (the normal case for sequence and transactional mail) is never named. A send's link covers its send's list. A campaign's link covers the campaign's own lists, not every list the subscriber is on. A transactional link covers none; its page is item 8. The page keeps the privacy forms and the manage link it offered before, and a back link when `app.site_url` is set. The manage view is not answered this way.
  5. **`app.site_url`.** A new native setting, empty by default. It takes an absolute `http://` or `https://` URL with a host, under the D107 rules with one difference: a query string is allowed, so a homepage's `?utm_source=email` works. It takes no user name, password or fragment, and no control character. The validator is `validateAbsoluteUrl` in `src/config/bootstrap.ts`, which takes a kind (`root-url` or `site-url`): the root URL keeps its messages and rules, and the site URL's messages say "site URL". The admin General card has the field. The header logo links to the site URL, else to the subscription page when that is on (as before), else to nothing. Every result, error and missing page of the public routes (the "Done", "Unsubscribed", "Data removed", error, expired-link, invalid-link and "Page not found" pages) links "Back to <site name>" when it is set. The control-character check is shared, so a root URL with a control character is now refused too: a tightening of D107 that no working address is affected by.
  6. **Page copy.** `public.linkNotValid` ("This link is not valid.") replaces "Invalid UUID(s)" on every page that shows a bad link. `public.errorFetchingEmail` now reads "This link has expired or the data was removed." `public.pageNotFound` ("Page not found") titles the 404, which keeps its status and has no footer. `public.tooManyRequests` ("Too many requests. Please try again in a minute.") is the 429 page. The data-removed text is "Your email data has been deleted.". A subscription form that is off answers the branded "Page not found" (404), on GET and POST, instead of the generic error message. The `{site}` and `{lists}` substitutions use a function replacer, so a name with `$&`, `$$` or `$1` prints as typed.
  7. **Label.** The manage page's name input has `<label for>` and `id`.
  8. **Transactional link (D126).** A transactional send's page reads its subscriber's memberships. A pending opt-in is an unconfirmed membership of a double opt-in list only: its form cancels only those, naming public lists only, its title and heading are its button's text ("Cancel pending subscriptions"), and its result says "Your pending subscription was cancelled." (plural, "Your pending subscriptions were cancelled.", when more than one was cancelled). An unconfirmed membership of a single opt-in list is an active subscription (an API create without `preconfirm_subscriptions`, or an import), so the page treats it like a confirmed one: no form for it, the manage view, or with preferences off a page titled and headed "Your email settings" that says "This message was sent about your account with <site name>. To stop other emails, use the unsubscribe link in one of them." A manage view with no list to show also shows that sentence. With no open membership the page is titled and headed "Unsubscribed", as a marketing link's already-unsubscribed page is, and says "You are already unsubscribed from these emails." without naming a list. Private pending lists are cancelled without being named: private list names are never shown, and cancelling an unconfirmed opt-in only stops mail. The page offers export, which mails the address on record, and no wipe: a transactional send can carry Cc and Bcc copies, so a link does not prove that the person who opens it is the subscriber, and the in-page confirmation proves intent, not identity. A wipe POST answers the same 404 as before this record and deletes nothing. Its blocklist stays refused.
- **Unchanged:** every status code (the wipe's first POST answers 200 with its confirmation page, as the other privacy answers do); the API's messages, including the JSON `Invalid UUID(s)` and `Too many requests`, which stay as listmonk has them (`globals.messages.invalidUUID` is still the API's text); the send and campaign pairing (D126), the export throttle (B125), the suppression (D125) and the hardening headers; the data the export mail carries; every template's other copy.
- **Deliberate differences from upstream** (in [07](07-api-and-ui.md) and `src/openapi/differences.ts`, marked in `test/contract/public.ts` as `wipe-asks`, `wipe`, `export-subject`, and in `test/contract/cases.ts` for the 404): the copy, the wipe confirmation, the already-unsubscribed state, the branding and credit, the site URL setting. Upstream is at 6fcd95f: `cmd/public.go` (`SubscriptionPage`, `WipeSubscriberData`, `SelfExportSubscriberData`) and `static/public/templates`.
- **Considered:**
  - *`app.root_url` for the back links:* it is iglo.email's own address, not the operator's site.
  - *No back link on the last page:* a dead end. The header logo already goes to the site when it is set, and every result, error and missing page gets an explicit link as well.
  - *`window.confirm()`:* needs script, is easy to click through, and a bare POST that deletes can be triggered by a preview or a retry. A second step on the server works without script.
  - *A GET confirmation route:* a second route to pair. The POST keeps the same pairing check, and a bare POST changes nothing.
  - *Changing `globals.messages.invalidUUID` everywhere:* the JSON API answers with it, and its text is listmonk's contract (AGENTS: listmonk API results). Only page output uses the new key.
  - *The site URL over the subscription page for the logo:* the operator's own site is what a person expects when they click a logo on a mail or a page.
- **Known edges:**
  - After a partial unsubscribe, a send's or a campaign's link says its own lists are already unsubscribed, even while the person is on another list. Blocking all mail is then on the manage page that the link offers, not on the link's own page; `test/e2e/specs/forms.e2e.js` follows that path.
  - Only `en.json` is updated. The other locales keep their old wording for the changed keys until their translators update them.
  - The wipe's confirmation page is a response to the POST, so a reload repeats the POST and shows the page again. It deletes nothing.
- **Cost:** no change to a send, a schedule or storage, so no measurement. The already-unsubscribed check reads one subscriber's memberships on a page view.
- **Evidence:** `test/public/copy.test.ts` (the pages, the already-unsubscribed state for private and campaign lists, the wipe confirmation and its keep link, the back links and the logo link, the `$` patterns, the label, the credit, the data mail and the opt-in mail through the sink with and without the site URL's footer link, the disabled form's 404), `test/scheduler/private-list-link.test.ts` (a sequence's private-list mail through the scheduler and the sink), `test/admin/settings-site-url.test.ts` (the setting through the API, the query string and the site-URL messages), the updated wipe tests (`test/public/paired-actions.test.ts`, `test/public/public.test.ts`, `test/public/suppression.test.ts`, `test/tx/fixture.ts`, and the bounce, webhook, import, MCP and scheduler tests that wipe through the route), `test/contract/contract.test.ts` (the parser's public 404 and its rejected mutations), `test/ui/public.test.ts` and `test/ui/verify.ts` (the compiled 404), and the e2e wipe and export specs. The transactional states are in `test/public/transactional-links.test.ts`: a transactional page's export and refused wipe, a Cc and Bcc copy that cannot wipe and whose export mails only the address on record, the single opt-in member whose cancel POST leaves them and whose next marketing send arrives, the plural result, and the email settings heading.

## D132. Lint adopts @cloudflare/oxlint-config, and what it reports is fixed or tracked per rule (lead, 2026-11-21)

Brief B134, owner request 2026-11-21: adopt [`@cloudflare/oxlint-config`](https://npmx.dev/package/@cloudflare/oxlint-config) and fix what it reports. The preset extends `@nkzw/oxlint-config` 3.0.0 with type-aware correctness and type-safety rules. On `e7b5a65` it reported 21,616 findings (src 10,873, test 10,702, scripts 41); after `oxlint --fix`, 11,451.

- **Decision:** `oxlint.config.ts` at the root replaces `.oxlintrc.json`, and spreads the preset as its README says. The dev dependencies are exact: `@cloudflare/oxlint-config` 0.1.0, `oxlint` 1.94.0 (the preset needs `^1.94.0`; the project was on 1.90.0), and `tsrs` 7.1.0-beta.tsrs4 (the type-aware backend; the preset's peer range is `>=0.25.0-ts7.1.0-beta`). `bun run lint` stays `oxlint .`; `typecheck` stays `bun check --no-script`. Lint is a dev dependency only. There are no runtime dependencies to add.
- **Lead decisions, with their reasons:**
  1. **Config carries forward.** `no-undef: error` stays, the `env` and the `Bun`/`HTMLRewriter` globals stay, and the three ignore patterns stay, added to the preset's `**/node_modules/**`.
  2. **React rules off, jsx-a11y on.** `react/no-unknown-property`, `react/jsx-key`, `react/checked-requires-onchange-or-readonly`, `react/rules-of-hooks` and `react/exhaustive-deps` are off. The admin and public pages render HTML strings through the in-house JSX runtime (no client framework, [AGENTS](../../AGENTS.md)), so `class`, Alpine's `x-*`, `@*` and `:*` attributes and HTML `checked` are correct, and nothing is reconciled or hooked. `jsx-a11y` stays on: its two `alt-text` findings are the thumbnails whose `alt` Alpine binds (`x-bind:alt`), and they now carry a static `alt=""` that Alpine replaces with the file name once the item renders, so the rendered page is unchanged.
  3. **Test code is test code.** Every file under `test/` gets the preset's test relaxations (`no-explicit-any`, `no-non-null-assertion`, `no-unsafe-argument`, `no-unsafe-assignment`, `no-unsafe-member-access`, `only-throw-error`, `unbound-method`) and no others. `scripts/` stays under the source rules.
  4. **`no-console` is scoped by path.** Application code logs errors and attention lines only through the error logger in `src/app.ts` (AGENTS). The CLI command modules (`src/main.ts`, `src/**/cli.ts`) and the build and release scripts (`scripts/**`) print their own command output, so they are exempt. `src/messaging/notify.ts` (its console fallback) and `test/` are not exempt: they are tracked below.
  5. **Every other rule is fixed, not disabled, except where it is tracked.** Silencing comments are not added, except for one `oxlint-disable-next-line no-control-regex` that moved onto the regex line it guards after formatting.
- **Autofix review (B134a).** `oxlint --fix` changed 611 files. It was reviewed by its risk, and the fixes that broke types or meaning were repaired by hand: a `prefer-set-has` rewrite whose type annotation still named an array; a `catch-error-name` rename that turned `{ failure }` into `{ error }`, which changes an object key (now `{ failure: error }`); a `prefer-at` rewrite that made `windows.at(-1)!++` an invalid target; a removed `as object` that was needed; and unused imports left by the type-assertion fixes. The `text-encoding-identifier-case` autofix rewrote the `"utf-8"` label that the XML declaration and charset checks compare, so every UTF-8 declaration would have been refused; those five comparisons are restored, and the rule is off in those five files only ([oxlint.config.ts](../../oxlint.config.ts)). A regex-to-string rewrite turned a byte-order-mark escape into the literal character, which is an escape again. `prefer-dom-node-append` rewrote `appendChild`, whose returned element an end-to-end spec used (`append` returns nothing); the spec now appends and then uses the element, which the `e2e` run caught. A check of every changed string and template value (the raw value for `String.raw`) and every numeric value, across `src/`, `scripts/` and `test/`, found no other change. `eqeqeq` is not autofixed, so every `== null` remains a finding (tracked). Numeric separators, `curly`, `array-type`, `prefer-string-replace-all` (only on global regexes), `prefer-string-raw`, `no-hex-escape` and the type-only import rewrites are behaviour-neutral by construction; the suite and the typecheck cover the rest.
- **Split.** Rules that still fail after autofix are `off` in `oxlint.config.ts` beside a `// B134b` (src or scripts findings remain) or `// B134c` (test-only findings) comment. **B134b** fixes every src and scripts finding and removes its comment, one rule per commit; **B134c** does the same for `test/`. Afterwards no `B134b`/`B134c` comment remains, and the path-scoped overrides left by B134b/c (`src/messaging/notify.ts`, `test/`, and the `@typescript-eslint/no-floating-promises` override the preset's TypeScript override needs) are removed with them.
- **Runtime proof.** The type-aware rules run in the CI image `oven/bun:1.4.5` (linux arm64): a throwaway probe in `src/` fails `typescript/no-unsafe-member-access` when it is enabled, and `typescript/only-throw-error` (type-aware, enabled by the preset) and `no-only-tests` (a JS plugin) fail it from the config alone. The config and the JS plugins load under Bun: `bun run lint` ran with no `node` on the login `PATH`, so the image's Bun-provided node shim is not required.
- **Unchanged:** the runtime dependencies (`liquidjs`, `nodemailer`, `qrcode`, `saxes`) and the patches; the compiled executable (`bun build --compile`); the HTTP API, SQL, stored formats and error messages. Errors that now carry `cause` (`preserve-caught-error`) keep their messages.
- **Considered:**
  - *Stay on the old `.oxlintrc.json` rules:* it does not check types (`no-unsafe-*`, `no-floating-promises`), and most of the preset's findings are real.
  - *Disable the type-aware rules:* the test-side `no-unsafe-*` findings are where the type gaps live (`test/contract/*.ts`, fixtures); keeping them enforced is the point of the migration.
  - *Inline disables for the React rules:* a rule that cannot apply to our runtime is better turned off once, in the config beside its reason, than silenced at every JSX file.
- **Cost:** no runtime, send, schedule or storage change, so no measurement. The lint run takes about 0.6 s on 674 files.
- **Evidence:** `bun run lint`, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run format:check` and the local `bun run check` in `oven/bun:1.4.5` (linux arm64) on the final commit, and every `run:` step of both `ci.yml` jobs, in the PR body.
- **Re-split and exceptions (lead, 2026-11-22).** The first B134b worker fixed 28 rules and stopped before the type-safety rules, so the src work is now three PRs: B134b (those 28 rules, `withDash`, the notify logger), B134d (`no-non-null-assertion` and the remaining `quality/*`, `consistent-function-scoping` and `unbound-method`) and B134e (`no-unsafe-*`), then B134c for `test/` ([brief](../briefs/B134.md)). These exceptions stay, scoped by file with the reason beside them in `oxlint.config.ts`: `@nkzw/no-instanceof` in twelve files whose checks are for own error classes, Promise, Response, Uint8Array and liquidjs Output/Drop (a duck-type check would also accept thenables and look-alikes); `typescript/no-explicit-any` in seven files (the JSX runtime and render, and five settings-record files) (typed props and a per-key settings schema are a design change: `unknown` there gives 479 and 128 type errors); `quality/no-chained-type-assertions` in `src/messaging/email.ts` (the Nodemailer envelope boundary). `quality/no-conditional-empty-object-spread` is fixed only where the object never reaches a wire or stored format, because JSON key order there is observable. `unicorn/text-encoding-identifier-case` runs with `{ withDash: true }` and the five-file exception is gone: the comparisons keep `"utf-8"`, and the remaining findings were `toString("utf8")` decodes, now spelled `"utf-8"` (the same encoding).
- **Test-side exceptions (lead, 2026-11-22, B134c #182).** `no-console` is off for `test/**` for good: test runners, measure and reference scripts print their own command output. Scoped by file with the reason beside them: `typescript/await-thenable` where bun:test types `expect(...).rejects` as `void` but the `await` runs the assertion; `@nkzw/no-instanceof` where a test needs the exact class (FormData bodies, an own error class, DOM-clobbering audits); `quality/no-reflect-get` for the crash fixture's Proxy; the two agent-browser scripts evaluated as an expression; the tail probe that patches built-ins by name; and the runners that keep a watcher beside their loop. The rest of `test/` follows the preset like `src/`, with the D132 test relaxations; the remaining test rules are B134g ([brief](../briefs/B134.md)).

## D133. Parallel work and macOS checks (owner, 2026-11-22)

- **Owner direction:** "why can't you run this stuff directly on macOS ... most of the work don't need it ... you can paralelize that, so instead of blindly waiting for this stuff to finish, actively keep improving that."
- **Cause of the Linux-container rule (lead):** the test harness, not the product, needed Linux for most of the failures. `listensOn()` in `test/serve-port.ts` confirms a spawned server owns its port through `/proc/net/tcp` and `/proc/<pid>/fd`, so on macOS every test that starts the compiled server waited 30 s and failed ("The server did not start"; `test/bootstrap` 13 pass / 29 fail in 585 s). The rest are tests of Linux-only product features (the `/proc` memory and system readers) and one macOS SQLite difference in `backup`. B136 makes the harness portable and skips only the Linux-only feature tests on other platforms.
- **Decision:** amends D90/D93's "one role at a time, serially": independent tasks run in parallel, each worker in its own worktree and containers; review, the adopter differential and the Linux acceptance of a PR run alongside each other, and the merge waits for all of them. Once B136 has merged, workers iterate and run `bun run check` on macOS; the Linux container run (both `ci.yml` jobs) stays required before merge, because Linux is the only supported platform (D89) and the reference and bubblewrap steps are Linux-only, but it no longer gates the review. Models and the single reviewer per round are unchanged.
- **Measurement:** per-step times of both `ci.yml` jobs and e2e on `main` are recorded in the B136 PR and `docs/perf/test-speed.md` once measured.

## D129. An event claims an address its holder has no external ID for, and 409 names the conflict (lead, 2026-11-20)

- **Decision:** an adopter that syncs an app's users through events built, in every integration, a search-then-PATCH/DELETE repair for two cases. Both are now answered by the API, and every API error says what happened in a stable form.
  1. **Claim.** An event names an external ID X and an e-mail E. If E belongs to a subscriber H with no `external_id` (imported or form-subscribed), and no subscriber holds X, the event sets H's `external_id` to X and applies its other identity fields (name, attribs) as any update does. H keeps its lists, memberships and history, and no subscriber is created. The claim needs H to be on no list, or the caller to manage one of H's lists (the rule of a grant, D99). A holder on no list is claimed by any caller: an e-mail-only event creates its holder on no list, and the events-only role (`subscriber_events:create`, [operations](../operations.md#api-user-for-an-app-integration)) depends on that anonymous-then-identified flow. **The one deliberate difference from main**, which links any holder: a caller that manages none of H's lists gets `409` `email_conflict` and nothing is written. Scope also decides whether a conflict may name H's external ID (2).
  2. **Conflict.** Any other case where E belongs to a subscriber other than the one the event names is `409` with `code` `email_conflict` and nothing written. The body names `conflict.external_id` (H's) only where H has one and the caller manages H as above; otherwise it names nothing, so a scoped caller learns nothing of a subscriber it cannot see. The same rule answers `POST /api/subscribers`, `PUT /api/subscribers/{id}` and `PUT /api/subscribers/external/{external_id}` for an e-mail another subscriber holds; each batch item carries its own `code` and `conflict`. A taken external ID is `409` `external_id_conflict`, and names nothing.
  3. **Stable codes.** Every API error keeps listmonk's `message` and adds `code`, one value of the fixed list in `src/server/error-codes.ts`. A route names a finer code on its `HttpError` (`email_conflict`, `external_id_conflict`, `held`, `not_found` on a by-ID 400, `invalid_credentials` on a 403 for a wrong credential, `subscriber_changed`, `test_running` and `upload_changed` on the 409s that are races, `result_too_large` and `client_closed` on the analytics refusals); every other error takes the code of its status. Every `429` and `503` sends `Retry-After` in seconds: `1` for `429` and `30` for `503`, unless the route names its own wait, as the limiters do.
  4. **Retry guidance.** `docs/operations.md#retrying-api-errors` and the OpenAPI envelope list the permanent statuses (400, 401, 403, 404, 409, 413, 422) and the transient ones (429, 503, other 5xx, network errors), with the three 409s that name a race (`subscriber_changed`, `test_running`, `upload_changed`) counted as transient. A write with an `idempotency_key` replays its first answer, so a retry is safe.
- **The case the brief left open (lead):** the event's X is held by subscriber S, and E by H with no external ID. Claiming would give H the X that S holds, which is either a duplicate or S's X moved to H. Re-pointing an existing external ID is refused everywhere (05-sequences.md, the `PUT` rules of D38), so the event is refused as in (2), names nothing, and the repair stays an operator's: merge or delete one of the two. This is the one place the claim does not apply.
- **Unchanged:** the lookup order (external ID, then e-mail) and the rule that an existing external ID is never re-pointed by a lookup by e-mail; a repeated `idempotency_key` replays its first result before any conflict is decided, so a retry after a claim is not a conflict; every route's status, permission and grant rules; listmonk's `message` text, which the contract suites check; the events' grants (D99) and their scope rule.
- **Considered:**
  - *Keep the 409 and let adopters repair:* what every adopter rebuilt. Refused: the cases in (1) are the only ones the API can settle safely by itself.
  - *Move X from S to H when H has none:* re-points an external ID that the app uses to find S. Refused for the reason above.
  - *Name the holder to every caller:* leaks an external ID across list scopes. The manage-scope rule keeps the name to callers that could read the holder.
  - *Take each route's `Retry-After` from its limiter everywhere:* only a limiter knows its window, so the default is a floor, and a route with a known wait keeps its own.
  - *Structured `code` only on the new errors:* clients would still parse English for the rest. The enum is additive, so every error carries one.
  - *Require list scope for every claim:* it breaks the events-only role. An e-mail-only event creates a holder on no list, which no list-scoped caller can then claim, so the anonymous-then-identified flow answers 409 where main answers 200. Refused: a holder on no list is claimed by any caller, and only a holder on lists needs the caller to manage one of them.
- **Cost:** the conflict decision runs once per event that names both an e-mail and an external ID: one indexed e-mail lookup inside the event's write transaction. The memberships of a holder are read only on the claim path (a holder with no external ID, found by e-mail), and never for the common event, a known subscriber sending its own address with its external ID. Every other event pays nothing new. No schema, send, schedule or storage-layout change. **Measured** in [B131-event-claim](../perf/B131-event-claim.md): three runs each of `test/events/measure.ts events` on the merge base and on the head, in one Linux container on a shared host. Five interleaved pairs on the real merge base (round-2 verification) give accept p50 6.45 vs 6.24 ms and p99 50.2 vs 48.9 ms (medians), both added queries are index lookups (about 13 µs on 200,000 subscribers): no regression.
- **Evidence:** `test/events/conflicts.test.ts` (the claim keeps lists and history, a scoped caller's refusal writes nothing, an external ID held elsewhere refuses the claim, the named and unnamed conflicts, the batch item, the replayed key, `PUT` by external ID, `POST` create, and the `code`s), `test/events/events.test.ts` and `test/events/grant.test.ts` (the existing rules with their exact bodies now carrying `code`), `test/server/error-codes.test.ts` (a code for every status, `Retry-After` for 429 and 503, `held`, HEAD, a logged 500, the events API's 413 and in-flight 429), `test/openapi/coverage.test.ts` (the envelope enum, the codes of each response, the 409 conflict body, and the rows of 07), and `test/server/server.test.ts` (the shared envelopes, with the `code` marked as the one additive difference).

## D130. Webhook secrets rotate with an overlap, and the receiver is documented and tested (lead, 2026-11-20)

- **Decision:** `POST /api/webhooks/{id}/rotate-secret` (`webhooks:manage`) makes a new secret and returns it once, with the webhook. The old secret keeps signing for `overlap_hours` (an integer from 0 to 168, default 24; 0 drops it at once). During the window the header carries one `v1` per secret, the new one first: `t=<unix>,v1=<new>,v1=<old>`, the form common webhook providers use. A receiver accepts a delivery when any `v1` verifies. After the window only the new secret signs. Each attempt judges the end by the injected clock, and the retention pass removes the stored old secret within about an hour of its end. A second rotation ends the earlier old secret at once, so at most two secrets sign. The answer's `previous_secret_until` states when the old one stops, and `GET` shows the same value.
- **Receiver guidance:** [operations.md](../operations.md#outbound-webhooks) documents what a receiver must do: verify the HMAC over the exact raw body with a constant-time compare; accept any `v1`; reject a `t` more than 5 minutes from its clock; keep each delivery `id` for 25 hours as a replay window, since iglo.email retries a delivery for up to 24 hours with the same `id`; cap the body at 64 KiB; and answer `2xx` only after the durable write, including for a duplicate. It embeds a TypeScript verification helper on `node:crypto` alone. `test/webhooks/verify.test.ts` tests the file the docs embed, and fails when the two differ.
- **Unchanged:** the signature's `t` and its HMAC-SHA256 over `t.body`, so a receiver built for one `v1` verifies every delivery outside a rotation; `PUT` still refuses a `secret` (the rotation is its own route); the stored form of secrets, which is not encrypted, as before; the error codes (a bad body is `invalid_fields`, an unknown webhook `not_found`, a missing permission `permission_denied`); delivery leases, retries, the 24-hour window and the at-least-once rules. Webhooks are native (no upstream route), so no listmonk difference applies and the contract suite has no reference run.
- **Considered:**
  - *A second header for the old signature:* receivers would read two headers, and providers already carry several `v1` values in one. Refused.
  - *Keep rotation as delete and recreate:* the URL, the event history and the delivery rows would be lost, and the old secret would stop at once with no overlap. Refused.
  - *Encrypt the stored secrets:* signing needs the secret, so the key would sit next to the database on the same host. Left to the host, as the operations guide says.
  - *Allow any number of secrets:* more rows and more `v1` values for an operation that runs rarely. Two secrets cover a rotation with a receiver still on the old one, and a second rotation ends the older window at once. Refused.
  - *Check replays on the server:* only the receiver knows what it has applied, so the replay window belongs in its transaction, and the docs say so.
- **Cost:** an attempt reads the body and the webhook's secrets in one primary-key join in place of reading the body alone, and computes one HMAC per live secret (one in the normal case). Storage: two nullable columns on a table of at most 20 rows, and one `UPDATE` per retention pass on that table. The delivery path measurement is in [B132-webhook-rotation](../perf/B132-webhook-rotation.md).
- **Evidence:** `test/webhooks/rotation.test.ts` (the header carries both signatures in the window and either secret verifies; only the new one after the window, by the injected clock; overlap 0 switches at once; a rotation ends an earlier old secret; the `overlap_hours` bounds and the default window; 404; 403 for a user without `webhooks:manage`, and a forged request; the retention pass ends a window at its end and not before), `test/webhooks/verify.test.ts` (the documented helper on a real delivery, a tampered body, a stale or future `t`, a replay and a wrong secret, and the docs snippet against the tested file), `test/contract/webhooks.ts` (rotation against the compiled server, and the window across a restart), `test/schema` and the bootstrap tests at version 26, and `test/openapi` and `test/mcp` for the new route and fields.

## D127. The operator's brand replaces the credit, and the source offer stays in the admin (lead, 2026-11-20)

Brief B129, from the first production adopter's report and the owner's direction of 2026-11-20: "fix as much as possible or reasonable in iglo.email, unless it is project-specific; good out of the box". Every item is generic, so each adopter would otherwise rebuild it.

- **Decision:**
  1. **No product credit (owner decision, not open).** No public page, system mail (HTML or text part), campaign layout or sample template says "Powered by" or names listmonk. The public shell's footer went in B126 (D123); this removes the rest: the footers of the default archive layout and the sample transactional template, and the sample's link to listmonk's documentation. The credit is removed, not hidden: no setting brings it back. The `public.poweredBy` key stays in `en.json` ("Powered by"), so an operator's own template that still prints it reads as before; no built-in uses it, and the other locales keep their translations.
  2. **Source offer (AGPL-3.0 §13).** The offer moves from the public footer to the admin sidebar: a "Source code" link to `https://github.com/iglo-tech/iglo.email` on every admin page. [`docs/operations.md`](../operations.md#brand-fonts-and-the-source-offer) tells an operator who modifies iglo.email to keep offering their source. The public pages carry no offer.
  3. **Brand settings.** `appearance.brand.accent` is a hex colour, `#rgb` or `#rrggbb` in any case, or empty for the built-in blue (`#0055d4`). `appearance.brand.font` is `system-sans` (the default), `system-serif`, `system-mono`, or a Google Fonts family name of at most 60 characters: a letter first, then letters and digits, with single spaces between words. `appearance.brand.header` is `auto` (the default), `logo`, `name` or `logo_and_name`. A value outside these rules is a 400 and nothing is saved. The public pages take the accent and the font as custom properties on the document element (`--brand-accent`, `--brand-font`), and the shell's CSS reads them with today's values as fallbacks. A set accent also draws a 4 px rule above the card; an empty one keeps the page as it was. A Google family's stylesheet is linked from `fonts.googleapis.com` only when a family is set.
