The opt-in and data mails take the accent and the font as inline styles; a mail never loads a web font.
  4. **Header.** `auto` shows the logo when a logo is set and the site name otherwise. `logo` and `name` show one of them, and `logo_and_name` shows both. The header's link wraps what it shows and goes to `app.site_url`, else to the subscription page, else nowhere (D123's rule, so no empty `href`).
  5. **Logo and favicon upload.** The admin's General card has Upload and Remove beside `app.logo_url` and `app.favicon_url`. Upload posts the file to the media library (`POST /api/media`, `media:manage`; the control is shown only to a user with `settings:manage` and `media:manage`) and puts the media URL in the form. Save stores it, as it stores every other field. The API keeps taking the URLs directly.
  6. **Sign-in pages.** The admin's Content-Security-Policy adds `fonts.googleapis.com` to `style-src` and `fonts.gstatic.com` to `font-src` only when a Google family is set. Otherwise the policy is the one D71 set.
- **Unchanged:** every send, schedule and storage path, except that migration 0027 rewrites the unedited built-in template rows (below); the copy, the status codes and the privacy forms of the public pages (D123); the default accent and the default font, so the public pages and the two mails read as before (the form controls' font and the focus ring are the exception, see below); the other settings; the API's messages and its contract answers. The new keys join the settings document and nothing else is renamed.
- **A visible change to a default install:** with no logo set, the automatic header shows the site name instead of the bundled iglo logo, as item 4 specifies. Upload a logo, or set the header to `logo`, to keep the logo. Inputs, selects, textareas and buttons on the public pages now take the page's font rather than the browser's (lead decision, review follow-up), and the default focus ring is the built-in blue, drawn as an outline.
- **Existing installs (migration 0027, lead decision on review of B129, round 1):** the template store seeds the built-in templates only into an empty database, so an install made before this record still holds the seeded "Default campaign template", "Default archive template" and "Sample transactional template" with their old credit or fixed blue. Migration 0027 rewrites each of the three rows only when its type and its whole stored body match the seeded body byte for byte (the two campaign layouts also need `body_source` null). It writes the body this release seeds and the row's `updated_at`, and changes nothing else: no name, subject, default flag, campaign reference or sender. An edited, renamed or cloned copy keeps its body. `public.poweredBy` is restored in `en.json`, so an operator's own template that still prints `{{ "public.poweredBy" | t }}` reads "Powered by" and does not show the raw key (the other locales kept their translations). The migration and its timing are in [`docs/perf/B129-template-credit.md`](../perf/B129-template-credit.md); its upgrade test is `test/bootstrap/template-credit-upgrade.test.ts`.
- **Review follow-ups (B129 round 1, lead decisions from the adopter check):** no bundled logo is shown: with no logo URL, `logo` and `logo_and_name` show the name, and `logo_and_name` is one row with a smaller logo. Every header mode is aligned to the start, so a long name wraps beside the logo (B129 round 2, adopter). The focus ring is the accent when it has 3:1 contrast against white, else `#111`. Button text is black or white by the WCAG contrast of the accent, so `#ffeb3b` takes black and `#009688` takes black too (5.1:1 against 3.7:1 for white), and `#ff0066` takes black (4.9:1 against 3.9:1). Form controls inherit the brand font. Link text takes the link colour (`--brand-link`, `brand_link`): the accent when it has 4.5:1 against white, else the accent with its HSL lightness stepped down until it has (`#ffeb3b` gives `#857700`, `#ff0066` gives `#e8005d`, `#009688` gives `#008478`, since it has 3.7:1). The outline button's text and border take it too (the border reaches 3:1 for a component boundary, WCAG 1.4.11). Filled buttons, the top rule and the remaining borders keep the accent, checkboxes and radios take it through `accent-color`, the underline takes `currentColor`, and a Google family is prepended to the surface's own stack with the 400, 600 and 700 weights. Every system mail, the campaign and archive data and the admin template preview take the brand, and an archived campaign page loads the Google stylesheet like the other public pages; the default archive layout shows the logo or name above its content. The mail header follows the header mode. A new install's `app.from_email` and SMTP `return_path` are placeholders (`Newsletter <noreply@example.com>`, `bounce@example.com`) that name no product; no stored value is rewritten. The logo upload needs `settings:manage` and `media:manage`, and the admin's Google hosts are added only on the four sign-in paths.
- **Considered:**
  - *Keeping the credit behind a setting:* the owner asked for its removal, and a credit that can be switched on is still shipped.
  - *Loading Google fonts through the server:* keeps the subscriber's browser off Google, but the server would then fetch and serve every font file, a second path to keep (D89). The cost is written in the operations guide instead.
  - *A top rule from the default accent:* changes every page's look. The rule shows only when an operator sets an accent.
  - *Web fonts in mail:* mail clients do not load them reliably, and a mail would contact Google when it is opened. Mail names the family in a font stack and falls back.
  - *An upload that saves the setting at once:* the settings form saves as a whole (D44), so a separate save could overwrite edits that are not saved yet. The form takes the URL and Save stores it.
- **Deliberate differences from upstream:** the brand settings are native, and the output has no credit where upstream's footer says "Powered by listmonk". Both are in [07](07-api-and-ui.md) and `src/openapi/differences.ts` ("Brand settings and no credit (B129, D127)").
- **Cost:** no change to a send, a schedule, a query or storage. A public page reads three more settings it already reads with the others. A mail's or a campaign's context gains eight brand values (`logo_url`, `site_name`, `site_url`, `brand_accent`, `brand_link`, `brand_on_accent`, `brand_font`, `brand_header`), and a template preview reads the settings once per request. Migration 0027 runs once, on three rows; its timing is in [`docs/perf/B129-template-credit.md`](../perf/B129-template-credit.md).
- **Evidence:** `test/public/brand.test.ts` (the default and branded shell; each header mode; no empty `href`; the data and opt-in mails' inline accent and font; no credit in the pages or the mails' HTML and text parts), `test/templates/credit.test.ts` (every built-in campaign layout, sample transactional template and system mail, HTML and text part), `test/admin/settings-brand.test.ts` (each validation rule, with nothing saved on a refusal; the source link; the sign-in page's policy with and without a Google family), `test/public/copy.test.ts` (the header and mail-link expectations that the header and colour changes move), the logo upload in `test/e2e/specs/settings.e2e.js`, `test/bootstrap/template-credit-upgrade.test.ts` (migration 0027 on unedited and edited stored templates, and the branded admin preview), and `test/campaigns/campaigns.test.ts` (a test-sent campaign carries the accent and the link colour in the delivered HTML).

## D124. Event times, late transactional steps and test subjects (lead, 2026-11-20)

- **Decision:** three changes, from a real adopter's scenario and visual audit of main 08ac50f.
  1. **Future `occurred_at`.** An event whose `occurred_at` is more than 15 minutes ahead of the server clock is refused with `400 Invalid fields: occurred_at` (`code` `invalid_fields`): a single event, or its batch item alone. Nothing is written, and its `idempotency_key` is not consumed, so the same key with a valid time is a first request. Exactly 15 minutes ahead is accepted. Within the skew the time is kept as given, and a sequence the event starts is due at that time, not before (`MAX_OCCURRED_AHEAD_MS` in `src/events/input.ts`, read against the injected clock).
  2. **Late transactional steps.** A transactional step with `max_late` is skipped as `late` (B116's reason) when it runs after `due_at + max_late`, as a marketing step is. The scheduler already applied a step's own `max_late` to any kind; this record names the rule, documents it in [05-sequences.md](05-sequences.md) and pins it with a test. Unset keeps today's behaviour: transactional mail is never skipped for lateness.
  3. **Campaign test subject.** A campaign test message's subject starts with `[Test] `, as a step test's does ([D105](#d105-a-sequence-step-previews-and-test-sends-with-sample-data-lead-2026-11-17)). This is a deliberate difference from listmonk, listed in [07](07-api-and-ui.md) and marked in `test/contract/campaigns.ts`. A real send's subject is unchanged.
- **Unchanged:** the 7-day trigger window; every other event answer and its statuses; the step's `max_late` default (none for transactional, `P2D` for marketing); campaign test inertness ([D117](#d117-campaign-test-sends-are-inert-their-links-name-a-test-placeholder-and-their-message-is-untracked-lead-2026-11-20)): its links, counts and tracking; the scheduler's decision and its commit rules.
- **Considered:**
  - *Clamp a future `occurred_at` to now:* changes the app's data without telling it, and the app would never learn its time was wrong. Refused: the answer names the field.
  - *Accept any future time:* a year typed as the wrong digit holds an enrollment for years with no error. Refused: 15 minutes covers the skew between an app's clock and the server's.
  - *A sequence-level `max_late` for transactional steps:* steps already carry the field, so a second place for the same limit is a second way to do one thing (D89). Refused.
  - *A default `max_late` for transactional steps (for example `P2D`):* would silently stop mail an app sends on purpose for an old event, such as a delayed receipt or a backfill. Refused: the author opts in per step.
  - *Keep the campaign test subject as given:* a recipient cannot tell a test from a real send. D117 already keeps the test inert, so the prefix is the only thing a test changes. Refused as a reason to keep listmonk's subject.
- **Cost:** the event path compares one parsed `occurred_at` with the clock per event that sends one: no query and no write. The scheduler's decision is unchanged. A campaign test message is one string concatenation longer per test recipient. No schema, storage or send-path change. The measurement is in [B128-event-time](../perf/B128-event-time.md).
- **Evidence:** `test/events/future-occurred.test.ts` (14 and exactly 15 minutes accepted; 16 minutes refused with nothing written and the key unused; a batch refuses only its far item), `test/scheduler/late-transactional.test.ts` (a six-day-old order: a step with `max_late` is a `late` skip, the same step without it sends; an event 14 minutes ahead sends at its time, not before), `test/campaigns/test-subject.test.ts` (a test's SMTP subject starts with `[Test] `; a real send's subject is unchanged), `test/contract/campaigns.ts` (the prefix on the compiled production, the bare subject on the reference), `test/openapi` (the new row in `differences.ts` and the operation text).

## D134. Liquid authoring: bounded filters, a save-time render, and the converter's names (lead, 2026-11-22)

Brief B135, from the adopter's differential runs on B134e and B134f (status item 1g). Every item was pre-existing on main.

- **Decision:** the allow-list of D23 gains twenty-three filters: `size`, `json`, `downcase`, `capitalize`, `prepend`, `append`, `replace` and `replace_first` (literal strings, no regular expression), `split`, `join`, `slice`, `truncate`, `truncatewords`, `strip_html`, and `plus`, `minus`, `times`, `divided_by`, `round`, `first`, `last`, `newline_to_br` and `url_encode`. Each charges the message budget before it builds its output (the table below). `template_reference` lists exactly the registered filters, and a test compares the two. A save that names an unknown filter says which one. A save renders the template once against the preview's sample and refuses a failure its own text causes, with the cause in words; a send that fails for a value names the expression and the kind of value. A `for` tag whose collection has a filter is refused at save. The `date` format is read left to right, so `%%` is a literal percent sign. A calendar date that does not exist (`2026-02-30`), and a value that is not a date, render empty as a value; as a literal they are refused at save. A list or object prints as text: a list as its items' text joined with nothing, an object as its JSON, both bounded by the message budget. A number in transactional `data` above 2^53 prints as the nearest double: documented as a JSON-number limit, with ids sent as strings. The text/plain converter decodes the HTML spec's Greek, symbol and uppercase names and numbers ordered lists.
- **Unchanged:** every existing filter and its arguments; the send path's status (a failing send is still 400); the preview; JSON parsing; the D54 budgets; the converter's linear time. `escape` is not added (below).
- **Object loops.** `{% for kv in data.obj %}` already renders each `[key, value]` pair as LiquidJS does, inside D23's plain-data boundary and the D54 work budget: an object costs the same as an array of its length, so a loop over about 1,000 keys is refused at send (measured: 500 keys render, 1,000 are refused). The tests pin keys and values, nested values, an empty object's else branch, `forloop`, and the budget. The adopter's "every send fails" symptom did not reproduce for any object shape or size up to 500 keys. The failure a value printed as text causes is real: an object or list printed as text (`{{ kv }}`, `{{ data.obj }}`) failed every send. It now prints as text (the review's blocker, below): a list as its items' text joined with nothing, an object as its JSON, both bounded and charged before they are built. The other value failures name their expression at send.
- **Filter cost.** Each filter writes at most the bytes its inputs already occupy, so it charges those bytes, or the bytes of the result it is about to write, before it allocates it. Where the count of work can grow, it counts first and charges a step per unit:

  | Filter | Args | Charged before the output is built |
  |---|---|---|
  | `size` | 0 | one step; one per key of a plain object (at most 1,024 keys) |
  | `json` | 0 | the exact UTF-8 length of `JSON.stringify`, counted from the value, and a step per node |
  | `downcase`, `capitalize` | 0 | the bytes of the mapped text, per character |
  | `prepend`, `append` | 1 | the bytes of the input and the argument |
  | `replace`, `replace_first` | 2 | occurrences counted first (a step each), then the result's bytes |
  | `split` | 1 | the parts, counted first (over 1,024 is refused), then the input's bytes |
  | `join` | 1 | the items (at most 1,024), then the result's bytes |
  | `slice` | 2 | a string: the piece's bytes, sliced by code point; an array: a step per item |
  | `truncate` | 2 | the input's and the ellipsis's bytes |
  | `truncatewords` | 2 | the input's and the ellipsis's bytes; a step per word scanned |
  | `strip_html` | 0 | the input's bytes, and a step per KiB; then the references decode, which never grow a text |
  | `plus`, `minus`, `times`, `divided_by` | 1 | one step; the result is a number of under 25 characters, printed when it is written |
  | `round` | 1 | one step; the digits are 0 to 15, and the result is a number of under 25 characters |
  | `first`, `last` | 0 | one step; a text's last character walks the text, a step per KiB |
  | `newline_to_br` | 0 | a step per line break; the escaped text's bytes, and seven bytes per break, before it is built |
  | `url_encode` | 0 | the bytes of the encoding, counted per character (three bytes per byte of one outside the unreserved set) before it is built |

  The counts are what keep the work bounded: an empty pattern in `replace` on a 60 KB text would build 60,000 pieces, so the occurrences are counted and charged first, and the work budget refuses them. `split` and `join` keep the array limit of D54. `strip_html` is LiquidJS's scanner, linear because a block opener with no closer is dropped from the search once. Replacement is literal, so there is no backtracking and no `$` pattern in the output. The exact-length `json` count is checked against `JSON.stringify` on lone surrogates, control characters and astral characters.
- **Not added.** `escape`: every HTML output is already escaped (`outputEscape`), so a second escape doubles it, and a plain-text template has nothing to escape for. Regular expressions. `sort`, `map`, `where`, `uniq` and `reverse`: each reads a list of records, and needs a reviewed cost bound for that first (`template_reference` names them as not supported). A filter in a `for` collection: LiquidJS reads only the value and drops the filter, so a save refuses the form, and a template that already saved keeps sending as before.
- **Save-time render.** Each part (body and subject, or a campaign layout with the preview's content) renders once through the same engine a send uses, against the preview's sample (`previewDummy`, `src/templates/sample.ts`). It refuses a failure caused by the template's own text, which the renderer lists explicitly: a filter with the wrong number of arguments, a date format with an unsupported directive (the message names it and the supported ones), a for tag with an unsupported argument, a property name not allowed, a loop property or collection not supported, nesting, and the render limits. It also refuses a literal failure, one whose expression reads no variable (`{{ "abc" | truncate: "x" }}`, `'2026-02-30' | date`, a literal `split` into more than 1,024 parts), and a `for` tag whose collection has a filter. Before the render, every part's whole text is read for literal filter arguments (`literalFaultOf`, `src/templates/renderer.ts`), in every branch the sample does not reach: a filter with too many arguments, a missing required argument (`slice`, `plus`, `minus`, `times`, `divided_by`), a literal number a filter cannot read (`truncate`, `truncatewords`, `slice`, `round` with digits outside 0 to 15, `plus`, `minus`, `times`, `divided_by`), a literal divisor of zero (it fails for every number; a nil input still renders empty), and a `date` format with an unsupported directive or over 256 bytes. The refusal names the filter and the argument, and reads `it fails to render: truncate: the first argument must be a whole number, got "x"`. Any other failure depends on a value, and is not refused: a non-integer filter argument from data, an array over 1,024 items, a URL that is not HTTP(S), a range whose bound comes from data, and a property key from data that is nil. The sample cannot tell a missing value from a present one, so refusing those would refuse templates that work for the messages that carry the value (`{{ data.delivery_date | date }}` with no `delivery_date` in the sample). Such a failure names its expression and the kind of value at send, in place of `invalid template`. An update is checked the same way, so a template that cannot render cannot be saved, even with only its name changed, until it is fixed. A busy renderer answers 429, as a preview does.
  - *Considered:* refusing every render failure (the false refusals above); a sample built per template from the names it reads (a second data model, and it still would not carry the values); a `sample` field on the save (a template API change, which D54 does not allow here).
  - *What the check does not judge:* a failure that depends on a value, in a branch the sample does not reach, saves and fails at send if its data is wrong. A value that reaches a filter through a loop or `assign` variable (`{% assign n = "x" %}{{ data.s | truncate: n }}`) is data to the check, so it saves and fails at send; so does a literal argument from data (`{{ data.s | truncate: data.n }}`). `06` states the general rule.
- **Converter.** The named references are the HTML spec's Greek letters, mathematical and arrow symbols, `&fnof;`, and the uppercase names `&AMP;`, `&LT;`, `&GT;`, `&QUOT;`, `&COPY;` and `&REG;` (`src/messaging/entities.ts`; each of the 124 code points checked against the WHATWG table). A name the spec does not list stays as written. An ordered list numbers its items from `start` (default 1) and continues after a nested list; a nested list is indented two spaces per level; bullets stay for unordered lists. A Markdown `1.` list renders as an `ol`, so the same rule covers it. *Considered:* the full 2,231-name table: data for names email does not use, and the brief's bar is the common names.
- **Decimal numbers and ids.** *Considered:* keeping a number's source text, or BigInt. Both change the data boundary of D55 for one class of values, and sending the ids as strings already works, so the limit is documented instead.
- **Cost:** a save renders its template once more, bounded by the same budgets. A send's render was measured before and after with the same script: no measurable change ([B135-render](../perf/B135-render.md)). The converter indents a list item by its list depth, capped at eight levels. When the indented text would be longer than the HTML, it is converted again without indentation, which is the text `main` writes, so the message keeps its text part (B135 review round 2). Each pass stops once its output is longer than the HTML, so time and memory stay linear in the input; the second pass costs about 1.7 times `main` at 1 MB ([measurements](../perf/B135-render.md)). A text longer than its HTML even without indentation goes without a text part, as before (D118). No schema, storage, send or API envelope change.
- **Evidence:** `test/templates/filters.test.ts` (each filter's output, nil and empty input, HTML escaping, and the emission and work budgets), `test/templates/reference.test.ts` (the Filters row equals the filters the renderer accepts, over LiquidJS's built-ins), `test/templates/object-loop.test.ts`, `test/templates/save-check.test.ts` (refusals with their causes, the value-failure boundary, updates, campaign layouts), `test/templates/date.test.ts` (`%%`, the existing directives, impossible dates, refused formats), `test/messaging/plain-lists.test.ts` (entities, ordered and nested lists, `start`, a literal `1.`), `test/tx/number-filter.test.ts` (a save refuses an argument to `number`, D102 unchanged otherwise), `test/templates/render.test.ts` and `compiled-fixture.ts` (`sort` is the refused name now that `replace` and `url_encode` are allowed), `test/templates/print-value.test.ts` (a list and an object print as text), `test/templates/code-points.test.ts` (`slice` and `truncate` by code point), `test/templates/strip-html.test.ts` (references decode after the tags go), `test/templates/bounded-filters.test.ts` (each new filter, its nil and literal cases, and the `url_encode` output limit), `test/templates/date.test.ts` (the directives, the empty results and the named refusal), `test/templates/save-check.test.ts` (the classification, the dynamic keys, the literal refusals and the value naming), `test/messaging/plain-nesting.test.ts` (the indent cap, the unindented fallback as `main` writes it, and the linear ratio).
- **Amended (B117e, lead, 2026-11-23):** four edges left after B135, each a save or send failure of the template's own text or its data. Unchanged: the filter set and every argument, the send status (a failing send is 400), the render budgets, the stored formats, and the API envelope. The save refusals add `it fails to render: …` reasons only.
  - *Loop objects.* A loop object an output prints, or a filter reads in its input or any argument, is refused at save, as `{{ forloop }}` already was: `{{ forloop | json }}`, `{{ 'x' | append: forloop }}`, `{% assign t = forloop | json %}`. So is a bare loop object that a `for` tag loops over or a range ends at (`{% for x in forloop %}`, `(1..forloop)`). A property the renderer does not expose is refused wherever it stands, in an output, a filter argument, a `for` tag's collection, `limit` or `offset`, or a bracket key: `forloop.parentloop`, `forloop["size"]`, `data.m[forloop.parentloop]` (the reason names the property and the offered ones). This holds outside a loop too, so `{{ forloop | default: 'x' }}` is refused at save, as 06 now states. A bare loop object in an `if`, an `assign`, a `limit`, an `offset` or a bracket key is not refused. The check reads the text the author wrote (`loopFaultOf`, `src/templates/renderer.ts`), so a branch the sample never reaches is refused too. *Considered:* refusing every `assign` of a loop object, which would break `{% assign f = forloop %}{{ f.index }}`, a template that works today; refusing only the forms the review named, which leaves `| append`, a `for` collection, a range and a bracket key open. *Not judged:* a loop object stored in a variable and printed later (`{% assign f = forloop %}{{ f }}`) saves and fails at send, when the branch is reached.
  - *The date guard.* 06's optional-date guard tests the formatted text, `{% assign d = data.delivery_date | date: "%d %B" %}{% if d != blank %}…`. The date filter renders empty for nil, empty, a non-date and an impossible date, so the guard holds for all of them. The raw guard `{% if data.delivery_date %}` stays documented as wrong for an optional date. *Considered:* keeping the raw guard and adding a note that `2026-02-30` still prints `Delivery `; a guard that holds is the better answer, and it costs one `assign`.
  - *Blank text in arithmetic.* In `plus`, `minus`, `times`, `divided_by` and `round`, a nil, empty or blank (spaces only) input renders empty, as B135 N6 set for `""`, and so does a nil, empty or blank argument from data (`plus: data.x`). Nil as an argument used to fail the send; it now renders empty, as nil does as an input. Both operands are read before either empty one returns, so a text that is not a number still fails whatever the other operand is. `round` follows the same rule for its digits (B117e): `round: data.x` with a blank or missing `x` renders empty, and `{{ x | round }}` with no argument still rounds to a whole number, because the template wrote no digits. Literal arguments are unchanged: a literal `""`, `" "` or `nil` is refused at save. *Considered:* failing blank text, which keeps the review's symptom (a message that cannot send for an optional field); a separate rule for nil arguments, which would leave `plus: data.x` failing for a missing field while `""` renders empty.
  - *Decimal division.* A decimal point in the dividend's text or in the divisor's makes the division a float: a divisor that is text with a decimal point (`divided_by: '4.0'`, text from data `"4.0"`, as the number literal `4.0` already did, B135), and a dividend that is text with one (`'10.0' | divided_by: 4` is 2.5, where it was 2; B117e review N4). The integer rule stays for every other pair: an integer divided by an integer rounds down, as Shopify's Liquid does. **This corrects D134's earlier wording**, which said the integer rule was LiquidJS's: LiquidJS 10.30 (`dist/liquid.node.js`, `divided_by`) divides every pair as a float, so it gives 2.5 for `10 | divided_by: 4` where this renderer gives 2. That difference is B135's integer rule, kept. *Considered:* a decimal type or BigInt for every operand (a change to the data boundary of D55 for one class of values, as D134 already decided); dividing every pair as a float, which drops Shopify's integer rule for every template. A JSON number with a decimal point is parsed to a JavaScript number, so `10.0` and `4.0` in data are `10` and `4` and divide as integers; 06 says to send them as text, and the limit is documented there rather than changed. A number literal dividend is its value too, so `10.0 | divided_by: 4` is 2.
  - *Evidence:* `test/templates/liquid-edges.test.ts` (the five loop reads of the round-1 review refused at save, the loop refusals, the stored loop and the outside-loop refusal, the date guard's values, blank text and a non-number, `round`'s digits, the decimal dividend and the quoted divisor), with `test/templates/save-check.test.ts` and `test/templates/bounded-filters.test.ts` unchanged and passing.

## D135. The send log names each message's subject, sequence and step, and operator views say what they count (B127, 2026-11-22)

- **Decision:** each send in `GET /api/sends`, `GET /api/sends/:id` and `GET /api/subscribers/:id/sends` carries these more fields: `subject` (the prepared message's subject, read from the journal's payload: null once the payload is dropped, thirty days after creation ([D70](#d70-send-history-is-kept-and-its-bodies-are-dropped-retention-shares-one-paced-loop)) or when the send is anonymized); `subject_missing`, which names why it is null: `skipped` (refused before any message was prepared), `wiped` (anonymized: the address is empty) or `retention`, and null when there is a subject; `template_name` (the send's template's name, null when it has none or the template was deleted, and kept after the payload is dropped); `sequence_id`, `sequence_name` and `step_position` (the sequence and the step's position, counted from zero as a timeline's `position` is; null for a transactional send and for a removed step). The admin send log (`/admin/sends`, `sends:get`) lists them with each send's status and skip reason, in one "Sequence / template" column (a sequence send's sequence and step, else the template's name), and a subscriber's sends tab lists the same. A row without a subject says why: "Not sent: <reason>", "Removed (data wiped)" or "Subject no longer kept (30-day retention)". Its filters are the API's own (status, source, kind, subscriber, list). The subscriber page counts campaign views and clicks as campaign figures and the journal's sends apart, and the dashboard's campaign count is labelled as one, with the sequence and tx count beside it for a viewer with `sends:get`. The subscriber list's search finds an exact external ID through the route the API already has (`GET /api/subscribers/external`), on the admin page only; that match is shown above the search's own rows and is not counted, so the pages are the search's. A campaign delivery skipped at its push names `membership_changed` when an audience membership was confirmed, deleted or otherwise changed, and `unsubscribed` only when one is unsubscribed (D131, [04](04-sending.md#campaigns)). An enrollment's timeline names a wait that the frequency cap holds, with the time it frees the step (`held`, `held_until`), and the subscriber's enrollment list carries `held_until` for the same wait. An enrollment's exit reads as its event and the time the event was recorded ("Event <name> at <time>"), or as the step condition with its text ("Step condition not met: <condition>", the stored condition JSON). The stored `exit_reason` is unchanged; a condition exit also keeps that condition's text in `exit_condition` (migration 0029, a snapshot taken at the exit, so a later edit of the step does not rewrite what ended the enrollment), and every other exit leaves it null. Each event on a subscriber's events list names the enrollments it started and exited, and each refused start names its sequence (`sequence_name`), read by ID so the names do not depend on a page of sequences. A sequence name links to its sequence only for a viewer with `sequences:get`; a viewer with `subscriber_events:get` (the events list) or `sends:get` (the send log) reads the names as plain text, since a sequence's name is an operator-visible label, as the send log already shows it. A send with no sent time shows a dash in its Sent column, never its creation time. The sidebar's send log has its own icon, and the subscriber's tabs wrap on a phone.
- **Unchanged:** the body, headers, attachments and the rest of the payload, which are still never projected; the global `sends:get` grant, which reads every send, with external recipients, regardless of list (D53); every status code, filter, page bound and permission; `GET /api/subscribers?search=`, whose matches are listmonk's (name and e-mail only); every other field of a send. Campaign mail is still not journaled, so the send log lists sequence and transactional sends only.
- **Considered:**
  - *A `subject` column, filled by a migration:* it keeps the subject past thirty days, which the payload does not, and it is the natural next step if the owner wants that. Not taken here: it writes four insert paths and the anonymizer, back-fills the whole table at upgrade, and needs a storage measurement. The subject is read with `json_extract` for the rows a page returns, and is shown while the payload is kept.
  - *The sequence names from the messaging module's own join:* the sends module would read the sequences tables, which the sequences module owns (AGENTS). The labels come from the sequences module through a lookup the wiring injects.
  - *Sequence names only for a viewer who may read sequences:* the journal is already the explicit global grant (D53), so a viewer of sends reads sequence names too. A sequence's steps, templates and conditions stay behind `sequences:get`.
  - *An `external_id` term in listmonk's `search`:* it would change the matches of every client's search. Refused: the admin page reads the exact route instead.
  - *Storing the cap hold on the enrollment:* a column and a write in every deferral, for a value the journal already settles. Refused: a timeline read of an active marketing wait asks the cap the same question the scheduler asks when it defers.
  - *`unsubscribed` for every change of membership:* it names a member who confirmed during an opt-in campaign as unsubscribed. Refused, hence `membership_changed`.
  - *Storing each event's started and exited enrollments with the event:* the events store keeps an outcome only for a keyed or refused event, so older events would show no links, and the events page would write for every triggering event. Refused: the enrollments already hold the trigger event and the exit (its reason and time), so the events read them, and the events of a database from before this release show their links too.
  - *Exit text and hold text as the enrollments' own labels:* a second copy of the enrollment wording on the subscriber tab would drift from the timeline. Refused: one helper names both.
- **Cost:** a send-log page reads the subject of each row it returns (`json_extract` on the stored payload, at most one page) and one step-label query for the page's steps. A timeline read of an active marketing wait adds one cap read, as the scheduler's defer does. The subscriber page makes one extra request for the send count. A send-log page reads each row's template name, one lookup per page. An events page reads, per page, one enrollment query on the subscriber's own index and one name lookup for its refused starts. An enrollment list reads each row's hold (the cap's latest-send read, per row with a deferred marketing wait). Migration 0028 replaces the status index (`idx_sends_outbox`, `(status, created_at)`, with `(status, id)`), so a status filter and the newest-first order share one index: the dashboard's newest sent row and the send log's `status=sent` page drop from about half a second to microseconds at a million sends, and the dashboard's count is 25 ms. Writes cost the same, one status index either way. Migration 0029 adds `enrollments.exit_condition`, a nullable column with no backfill (an `ALTER TABLE` that rewrites no row), and a condition exit writes its text once, in the same statement that ends the enrollment. The measurement is in [`docs/perf/B127-operator-views.md`](../perf/B127-operator-views.md).
- **Labels:** every new string is in `i18n/en.json` and, in English until a translator updates it, in each other pack ([i18n/README.md](../../i18n/README.md)): the send log's and the subscriber's words (`sends.subject*`, `sends.sequenceOrTemplate`, `subscribers.sequences`, `subscribers.started`, `subscribers.exited`, `subscribers.notStarted`). The `sequences.*` keys (the exit text and the cap hold) stay in `en.json` only, as before.
- **Evidence:** `test/scheduler/operator-views.test.ts` (the subject and the step on the send log and the subscriber's sends, the source filter, a held marketing wait and when it ends, a transactional wait that is not held), `test/campaigns/runner/runner.test.ts` (a membership removed during a batch is `membership_changed`, an unsubscribe `unsubscribed`), `test/retention/retention.test.ts` (the subject is null once retention drops the payload, the rest of the row unchanged), `test/admin/operator-views.test.ts` (the send log's page and permission, the subscriber's counts and tabs by permission, the events and timeline markup, the dashboard's sends count by permission), `test/e2e/specs/operator-views.e2e.js` (the send log and its filter, the counts and sends tab, the exact external ID, a refused start, and no sideways scroll at 390 px), and `test/e2e/browser-operator.ts` (one agent-browser pass over each changed screen at 1280 and 390 px). Round 1 adds: `test/retention/retention.test.ts` (the three reasons a subject is missing, with the injected clock), `test/tx/tx.test.ts` (the template name, and none once it is deleted), `test/scheduler/operator-views.test.ts` (an event naming 101 sequences it started and exited, and a cap hold in the subscriber's list), `test/scheduler/skip-reasons.test.ts` (a refusal names its sequence), `test/admin/operator-views.test.ts` (the sidebar icon, and the events links by permission), `test/e2e/specs/operator-views.e2e.js` (a refused and a wiped send, an exit named on the timeline and the tab, the phone's tabs), and the migration's upgrade tests in `test/bootstrap/`. The index and its timings are in [`docs/perf/B127-operator-views.md`](../perf/B127-operator-views.md).

## D128. Public pages offer only what a subscriber can act on, and all-lists unsubscribing is not a blocklist (lead, 2026-11-20)

Brief B130, from the first production adopter's report and the owner's direction of 2026-11-20: "fix as much as possible or reasonable in iglo.email, unless it is project-specific; good out of the box". Every item is generic, so each adopter would otherwise rebuild it.

- **Decision:**
  1. **The manage link and form need a public list.** The unsubscribe page shows "Manage preferences" only when the subscriber has a public list the manage view would name: a membership not unsubscribed, or unconfirmed for an opt-in link. With none, the manage view shows the privacy actions (export, and wipe where offered) and no preference form: no name field, no list checkboxes, no save. With no privacy action either, the view says there is nothing to manage (`public.nothingToManage`) and links back: never a page with only its header (B130 r1). `privacy.allow_preferences` stays the explicit switch, and off hides the link and the view as before. A transactional link keeps its account sentence and its privacy actions.
  2. **"Unsubscribe from all mailing lists" is not a blocklist.** The public option (still gated by `privacy.allow_blocklist`, the name unchanged) unsubscribes the subscriber from every list and writes the marketing suppression a self-wipe writes (B124, D125: keyed hashes of the address and external ID, the list IDs, the time; no address is stored). The status stays as it was, and the webhook is `subscriber.unsubscribed` with the list IDs and `"suppressed": true`, not `subscriber.blocklisted`. A single-list unsubscribe's `subscriber.unsubscribed` omits `suppressed` (B130 r1, adopter nit 4b). Enrollments: each running enrollment whose next step is marketing ends now as `exited`/`unsubscribed`, through a new effect `unsubscribedAll` and a statement that filters on the step the enrollment runs next (item 9's rule). An enrollment whose next step is transactional keeps running, even when its sequence is tied to a list. Marketing sends are refused by consent, a marketing start is `suppressed`, and a transactional send or step still delivers. The admin and API blocklist, the bounce blocklist and `PUT /api/subscribers/blocklist` are unchanged and still stop every mail.
  3. **The archive is embeddable.** `/archive` and `/archive/<slug>` send no `frame-ancestors`: D122 left the archive unchanged, and an archive shows no subscriber data. The subscriber pages keep their hardening headers.
  4. **The archive's feed link is text.** The index named `/public/static/rss.svg`, which was never shipped. The link reads "RSS" and keeps its class.
  5. **The sample transactional template takes the brand**, as the built-in layouts do: `brand_accent` on its button, `brand_on_accent` on the button's text, `brand_link` on its links, and `brand_font` on the body when a font is set. Migration 0030 rewrites the stored copy only while its body is byte-for-byte the B129 body; an edited or renamed copy keeps its body.
  6. **Transactional data carries the brand.** A transactional template's data (`POST /api/tx`, a sequence step and their previews) carries the eight brand values a campaign's data carries, at the top level beside `subscriber` and `data`. They are additive: no existing key moves. The sample needs them to read the brand on a real send.
  7. **An archived page whose template has no head** gets its Google font link right after the doctype, so the page is not parsed in quirks mode. A body with no doctype and no head starts with the link, as before.
  8. **Wording.** The option's label is "Unsubscribe from all mailing lists." under `public.unsubAllLists` in every locale. `public.unsubFull` ("Also block all future emails from this service.") is removed. The admin setting `privacy.allow_blocklist` keeps its key (listmonk's), and its label and help now describe the option (`settings.privacy.allowBlocklist` "Offer unsubscribe from all lists", `settings.privacy.allowBlocklistHelp`, every locale), not a blocklist (B130 r1, review adopter nit 4a).
  9. **A single-list unsubscribe ends only marketing enrollments (B130 r1, lead, 2026-11-22).** Leaving one list, by a mail's link, the page's form, the API or an admin action (and a bounce's `unsubscribe` action, which takes the same path, item 11), ends only the running enrollments of sequences tied to that list whose next step is marketing (`exit-by-lists` filters on the step the enrollment runs next, as `exit-marketing-by-subscriber` does for the all-lists option). An enrollment whose next step is transactional keeps running, even when its sequence is tied to the list. A mixed sequence delivers its transactional step; its marketing step then ends it when that step comes due, through the scheduler's consent check, with no marketing mail. The unsubscribe's status, webhook and list rows are as before.
  10. **The step an enrollment runs next (B130 r2, lead, 2026-11-22).** Both exits decide by that step, as the scheduler advances an enrollment: its current step while it is active; when the current step was deleted, the first active step at or after its position, the rule `advance` applies (`next-after-deleted`). So a waiting enrollment whose deleted step was marketing, and whose next active step is transactional, keeps running and delivers that step. The reverse, a deleted transactional step with a next active marketing step, ends now as `unsubscribed`, and no marketing mail goes out. A deleted current step with no active step after it does not end: the enrollment completes at its due time, as it would without the unsubscribe. Test: `test/scheduler/deleted-step-exit.test.ts`, through the mail link, the sink and `GET /api/enrollments`.
  11. **The bounce `unsubscribe` action (B130 r2, lead, 2026-11-22).** A bounce whose action is `unsubscribe` takes every list the subscriber is still subscribed to, keeping the status (`bounceUnsubscribe`, `membership-unsubscribe-all`, `src/subscribers/store.ts`). Its `changed` effect then reaches the same exit as item 9 for each list it changed. So a transactional-only sequence tied to any of those lists keeps sending to the address that bounced, while its marketing steps end. This follows from an unsubscribe not being a blocklist, and it is kept on purpose. The bounce's other actions are unchanged and still stop everything: `blocklist` blocklists the subscriber, and `delete` removes them, as before.

- **Unchanged:** every send, schedule and storage path, except migration 0030 (one row) and the additive brand keys of transactional data and the single-list exit of item 9; the single-list unsubscribe's status, webhook and list rows; every API status, message and `code`; the paired routes and their rules (D126); the blocklist's meaning on every API and admin path; the suppression's rules (a create, an import or a grant takes no list, a public signup joins double opt-in only, and the person's own confirmation or `DELETE /api/suppressions` clears it); the name `privacy.allow_blocklist`.
- **Considered:**
  - *Keep the public option a blocklist and say so in the label:* it cuts receipts and password resets off, which is the failure the adopter reported. Refused.
  - *End every enrollment of a sequence tied to one of the lists, as the single-list path does:* that also ends a transactional-only sequence tied to a list, which the option must not do. Refused for this path.
  - *Let a marketing enrollment end at its next marketing step through the scheduler's consent check, with no effect:* no mail goes out either way, but the enrollment stays `active` for a waiting day after the person left every list. Refused: the step the enrollment runs next decides (item 10), and the end is eager.
  - *A new setting or a choice on the page for the option:* the owner asked for none, and `privacy.allow_blocklist` already gates it.
  - *An RSS icon file:* a new static asset for one link. A text link is what the page needs.
  - *Keep the archive under the personal-page headers:* operators could not embed their own public feed or campaigns, and the archive shows no subscriber data.
- **Single-list exits (answers the open question of the first review, B130 r1, item 9):** a single-list unsubscribe used to end every enrollment of a sequence tied to that list, whatever its steps. Refused: keeping that rule, because it stops a transactional-only sequence (a payment-failed notice) when the person leaves a newsletter list. Item 9 is the change; the all-lists option keeps its own statement.
- **Deliberate differences from upstream:** the public option (not a blocklist, a suppression, an unsubscribe's webhook), the manage link and form that wait for a public list, the archive's text link and its embeddability, and the brand values of transactional data. They are in [07](07-api-and-ui.md) and `src/openapi/differences.ts` ("Public manage link and all-lists option (B130, D128)" and "Transactional template data carries the brand (B130, D128)"), and marked in `test/contract/public.ts` (`prefs-all-lists-d128` and `prefs-all-lists-page`, against the reference's `prefs-blocklist` and `prefs-blocklisted-page`).
- **Cost:** the option runs one statement in its transaction, filtered on `subscriber_id` and `+status = 'active'`, which makes SQLite use the `(subscriber_id, sequence_id)` index. Without the `+`, SQLite chose the status-only claim index and read every active enrollment in the install: 35–45 ms at 603k active enrollments, against 0.02–0.06 ms with it (B130 r2, blocker 1). The single-list and block-list exits take the same predicate. The option also runs at most one suppression read and insert. The page reads the memberships it already reads. A transactional send, step or preview builds the brand values once per request (a few string writes). Migration 0030 rewrites at most one row per install. Measured in [B130-public-defaults](../perf/B130-public-defaults.md).
- **Evidence:** `test/public/defaults.test.ts` (the manage link and form with and without a public list, the private-only case, `privacy.allow_preferences` off, the archive's text link, its embeddability, and the font link after the doctype), `test/public/brand.test.ts` (the three built-in templates in preview; a transactional send of the sample takes the accent, the link colour and the font), `test/unsubscribe/all-lists.test.ts` (the option through POST /api/tx and the sink: every list ends, the status stays enabled, a marketing send is skipped, a transactional send goes out, the admin blocklist still refuses, and the webhook is `subscriber.unsubscribed` only), `test/scheduler/all-lists.test.ts` (a waiting marketing step ends, a transactional step keeps running, a later marketing start is suppressed), `test/scheduler/single-list-exit.test.ts` (leaving one list: a waiting marketing step ends, a transactional-only sequence delivers, a mixed sequence delivers its transactional step and ends at its marketing step), `test/scheduler/deleted-step-exit.test.ts` (item 10: a deleted marketing step with a transactional next step keeps running; a deleted transactional step with a marketing next step ends now), `test/sequences/enrollments.test.ts` (the option's exits, the transactional sequence kept), `test/bootstrap/template-credit-upgrade.test.ts` (migrations 0027 to 0030 chained from schema 26, the migrated sample, and the brand on an archived page), the updated public, unsubscribe, campaign, paired-action and transactional-link tests, `test/e2e/specs/forms.e2e.js`, `consent.e2e.js` and `webhooks.e2e.js`, and the contract cases named above.

## D137. A transactional manage view never renames, is titled as the email settings page, and the placeholder's links end pending double opt-ins only (lead, 2026-11-23)

Brief B117b, from the B126 r3 backlog. It amends [D126](#d126-privacy-and-blocklist-need-a-paired-send-or-campaign-lead-2026-11-20) items (a) and (c) of its transactional and placeholder rules, in this record and not in D126's. All three items are decided and done in this change; none is left open.

- **Decision:**
  1. **No rename from a transactional manage view, with or without copies (item 1).** A transactional send can carry Cc and Bcc copies, and a copy holder reads the same manage view as the subscriber. The manage view of a transactional link has no name field, and a save that carries a `name` changes nothing (a save without one works the same). The save still changes lists: a confirmed list the save does not keep is ended, as upstream does, so unsubscribing from lists stays. A wipe is still refused on a transactional link, and export stays as D126 says (it mails the address on record). A marketing or campaign manage view keeps its name field and its rename.
  2. **Title (item 2).** With preferences on, a transactional link's manage view is titled and headed "Your email settings" (`public.emailSettings`, the key every bundled locale already carries), in its `<title>` and its H2. A marketing or campaign manage view keeps "Manage preferences" and the unsubscribe title, which D126 does not change.
  3. **Pending-only links (item 3).** A pending opt-in is an unconfirmed membership of a double opt-in list, as a transactional link already reads it. The placeholder campaign's link (the opt-in mail's, and a transactional mail sent before D126) ends pending opt-ins only on both of its POSTs: the one-click POST and the manage save. Its manage page lists pending opt-ins only. An unconfirmed membership of a single opt-in list is an active subscription (an API create without `preconfirm_subscriptions`, or an import), so these POSTs neither name it nor end it. The one-click POST calls `unsubscribeDoubleOptinPending`, the call a transactional link's form already makes.
- **Unchanged:** every status code and every page's other copy; the pairing rules of D126 (r1 and r2); the transactional page's pending form, which ends double opt-in pendings only (D123 item 8); the export on transactional links; the blocklist and wipe refusals; marketing at-most-once; every send path, schema and migration (none is added).
- **Considered:**
  - *Keep the rename when the send had no copies, and refuse it on a send with copies:* the rule would need a fact about each send. Whether a send had copies is recorded only in its journal payload (`sends.payload`, kept thirty days, D70), and a server's `email_headers` can add copies that the payload does not hold. A rule that depends on that fact fails silently when the fact is missing, and a copy holder would get the rename on exactly the sends the rule was written for. Not chosen.
  - *A copies flag on the send, written when it is queued (a migration, and a change in the journal's module):* it would make a copies check exact for new sends only. A send queued before the migration has no flag, so the rule would need a default for it, and the journal's owner would carry the field. Not chosen: the rule above reads no send fact at all.
  - *Hide every change on a transactional manage view (no name and no lists):* a forwarded unsubscribe link already gives a copy holder the same power over lists, and the subscriber can undo a list end by subscribing again. Hiding lists would remove the subscriber's own control over a receipt's list. Not chosen. The name is the identity change, and only the name is removed.
  - *Change the placeholder's POST here without its manage save:* the one-click POST and the manage save are the same link's two POSTs, so both change together.
- **Labels:** no new string. `public.emailSettings` is in every bundled locale already.
- **Evidence:** `test/public/transactional-links.test.ts` (the title and H2 on the link and with `?manage=true`, with no name field; a save with a name, with no name, with a Bcc copy and without one, changes no name and still ends an unchecked list), `test/public/paired-actions.test.ts` (a transactional manage view has no name field and a name it is sent changes nothing; the placeholder's manage page lists pending double opt-ins only and its save ends them, keeping an active single opt-in; its one-click POST does the same, and a single opt-in stays unconfirmed).

## D138. An event's profile write is not list-scoped, a held event queues, and every error code belongs to its status (lead, 2026-11-23)

Brief B117c, from the B131 review (items 1 and 4) and the B117 entry in [`status.md`](../status.md). Five items, each decided here.

- **Decision:**
  1. **An e-mail-only event's profile write stands.** An event that names no external ID claims nothing, so the claim rule of [D129](#d129-an-event-claims-an-address-its-holder-has-no-external-id-for-and-409-names-the-conflict-lead-2026-11-20) does not apply to it. Its update of the holder's `name` and `attribs` is the events role's profile write, as an event that names an external ID has always been on the holder it finds by that ID, and it is not gated by list scope. List scope governs the claim (D129) and the grants (D99), and nothing else in an event. A caller that manages none of the holder's lists still gets `200`, the holder's lists and history are unchanged, and the response names the holder's numeric `subscriber_id`, as an external-ID event does; a list-scoped read of that subscriber stays refused. The response also reveals more than the write. It names the holder's numeric `subscriber_id`, and when the event starts a sequence it tells the caller whether the subscriber is blocklisted or suppressed (a past self-wipe, [D125](#d125)) and whether a sequence's conditions matched (`enrollments.skipped` and `enrollments.started`). An external-ID event already answers the same, and an e-mail-only event answers the same way. That is an accepted part of the events permission. The answer adds no subscriber data beyond these facts and the grant results D99 already returns for the lists the caller may grant, and a list-scoped read stays refused.
  2. **A held install queues events.** During a restore hold ([D67](#d67-a-restored-database-holds-all-sending-until-an-operator-releases-it)) `POST /api/subscriber-events`, its batch and an `async` `POST /api/tx` record and answer `200`. Their sequence starts wait, since the scheduler claims nothing while held, and a list grant takes effect at once, so a marketing send to that list waits for the release, when its consent is checked again. The synchronous sends, resends, test sends and the other paths of the D67 table answer `503` `held`. This is by design: an app's facts about a person (an opt-in, an event, a profile) are recorded when they happen, and no mail leaves until the operator releases the hold. 07 and [operations](../operations.md#restore) say so.
  3. **Every HTML `429` and `503` sends `Retry-After`.** The archive's rate-limit answer sends `1`. The privacy export and `/admin/forgot` under a hold send `30`, as the API's holds do. Pinned by `test/backup/hold.test.ts` (the hold's API refusals and both pages) and `test/public/archive-retry.test.ts`.
  4. **Every error code belongs to its status, and the contract checks it.** A `not_found` on a `400` is listmonk's answer for a missing by-ID record (a subscriber, list, bounce, user, template, campaign or transactional reference). `test/contract/openapi.ts` probes every by-ID operation with one path parameter and no body with a well-formed absent ID, and a `PUT /api/subscribers/{id}` with a body. Each answer is checked against the operation's own response for its status, and a failure answer must carry `not_found`, except three refusals that name their own `invalid_fields` (a user delete checked against the last active Super Admin, a template delete refused as "non-existent or default", and a default set of a template that is not a campaign's). The probe found two answers the document did not list: `GET /api/users/{id}` and `GET /api/media/{id}` answer `404` (listmonk's `GetUser` answers 404, 6fcd95f), and now list it. The shared helpers (`test/contract/runner.ts`, `test/contract/envelope.ts`) take the response's status and accept only the codes of that status (`errorCodesFor`), not any known code. The router's plain-text `400 Bad Request` for an API path that does not decode, answered before routing, stays listmonk's bytes and has no `code`: it is the one error without one, and 07 names it.
  5. **A wrong password on the two-factor change is `403` `invalid_credentials`.** The status stays listmonk's `403`. A wrong password is a wrong credential, as a wrong API token is, so a client that branches on the code does not read it as a missing permission.
- **Unchanged:** every status and message (listmonk's text, which the contract suites check); the claim rule and its three 409s (D129); the hold's set of refusals (D67); the events' grants (D99) and their scope rule; each public page's body; the event path's write (no statement is added to it).
- **Considered:**
  - *Gate an e-mail-only update by manage scope (refuse it with 409 for a caller that manages none of the holder's lists):* every e-mail-only event for a subscriber on any list would answer 409 for the events-only role, which is the documented anonymous-then-identified flow. Refused.
  - *Gate only `name` and `attribs`, and answer 200 without them:* an event that succeeds with its profile fields silently dropped is worse than either a write or a refusal. Refused.
  - *Refuse events during a hold:* an app's event would be lost or retried for the length of a restore review, and the facts it carries (an opt-in, a profile) are the record the operator reconciles from. Refused; the sends stay stopped.
  - *Keep the wrong-password 403 as `permission_denied`:* a client would read a bad password as a missing permission. Refused.
  - *Drop the numeric `subscriber_id` from an e-mail-only event for a caller that manages none of the holder's lists:* changes the response shape of a documented event for a small gain, since the ID does not open the subscriber. Refused.
- **Cost:** no change to the event write path, to sending, scheduling or storage, so no measurement is required. The new code paths are error answers only.
- **Evidence:** `test/events/conflicts.test.ts` (an e-mail-only event by a caller that manages none of the holder's lists updates its name and attribs and keeps its lists, and the same for the external ID it then has), `test/backup/hold.test.ts` (each held refusal carries `code` `held` and `Retry-After: 30`; `/admin/forgot` and the export page under a hold do too; async `POST /api/tx` and events queue), `test/public/archive-retry.test.ts` (the archive answers 429 with `Retry-After: 1`), `test/users/` (a wrong password on the 2FA change answers 403 `invalid_credentials`), `test/contract/openapi.ts` (the by-ID 400 probes, and the per-status envelope check), `test/contract/runner.ts` and `test/contract/envelope.ts` (the code of the status, not any known code).

## D136. Settings and input validation: what is checked on save, and what matches listmonk (lead, 2026-11-23)

Brief B117a, from an adopter's checks and reviews of B126, B127, B131, B134f and B135. Eight inputs were accepted when they should not be, or refused with a message that did not say what to change. The stored formats, the authentication, the permissions and the atomicity of a save do not change.

- **Decision:**
  1. **Administrator addresses.** Every `app.notify_emails` address is checked on save with the rule `app.from_email` uses: a valid address of at most 2,000 characters. A blank entry is kept blank, as a send skips it. An entry already stored is not checked again (item 9). A send still skips a stored invalid address and logs it (B38).
  2. **Values the server reads.** Each is checked on save when its feature is on and its value changed (item 9, and the rule the bounce actions already use): `app.message_sliding_window_duration` longer than one second, which is the runner's own test for sliding (`src/campaigns/runner/runner.ts`); `maintenance.db.vacuum_cron_interval` a cron, while vacuum is on; `upload.s3.expiry` a positive duration; the SMTP `idle_timeout` from 1 s to 5 min, the sender's range (any other is replaced by its default at send time); the SMTP `msg_retry_delay` a duration (no sender reads it yet). An empty `upload.s3.expiry`, `idle_timeout` or `msg_retry_delay` is accepted: the server uses its default for an empty one. An empty vacuum cron is refused while vacuum is on, as a cron with no schedule is, and so is an empty or one-second sliding window duration, since the window does not run then.
  3. **From addresses.** `app.from_email` refuses an encoded word with no charset (`=?Q?x?=`, `=??Q?x?=`) or an encoding other than B or Q (`=?utf-8?X?x?=`), wherever the word stands. Only the settings check applies this rule; the campaign, transactional and send paths parse addresses as before. An unsupported charset keeps Go's rule, which the parser already had: refused as the first word, and kept as text after another word (the shared contract checks both). An entry already stored is not checked again (item 9).
  4. **Trusted URLs.** `security.trusted_urls` refuses a port outside 1 to 65535 (0 included) and a user name or password in the URL. An entry already stored is not checked again (item 9).
  5. **Campaign `send_at`.** Each field is checked against its own range before the instant is taken: the day against its month (leap years counted), the hour from 0 to 23 (so `T24:00` is refused), the minute and second from 0 to 59 (so a leap second is refused), and the offset's hour and minute. The `T` and `Z` are upper case only: a lower-case `t` or `z` is refused, as Go's RFC 3339 parse refuses it. `2030-02-29` and `T24:00` were stored as the next day and are now 400, as upstream's RFC 3339 parse refuses them. The instant is the one the text names, offset included.
  6. **Sequence step preview and test.** The caller's permissions are checked after the body is read and before it is parsed, so a refused caller gets 403 for any body. The test route checks `tx:send` there too, before it reads the addresses. A permitted caller's bad body is still 400.
  7. **Root and site URL messages (`src/config/bootstrap.ts`).** The root URL is set at start-up, not through the settings API, so its messages come from the start-up check. A DEL or C1 control character in the value is refused, and it is shown escaped, as JSON already escapes the C0 controls. `host:00080` names its leading zero and the number to write instead (no suggestion, as D107 gives none for it). `a.b:/c` names the scheme it reads as, `"a.b"`, which is not http or https. A value with credentials, a query or a fragment is shown as `(value not shown)`, and its message names nothing taken from it: neither its scheme nor its port number. The site URL (`app.site_url`) uses the same checks through the settings API, so its control-character refusal applies when it is saved and changed (item 9). The values that were accepted stay accepted.
  8. **CSV headers: kept, and the same as upstream.** The importer trims each header and then matches it exactly against `email`, `name` and `attributes`. Upstream's `mapCSVHeaders` (`internal/subimporter/importer.go` at 6fcd95f) does the same: it trims and does not fold case. So `" Email , NAME "` is refused with "'email' column not found", as upstream refuses it. No difference is recorded, and `test/contract/import.ts` checks the refusal in both modes.
  9. **Only what a save changes is checked (lead, B117a round 1).** A save validates what it changes. An entry already stored is not checked again: an address of `app.notify_emails` (compared trimmed on both sides), an address of `app.from_email`, and a `security.trusted_urls` entry; and `app.site_url` while its value is the stored one. So a value an older build or upstream stored cannot block an unrelated save, the admin's form included. A new or changed entry is checked as items 1 to 4 and 7 say, and a refused save writes nothing. A kept entry is stored as every save stores it, trimmed. Pinned by `test/admin/settings-input.test.ts`, through `PUT /api/settings/app.site_name` and the whole form, with each stored value an older build saved.
- **Unchanged:** every stored format (a list stays a list, an address is stored trimmed); the send-time skip of a stored bad notification address; the bounce scan interval and session duration checks; the other from-address rules; a save as one atomic write, so a refused value writes nothing; the root URL and site URL rules, except the messages and the C1 refusal; the trusted URL rules other than the two refusals.
- **Upstream:** items 1 to 4 are deliberate differences, with one row each in [07](07-api-and-ui.md) and in `src/openapi/differences.ts`. Item 5 matches listmonk. Items 6, 7 and 9 are native to iglo.email (a sequence route; the start-up messages and the site URL; a save rule). Item 8 matches listmonk.
- **Considered:**
  - *Check every stored value on every save:* a value an older build stored would then block saving any setting, the admin's form included. The changed-only rule keeps the form usable, and the bounce actions already work this way.
  - *Refuse an empty idle timeout or S3 expiry:* the sender and the S3 settings already use their defaults for an empty one, and full-object writes send an empty value for a field they do not show (the XML form, for one). Refusing it would refuse those writes.
  - *Fold the header case:* upstream does not, so a file that works here would fail in listmonk, and the operator's fix is a lowercase header. The error names the missing column.
  - *The strict encoded-word rule in every address parser:* the campaign and transactional senders keep the parser's rules. Only the settings check, which the operator types into, refuses the malformed forms. The later-word fix does reach every caller, because upstream refuses that charset too.
- **Cost:** no change to sending, scheduling or storage, so no perf record is needed. A save builds one set of the stored entries for each list it checks, and runs one more pass over the changed values; both cost nothing measurable beside the existing checks.
- **Numbering:** this record takes D136; the other B117 parts take D137 to D139 ([B117](../briefs/B117.md)). D136 is appended after D138 because D137 and D138 landed first.
- **Evidence:** `test/admin/settings-input.test.ts` (each rule's refusal and its accepted side; the four values an older build saved, a trusted URL, a from address, an untrimmed notification address and a site URL with a control character, each through the single key and the whole form, with a new invalid entry still refused); `test/contract/settings.ts` (the same refusals on the compiled build, outside the reference run, with the accepted side and a restore), `test/contract/settings-binding.ts` (valid administrator addresses), `test/contract/import.ts` (a mixed-case header refused in both modes); `test/campaigns/campaigns.test.ts` (`send_at` refusals, lower-case `t` and `z` included, and the offset); `test/sequences/step-preview.test.ts` (a revoked caller's invalid body is 403, and a test without `tx:send` is 403 for any body); `test/bootstrap/cli.test.ts` (the leading-zero port, the scheme, DEL and C1, and a withheld root URL that names nothing from it); `test/bootstrap/attention-stderr.test.ts` and `test/messaging/notify.test.ts` (a stored invalid address is still skipped at send time).
